# Flight model: the player aircraft step (0x26AF8, 0x2A2A2 and what they call)

Scope: everything that moves the player's aircraft in one simulation step: the key bytes it reads, the throttle and
fuel rules, the speed rules, the control surfaces and the attitude, the velocity and the sink term, the position, the ground,
deck and arrestor contact, the catapult, the crash conditions, the flags other code reads, and the HUD figures derived from
the state. Weapons, AI, damage effects and the mission flow are other notes; where the step hands over to them the hand-over
words are named. Addresses are flat-image addresses (base `0x10000`, `00-binary-layout.md`). `V:xxxxx` is an absolute address
in the variable hunks 71 to 73 (`0x4A6E8`-`0x54EBF`), `+0xNN` is an offset in the player record (`0x4B23C`, note 03) unless it says
otherwise. `W0` is the word at record `+0x00`, `W2` the word at `+0x02`, `B4` the byte at `+0x04`, `B20` the byte at `+0x20`,
`T` the signed byte at `+0x2B`, `S` the signed word at `+0x6C`, `W` the signed word at `+0x6E`.

Provenance and status. Every rule below was read from the corrected relocated image (Capstone listing) and then checked
against the original code itself in the Musashi rig (`tools/m68k-rig`): the routines were run on random states, and a
private re-implementation of the rules (not committed; scratch only) was compared with the rig bit for bit over thousands of
random states per routine and for the whole step (see "Verified by"). Rules marked "(read only)" were read from the code but
not exercised by a rig comparison; rules marked "(open)" are not understood. The worked examples at the end are rig output
of the real code.

## Conventions

- **Position**: three 32-bit longs at `+0x14` (x), `+0x18` (y, altitude), `+0x1C` (z); the step limits z to 0..`0x1FFFFFFF` and x to at most `0x1FFFFFFF`
  (x is **not** limited below: a negative x stays negative and resets z, see "Position" and 17.10; the cell word `+0x06` is `x >> 22`, which gives 0 to 127 for the permitted x and a negative word for a negative x; only the fine cell words wrap, at 2^22), and y is limited to the ceiling `V:4A7B2` (value `0x800000` in every capture).
  **Altitude unit**: 1 ft = 204.8 position units; the HUD and the external-view text show `5 * (y >> 10)` feet (y = 1800 shows 5 ft
  on the runway, y = 30472 shows 145 ft on the carrier deck; the capture `demo-deck.ppm` shows the altitude figure 145). This settles note 02
  open question 6 for the altitude; the horizontal unit is the same (a velocity is applied to x, y and z alike). The panel's altitude
  digits are a cache refreshed every 4 steps (they lag y by up to 5 steps, note 01b "altitude word"; the draw schedule is in note 06);
  the figure in the HUD itself is drawn every step from the current y.
- **Speed unit**: the speed words `+0x6C` and `+0x6E` count twelfths of a knot: the HUD figure is `|W| / 12` knots, **truncated** (integer
  division; note 06 ran the real HUD routine: 4231 reads 352 and 4271 reads 355; the 353 and 356 of note 01b were read from later speed
  words, not rounded values). One speed unit moves the aircraft by **4 position units per step** along its nose
  (displacement per step = `4 * W`, up to the smoothing described in "Motion").
- **Angles**: 28800 units per circle (note 03), values stored as words, pitch `+0x66`, heading `+0x68`, roll `+0x6A`.
  Signs (measured, 01b, and checked in the rig): positive pitch is nose down (nose up is below the circle: 26360 is 30.5 degrees
  up), positive heading is clockwise (a right turn raises it), a positive roll is a left bank (right wing up); the key RIGHT
  gives a negative roll. World axes: +z is north, -x is east, +y up.
- **Time**: one step per displayed frame, not scaled by elapsed time (note 04). The reference machine ran 7.97 steps per second on average
  while flying (01b: four flight scenes, 230 samples); section 14 gives the rule and the port's constant.
- **Arithmetic**: all state words are signed 16-bit and wrap; right shifts of negative numbers are arithmetic (they round
  toward minus infinity); products of two words are 32-bit; "a - (a >> k)" style decays therefore behave asymmetrically for negative
  values, and the rules below keep that. "Slew(v, th, k)" means: if |v| is greater than th then v becomes v - (v >> k), else v
  becomes 0. "Relax(x, r)" for the ground term `+0x26` means x becomes x - ((x - r) >> 3) (word arithmetic).

## Location

| what | where |
| --- | --- |
| frame hook: world update and the player | stage `0x1F54E` (called from the frame routine at `0x11166`) calls `0x26AF8` (entity scan) at `0x1F5C6`; also `0x12CF6` |
| world update, all 15 entity slots in order | `0x26AF8`; slot 0 (the player) is processed at `0x26BCA`-`0x26C00` |
| per-entity update (the player step) | `0x2A2A2` (entry with A1 = `0x4B23C`, `V:4AA6C` = `V:4AA6E` = 0); its tail `0x2A9BA` |
| live input conversion | `0x1DFAA` (key byte `+0x65` and preset `V:4A928` to the command bytes `+0x28`, `+0x29`, `+0x2A`); recorder `0x2A1A6` |
| key bits | handlers `0x1E228`-`0x1E340` (cursor keys, rudder keys, throttle keys), `0x1E93E` (gear), `0x1E342` (hook), `0x1E308` (brake), `0x1EA26` (F1-F10 presets), `0x1DE52` (eject) |
| throttle, fuel and speed | `0x15ED4`; helpers `0x169C6` (relax), `0x169F2` (slew), `0x16A32` (climb and altitude term G), `0x2AB64` (gust and sink) |
| control surfaces (stick to angular rates) | `0x1557C`; helpers `0x15B7A` (decay), `0x15BDE` (nose settle), `0x15CAA` (ground roll reset), `0x15CF0` (pitch rate), `0x15D5A` (yaw rate), `0x15DB4` (roll rate), `0x15E2E` (rate boost) |
| attitude integration | `0x31AE0`; turn coupling `0x32426`; matrix composition and Euler extraction `0x325B8`; matrix builders `0x32B52`, `0x32BEC` (note 03); store and matrices `0x3202C`; sine and cosine of an angle in tenths of a degree (quadrant folding of the table at `0x436E8`) `0x32DB2` |
| velocity, floor, touchdown, take-off | `0x16B0E`; helpers `0x29C18` (direction times speed), `0x20884` (vector length), `0x17288` (velocity smoothing) |
| collision and contact | `0x2B5E2` (carrier deck and hull `0x2B7D2`-`0x2BB70`, planes `0x2BB7A`, landmarks `0x2BC28`-`0x2C08C`, ground vertices `0x2C08C`); handling of its result in `0x2A758`-`0x2A9C0` |
| terrain class (sea, pad) | `0x2F75A`; data: grid `0x47F40`, pads `0x47B72`, offsets `0x206F2` |
| landing complete, re-arm | `0x1F11E` (called from `0x170A6`) |
| start of a flight | `0x1D4E2` (state), `0x1D8B8` (record), `0x1D38A` (switch defaults), `0x1D858` (clears) |
| mission state machine (crash hand-over) | `0x12EFE`, `0x138D8`, `0x13980` |
| sound cues from the step | `0x270A0` (high turn rate), `0x2835A` (gear down above `0x1200`), `0x37C32` |
| warning message chooser | `0x13D4C` (flag words to message ids); message table `0x41FF0` (28 bytes per entry); the post routine `0x29BC8` (17.13) |
| ejection seat | `0x26F60` (world update, records 1 to 3), `0x27522` (seat), `0x276D2` (spawner tail), `0x1EC14` (view to the seat), `0x1EFF4` (request table fill); latch `V:4AA7A` (17.10) |
| mouse throttle | handler `0x192DE` (installed by `0x195A6`), limits `0x19254` (called at `0x1167E`), follow and fire rules inside `0x15ED4` (17.4) |
| HUD figures | speed `0x36A10` and `0x36FB2`, altitude `0x36ADE` and `0x36F40`, fuel `0x36BEE`, thrust `0x36C3C`, heading `0x36F74`, G meter `0x36880` |
| camera trail of the followed entity | `0x2AC5A` (`V:54E70`..), used by the crash and ejection cameras |

### State block: the player record (`0x4B23C`, 0x200 bytes)

Only fields the step reads or writes are listed. "R" = read, "W" = written by the step, "I" = set at start (`0x1D4E2`).

| offset | size | name | meaning, unit | use |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| +0x00 | word | W0 | flags: `0x8000` hit this step (set by contact, cleared by the tail of the step), `0x4000` radar and lock bookkeeping (set and cleared by the radar code `0x36082`/`0x35F5E`, cleared by the dying path; no rule of the player step reads it; the carrier record starts with it set; read only), `0x1000` active (simulated; the step does nothing to position without it, external view only), `0x0800` brake (wheel brake on the ground, air brake in flight; toggled by the brake key), `0x0400` dying countdown running, `0x0200` crashed, `0x0100` keeps the entity moving in external view when `0x1000` is clear, `0x0080`, `0x0040` (entity valid), `0x0008` (collidable), `0x0002` cleared **for the player only** by the world update before its step (`0x26BEE`; other records keep it, note 07 1.4), set by the entity spawner (`0x2781A`) and read by a weapon or AI routine (`0x27E54`); the player step does not read it (read only); start value `0x11C8` | R W I |
| +0x02 | word | W2 | `0x8000` hook down, `0x4000` hook engaged (arrestor corridor), `0x2000` military-power detent, `0x1000` skid or crash trail, `0x0400` idle detent, `0x0100` (external AI flag, 0 for the player), `0x0080` on the ground (wheels down on a surface), `0x0040` nose settled (set by the ground pitch rule), `0x0020` eject animation release, `0x0010` recentre the roll once, `0x0008` afterburner, `0x0004` gimbal-skip flag, `0x0002` contact flag set by the collision scan, `0x0001` proximity flag set by another entity's scan (cleared at the start of every world update) | R W |
| +0x04 | byte | B4 | `0x80` tail point over the deck, `0x40` nose point over the deck (both recomputed every step), `0x10` controls locked while `+0x4C` is non-negative, `0x08` catapult armed (carrier start; cleared at lift-off), `0x04` over an airfield pad, `0x02` over water | R W |
| +0x05 | byte | state | 0 for the player in flight; `0x0A` makes the pitch drift to level at `0x50` per step (wreck), 1 after an entity collision | R W |
| +0x06, +0x08 | word | cell x, z | `(x >> 16) >> 6` (signed word shift) | W |
| +0x0A, +0x0B | byte | cell bytes | `+0x0A`: sub-cell index (4 by 4) of the position, derived; `+0x0B`: the neighbour index, **set to `0x0F`** when `+0x0A` changes and the cell is within 2 of the viewed cell (corrected, 17.10; the start routine writes the direction index n, which stays until the first rewrite) | W |
| +0x0C, +0x0E | word | fine x, z | `(x & 0x3FFFFF) >> 8`, `(z & 0x3FFFFF) >> 8` (256-unit steps, wraps at 16384) | W |
| +0x10 | long | alt >> 8 | `y >> 8` | W |
| +0x14, +0x18, +0x1C | long | position | x, y, z, see Conventions | R W |
| +0x20 | byte | B20 | `0x80`, `0x20`, `0x04` stall (indicated speed at or below the threshold), `0x02` out of control (wrecked: speed rules skipped, nose drops), `0x01` skid (belly or ditching: speed decays fast) | R W |
| +0x21 | byte | | bit 0 set at the first lift-off | W |
| +0x22, +0x24 | word | roll sine, cosine | Q14 of the roll angle folded into one quadrant, from the turn coupling | W |
| +0x26 | word | ground term | first-order lag toward the ground acceleration, or the stall nose-drop ramp (up to `0x50`) | R W |
| +0x28, +0x29, +0x2A | signed byte | commands | pitch command (`-20..20`, positive = stick forward = nose down), rudder command (`-20..20`), roll command (`-60..60`, negative = left) | R W |
| +0x2B | signed byte | T | throttle, `-32..120`; 120 = military power, negative = reverse thrust on the ground; initial 9 | R W I |
| +0x39 | byte | ramp | low nibble counts the frames a throttle key has been held (1 when none) | R W |
| +0x3D | byte | trail | camera trail length | W |
| +0x3E, +0x42, +0x46 | long | velocity | the displacement added to x, y, z per step, stored with the sign that is **added** to the position (y is added to altitude) | R W |
| +0x4C | word | counter | decreased by 1 every step by the world update (not in a paused pass, 17.2); crash countdown (set to 15); on the ground the terrain query runs when its low three bits are 0 | R W |
| +0x4E | word | deck height | height of the deck polygon under the aircraft in 256-unit steps (carrier deck 112); raises the floor | R W |
| +0x50, +0x52, +0x54 | word | roll quadrant state, roll rate state | turn coupling state | R W |
| +0x56, +0x58, +0x5A | word | angular rates | pitch, heading and roll increments applied to the attitude each step (angle units) | R W |
| +0x5E | byte | group | entities with equal group do not collide | R |
| +0x62, +0x63 | byte | class, id | class 0x11 F/A-18, 0x10 F-16 (written from `V:4A901`), id 0x0D (read by no rule of the step). **Every class test of the step compares the high nibble** (`class & 0xF0`; 1 = flying aircraft, 0x20 ships, 0x30 and 0 other): the speed-step gate, the stall rule, the landmark phase and the three-vertex ground test apply to nibble 1, that is 0x10 to 0x1F; exact values matter only for the floor (0x14, 0x15) and the roll lag (0x14). Rig: the same state with class 0x10, 0x11, 0x14 and 0x1F gives identical traces (speed rules and stall both active); with class 0x00, 0x20 and 0x30 the speed rules and the stall do not run | R I |
| +0x65 | byte | key byte | the step's input, see "Inputs" | R W |
| +0x66, +0x68, +0x6A | word | pitch, heading, roll | attitude | R W |
| +0x6C | word | S | target (undisturbed) speed | R W |
| +0x6E | word | W | indicated speed, `S + g`; the figure shown and the one used for movement | R W |
| +0x71 | byte | | `0xFF` from the start routine; read only by a radar or target routine (`0x300D8`, a negative value skips it) outside the step; no rule of the step depends on it (read only) | I |
| +0x72 | long | fuel | initial and refuelled value `0x61A800` (6,400,000); the panel shows `fuel >> 8` (25000 when full) | R W |
| +0x76 | word | sink | vertical sink term from the speed table (see "Gust and sink") | W |
| +0x78 | word | g | speed gust term added to S | R W |
| +0x7B | byte | part id | the renderer's part id: low byte of the first word of the model part drawn last for this entity (single writer `0x21F10`; the aircraft body is part 0, the cockpit block `0xFF`). The three-vertex ground test of the collision routine is live only when it is non-negative with a zero low nibble (17.12). In the cockpit it is 0 (body) only with pitch band `V:4A923` = 3, or with band 1 or 2 and the eye measure (`V:4AB30 >> LOD`) at or above -32, in the head views 3 to 9 and for one pass after leaving an external view (`V:4A8ED`); level flight (band 0), flares and the roll-out draw the cockpit block (`0xFF`) at any height; in the external views the ring cameras give 0 and the approach cameras (note 07 6.1) 0, 1, 2 or `0xFF` (note 04a section 8a; read by the collision step one pass late, 17.12) [read; emu]. Inert in the examples because no model vertex is below y = 0 there | R |
| +0x7C | byte | gear, eject | bit 7 gear retract command; bits 4-6 retract progress 0..6 (6 = up, 0 = down and locked); low nibble non-zero = ejection animation, controls ignored | R W |
| +0x7D | byte | | low nibble L = 3: model unit shift (one model unit = 2^(8-L) = 32 position units); the high nibble (5 in the captured state) is read by nothing in the step | R |
| +0x7E | word | turn limit | `0x1400`, ceiling of the bank-turn coupling | R I |
| +0x80..+0x91 | 9 words | matrix A | attitude matrix Q14 (`fx_matrix_a` of the three angles), note 03 | R W |
| +0x92..+0xA3 | 9 words | matrix B | Q14 matrix of the negated angles; its third column (`+0x96`, `+0x9C`, `+0xA2`) is the nose direction in world axes | R W |
| +0xA4... | 6 bytes per slot | model vertices | rotated model vertices written by the draw pass (not the step): slot k = `(M[row] . v_k) >> 8` with `M = matrix B >> 6`; the deck test reads slot 0 (`+0xA4`) and slot 3 (`+0xB6`) of the player | R |

### Variables outside the record

| address | size | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `V:4AA6C`, `V:4AA6E` | word | current entity index and its byte offset (`index << 9`); 0 for the player |
| `V:4A994`, `V:4A996` | word | viewed entity index and offset (0 for the player) |
| `V:4A984` | word | bit 6 (0x40) flight simulation running (the speed, motion and the surfaces rules need it), bit 0 message posted, bit 8 damage; bit `0x0800` is set in the captured game states and read by no rule of the step (rig traces identical without it) |
| `V:4A95E` | byte | display mode: 1 cockpit, 2 the external views; in the step only the pointer-throttle rule reads it (the value 2 turns that rule off); note 06 uses it for the HUD schedule |
| `V:4A986` | word | bit 14 (0x4000) set when fuel is exhausted on the ground, bit 13 message colour class |
| `V:4A98A` | word | bit 14 overspeed warning, bit 1 landed and stopped |
| `V:4A982` | word | bit 8 set at lift-off (armed for the landing-complete event), bit 1 the sign of the G readout |
| `V:4A866` | byte | pause (all of the step is skipped), `V:4A867` freeze the surfaces (rates forced to 0) |
| `V:4A83C`, `V:4A847` | byte | feature switches, 1 in free flight (set to 1 by `0x1D38A` together with the bytes up to `V:4A847`): `V:4A83C` enables the gust and sink term and the stall rule, `V:4A847` the bank-turn coupling |
| `V:4A83D` | byte | view selector, 0 cockpit, non-zero external (corrected, 17.2: the cockpit always runs the whole step; in the external view a record with neither `W0` bit `0x1000` nor `0x0100` is not stepped at all, and one with only `0x0100` runs the attitude and motion steps but its position is not advanced) |
| `V:4A844` | byte | collisions enabled (the game's value is 1, set by `0x1D38A`; no store in the image writes 0 to it, training included: note 08 section 8.10; what it gates: 17.12) |
| `V:4A857`, `V:4A858` | byte | scaled-motion switches, 0 in play (never set by any code found) |
| `V:4A890` | byte | mouse throttle, set to 1 at every start: the pointer height `V:4A830` (-960..960, corrected 17.4) is the throttle times 8 |
| `V:4A903` | byte | input source: 0 live keys, 1 recording, 3 playback of a recorded byte stream, 4 stream full |
| `V:4A928` | byte | throttle preset requested by F1-F10 (12 x n; 121 = afterburner) or -1 for idle |
| `V:4A7B2` | long | altitude ceiling, `0x800000` |
| `V:4A9FE` | word | the pitch contribution of the bank-turn coupling this step (G meter); 0 when the coupling is cut off (pitch beyond 3600, `W0` `0x8000`), unchanged when the routine is not called (17.8) |
| `V:4AC08` (long) | | warning flags: low byte `0x01` low fuel, `0x02` fuel critical, `0x40` fuel exhausted, `0x80` stall (exclusive fuel flags, 17.5) |
| `V:4AC0C` (long) | | one-shot cue bits taken one per pass by the cue player `0x14B5A`: `0x80` crash-imminent and proximity, `0x40` eject, `0x20` stall lamp, `0x200` roll-out (no branch tests it); the full list is 17.13 |
| `V:4AB98` | word | current message id (low byte indexes the table `0x41FF0`, high byte colour and priority) |
| `V:4A878`, `V:4A8F6`, `V:4A8FD` | byte | sound requests (note 08): `V:4A878` 1 touchdown, 3 skid, 4 stopped on the ground, 2 and 3 overspeed |
| `V:4A968`, `V:4A84F` | byte | centre-screen notice selectors for the HUD: `0xFE` throttle moved from zero or landing, `0xFD` afterburner on, `0xFC` afterburner off, `0xFA` overspeed |
| `V:4A87D`, `V:4A850` | byte | crash hand-over: 1 and 4 |
| `V:4ABAA` | long | seconds sample of the system clock (read by lift-off and touchdown statistics) |

### Tables in the image (extractor outputs: the port loads these, it does not approximate them)

Each table is characterised by address, size, shape and a few samples; the port loads the contents from the image with the extractor. Sizes in words (2 bytes) unless stated.

| address | size | shape and role | samples |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `0x425D8` | 62 words used (indices 0..61); the table `0x42654` starts right after them | stick-to-rate response of the **player**: index = command value (0..20 for pitch and rudder, 0..60 for roll), result = commanded angular rate (angle units per step before the lag); monotonically rising | index 1: 27, 10: 225, 20: 468, 60: 1611 |
| `0x42654` | 64 words | the same for other (AI) entities, larger gain | index 1: 117, 10: 360, 60: 1611 |
| `0x2AB18` | 31 words | gust target by speed: index = `min(abs(S) >> 7, 30)`; monotonically falling | 384, 144, 6 at indices 0, 8, 30 |
| `0x2AAD8` | 31 words | sink term by speed (see "Gust and sink"): index = `min(abs(W) >> 7, 30)` | 512, 144, 1 at indices 0, 10, 30 |
| `0x3259A` | 15 words | pitch scale of the bank-turn coupling, index = `abs(pitch) >> 8` (0..14): 256 at index 0, then 240 at index 1 and 20 less for each further index down to 20 at index 12, 0 at indices 13 and 14 | 256, 240, 20, 0 at indices 0, 1, 12, 13 |
| `0x436E8` | 901 words | quarter-wave sine table, Q14, index = angle in tenths of a degree 0..900; indices below 0 read the 256 words before it (`0x434E8`, which is the third inverse-sine table below), see the gust rule | index 0: 0, index 900: 16384 |
| `0x42E92`, `0x43098`, `0x434E8` | 259, 552, 256 words (contiguous, ending where the sine table starts) | inverse sine tables (note 02) used by the Euler extraction, one per range of the sine magnitude (below 0.883, up to 0.995, beyond) | |
| `0x208E8` | 257 words | `sqrt(1 + (i/256)^2) * 16384`, the vector length helper | 16384, 18318 (i = 128), 23170 (i = 256) |
| `0x47F40` | 32 x 32 words (2048 bytes) | terrain grid (row = (z >> 24) & 0xFF, column = (x >> 24) & 0xFF as an unsigned byte, so a negative x reads cells 128 to 255; corrected, 17.10 [rig]): offset of a cell record from the table start; in this image every cell has one (103 distinct records) | offsets 2048 to 5746 |
| cell records (hunk 68, `0x48740`-`0x495CB`) | 3724 bytes | header (two words), then polygons: count word, then (x, z) word pairs in 1/4096 of a cell; terminator `0xFFFF`; a record whose first word is negative means "no water" (38 of the 103 records are of this kind, each used by 1 to 21 cells) | |
| `0x47B72` | 4 entries of 16 bytes plus a terminating entry (80 bytes) | airfield pads: word 0 offset of the item descriptor from `0x2600C` (the four offsets are disk data and are not listed: `tables/flight_pads.bin`, the first word of each of the four 16-byte entries; counted from `0x2600C` as note 04a does they are descriptor numbers, and the object id of note 02 is the descriptor index plus 16), word 1 cell x (an entry whose cell x is -1 ends the table, another negative one skips the entry), word 2 cell z, word 3 direction index (disk data, not listed: the file `tables/flight_pads.bin`, 256 bytes, FNV-1a-32 `e89381f3`, identifies the four), words 4 and 5 x and z offsets, words 6 and 7 unused (0) | cell x, z of the first entry: 16, 16 |
| `0x206F2` | 16 pairs of words (64 bytes) | per direction index n the (x, z) offset pair `4096 * (3 - n mod 4, 3 - n div 4)`, used in units of 1024 position units by the pad query (also the phase offsets of note 04a) | n = 0: (12288, 12288); n = 6: (4096, 8192) |
| pad quad lists (long at `0x2600C + word0 + 0x10`) | 4 lists: `0x3BF34` (4 quads), `0x3CE2C` (1), `0x3CE96` (3), `0x3C918` (2); 16 bytes per quad plus a word `0xFFFF` | each quad is four (x, z) word pairs in units of 256 position units, relative to the pad reference point R (section 10), in order around the quadrilateral | the quad words are disk data and are not listed (`tables/flight_pads.bin`; FNV-1a-32 `8d45f03f` of the 16 bytes of the first quad of the first list, little-endian words, x then z of each corner) |
| `0x5283C`, `0x52868` | 11 longs, 11 x 256 bytes | landmark collision slots written by the display-list builder at build time (section 11b); not a table of the image | |
| `0x3E0F8` (carrier, first list) | 124 bytes: 7 groups of 5, 4, 4, 4, 4, 3, 3 pointers (longs, flat image addresses), each group followed by a word `0xFFFF`, the list by a second `0xFFFF` | first pointer of group 0 = the deck polygon structure S0 (`0x3E78A`); the other pointers name 12-byte plane records (word 0 `0xC00C`, words 1 to 3 the three vertex byte offsets, two more words not read by the tests) | `0x3E78A`, `0x3E854`, `0x3E860` |
| `0x3E174` (carrier, second list) | only the first 44 bytes are read: 2 groups of 5 pointers, each followed by `0xFFFF` | volumes tested after the arrestor corridor (section 9) | `0x3E74E`, `0x3E75A`, `0x3E6AE` |
| `0x3EDCC`, `0x3EDEC` (other ship) | 32 bytes (one group of 7 pointers, then `0xFFFF 0xFFFF`) and the first 44 bytes (2 groups of 5) | the same two lists for the second ship type | `0x3F32E`, `0x3F2F2` |
| structures named by those lists | carrier `0x3E6AE`-`0x3E95A` (684 bytes), other ship `0x3F2B6`-`0x3F420` (362 bytes) | deck polygon lists (vertex byte offsets, bit 15 on the last) and 12-byte plane records; all vertex byte offsets are relative to record `+0xA4` (slot k = 6k) | |
| `0x41FF0` | 28-byte entries | message table, byte 0 attribute, bytes 1-26 text; the port needs only the ids of the messages named in this note (the cockpit message texts are in note 06; every other text screen is in note 08) | |

The folding rule of `0x32DB2` (sine and cosine from the quarter table, angle t in tenths of a degree, 0 <= t < 3600; T[i] is the word at `0x436E8 + 2i`):
t < 900: sin = T[t], cos = T[900 - t]; 900 <= t < 1800: sin = T[1800 - t], cos = -T[t - 900]; 1800 <= t < 2700: sin = -T[t - 1800], cos = -T[2700 - t];
t >= 2700: sin = -T[3600 - t], cos = T[t - 2700]. There is no range guard: a t outside 0..3599 reads outside the table. (Verified: used by the rig-checked bank-turn coupling and matrix builders.)

### Extractor needs

Every table the port loads from the image, in one list (all other numbers in this note are constants of the rules). "Model data" comes from the model decoder of note 02, not from raw addresses.

| item | address | size | shape |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| player stick response | `0x425D8` | 62 words | rate by command index |
| AI stick response | `0x42654` | 64 words | the same, larger gain |
| gust target | `0x2AB18` | 31 words | by speed index |
| sink term | `0x2AAD8` | 31 words | by speed index |
| bank-turn pitch scale | `0x3259A` | 15 words | by pitch index |
| sine, quarter wave | `0x436E8` | 901 words | Q14; the words before and after it that the over-reads touch are not a table of their own: they lie inside junk span 2 below (17.9) |
| inverse sine, three parts | `0x42E92`, `0x43098`, `0x434E8` | 259, 552, 256 words | angle in tenths of a degree by index (inside junk span 2) |
| length helper | `0x208E8` | 257 words | Q14 |
| junk span 1 (words before the bank-turn pitch scale) | `0x3249A..0x325B8` | 286 bytes | raw image bytes, read as big-endian words at negative indices of the scale table `0x3259A` (index -1 is the word at `0x32598`); it is code and data of the bank-turn routine, with three relocated address longs (`0x32540..0x32543`, `0x32560..0x32563`, `0x32568..0x3256B`, rule in 17.9); purpose: the exact answer of the coupling scale index for a negative pitch word [rig, read] |
| junk span 2 (over-reads of the sine, gust and composition lookups) | `0x3AE92..0x49A12` | 60,288 bytes | raw image bytes with the 337 relocated address longs set to their reference-machine values (rule in 17.9, target hunk of the relocation entry); it contains the three inverse-sine tables and the sine table in full and everything around them; purpose: the exact answer of the two matrix builders (reads from `0x4130A` to `0x461CF`), of the gust index 901 to 4996 and of the composition's heading and roll lookups (`0x3AE92` to `0x4AE91`: below the table for a quotient of 16384 or more in magnitude, 17.18 a; the reads above `0x49A12` are answered 0) for angle words outside 0..28799 [rig] |
| terrain grid | `0x47F40` | 32 x 32 words | offsets of cell records |
| terrain cell records | `0x48740`-`0x495CB` | 3724 bytes | water polygons, hunk 68 |
| airfield pads | `0x47B72` | 4 x 16 bytes + terminator | see table above |
| direction offsets | `0x206F2` | 16 word pairs | see table above |
| pad quad lists | `0x3BF34`, `0x3CE2C`, `0x3CE96`, `0x3C918` | 66, 18, 50, 34 bytes | quads of four word pairs, `0xFFFF` end |
| carrier lists | `0x3E0F8` (124 bytes), `0x3E174` (44 bytes read) and the structures `0x3E6AE`-`0x3E95A` | 684 bytes of structures | pointer groups, deck polygons, plane records |
| other-ship lists | `0x3EDCC` (32 bytes), `0x3EDEC` (44 bytes read), structures `0x3F2B6`-`0x3F420` | 362 bytes of structures | the same |
| carrier model | model 8, level 0 | 58 vertices | the deck and hull tests index them by the byte offsets above; the port rotates them each step |
| aircraft models | models 4 (F/A-18) and 3 (F-16), level 0 | vertices 0 to 3 | rotated vertices 0, 1, 2 (landmark test) and 0, 3 (deck test) |
| message table | `0x41FF0` | ids only | low byte of the id indexes it; the ids used here are in the Constants table |

## Behaviour

### 1. One step: order of work

Per frame the frame routine first runs the current mission state routine (called at `0x11956`, 17.1) and then calls `0x26AF8` (17.1: the state routine runs **before** the entity update in every pass). `0x26AF8` does, in this order (while `V:4A866` is non-zero the effect timers, the damage cool-downs, the `+0x4C` decrement, `0x27B1E`, the player step and its camera trail are skipped;
the clearing of `W2` bit `0x0001`, the player's `W0` bit `0x0002`, the cues `0x270A0` and `0x2835A`, the AI tick of item 2 and `0x26F28` still run: corrected, 17.2):

1. the 16 effect timers at `V:4D240` count down (not in a paused pass: they do not count then, 17.2); `W2` bit `0x0001` (proximity) of records 0 to 14 is cleared;
2. every 16th frame (frame counter low nibble 3) `0x2D0AA` runs (AI, not the player); the two damage cool-down counters
   `V:4A907` and `V:4A906` count down toward 0; `0x26F28` drops a stale target lock;
3. the **player**: `V:4AA6C` and `V:4AA6E` are set to 0, `+0x4C` decreases by 1, `W0` bit `0x0002` is cleared, then the sound cue `0x270A0`
   (a sound `8` once when `|(E + (E >> 3) + V:4A9FE) >> 3| >= 78`, with E the pitch rate `+0x56`), `0x27B1E` (does nothing for the
   player and returns 1), `0x2835A` (a sound `4` once while `S >= 0x1200` with the gear down; no damage), and **`0x2A2A2`**;
4. records 1 to 14 in order with their own `V:4AA6C` (AI, weapons, the carrier).

The player step `0x2A2A2` does, in this order (`V:4A866` set makes this routine itself return at once, the pre-steps of item 3 having run, 17.2; in the cockpit view the whole step always runs, in the external view the `W0` bits decide, 17.2):

a. If neither `W0` bit `0x1000` nor bit `0x0100` is set while the view is external, steps c to g are **all skipped** (no input conversion, warnings, speed, attitude or motion step; the displacement is zero) and only the
   cell bookkeeping (the code of item h with D = 0), items i and j run (corrected, 17.2: the other flag combinations are in the table there). If `W0` bit `0x0400` is set in the external view the dying path of the AI is taken (not part of the
   player's flight, see the crash hand-over).
b. If `V:4A984` bit 6 is clear, steps c to e are skipped (the attitude step still runs).
c. Input conversion `0x1DFAA` (section 2).
d. The warnings that depend on the state (section 12).
e. The speed step `0x15ED4` (sections 3 and 4), called only when `B20` bit `0x02` is clear, the class is a flying aircraft (high nibble of `+0x62` = 1) and `W0` bit `0x1000` is set (read from the call site at `0x2A49A`-`0x2A4BA`; the rig traces with class `0x00`, `0x20`, `0x30` show no speed rules, section 4).
f. The attitude step `0x31AE0` (sections 5 and 6) unless `W0` bit `0x8000` is set.
g. The motion step `0x16B0E` (section 7) gives the displacement D = (`+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46`); without `V:4A984` bit 6 the
   displacement is zero.
h. The position update and the cell bookkeeping (section 7), only with `W0` bit `0x1000` (the cell bookkeeping alone also runs, with D = 0, in the shortcut of the last row of the table in 17.2).
i. Gear and hook counters (section 8).
j. Unless `W0` bit `0x0400` is set: the collision routine (section 10 and 11) with the previous position (position minus D, also when the position update of item h did not run, 17.2),
   its result handled, or, with no result, the tail: if `B20` bit `0x02` and the wheels are down the entity becomes
   inactive (`W0` bit `0x1000` cleared) and enters the dying state (`W0` bits `0x0400` and `0x0200`, `+0x4C` = 15); otherwise `W0` bit `0x8000` is cleared, and then
   either the dying state is entered when `W0` bit `0x0200` (crashed) is set, or `W2` bit `0x0002` follows the contact flag `V:4A957`; finally the camera trail `0x2AC5A`.

**Rig convention** (used by the worked examples and by the differential test of Task 5; the single convention of the note): load `interceptor.bin` at `0x10000`; clear
the whole of hunks 71-73 (`0x4A6E8`-`0x54EBF`, the range that is also compared after every call); write the base block B0 below plus the changes of the case; patch a return instruction (`0x4E75`) over the first
word of `0x18E54` (system-clock sample through the Amiga OS), `0x1A18C` (visual effect), `0x1F11E` (re-arm effect), `0x1F0E2`
(AI hook); `0x2F75A` is **not** patched (the terrain query is pure); per step write the key byte to `+0x65` (and `V:4A928` if a
preset is wanted) and call `rig_run(0x26AF8)` with all registers 0; read the state afterwards. The examples run with the cockpit view (`V:4A83D` = 0). Before a first step the carrier
(see section 9) needs its vertex list. Entry `0x2A2A2` (A1 = `0x4B23C`) gives the same result for the player except for the three
pre-steps of item 3 (`+0x4C` minus 1, `W2` bit `0x0001` clear, `W0` bit `0x0002` clear). The landmark phase has one machine-dependent input, the constant z of section 11b; the rig image at `0x10000` gives z = 1, the reference machine 48, so a test of that phase takes z as a parameter.

**Base block B0** (every byte not listed is 0, in particular `V:4A890` = 0 (no pointer throttle), `V:4A903` = 0 (live keys), `V:4A928` = 0, `V:4A866` = `V:4A867` = 0, `V:4A952` = 0; `V:4A844` = 1 is added by the addendum: the game's value, no example has a collision result so no printed row changes, 17.12). "Needed" says whether dropping the word changes any worked-example trace. Only the words `+0x39`, `+0x63`, `+0x71`, `+0x7B`, `+0x7D` (low and high nibble separately), `V:4A95E` and bit `0x0800` of `V:4A984` were ablated (each set to 0 in turn, in each of the examples E1 to E9, comparing the whole variable block after every step apart from the word itself; E6 was compared on its printed columns only); the "yes" in the other rows (`+0x00`, the matrices, `+0x2B`, `+0x62`, `+0x7E`, `V:4A7B2`, `V:4A83C`/`V:4A847`, bit 6 of `V:4A984`) is read from the rules that use the word, not from an ablation run.

| address | value | meaning | needed |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `+0x00` | `0x11C8` | active (`0x1000`), brake off, entity valid and collidable; the start value | yes |
| `+0x02`, `+0x04` | per case | wheels down `0x0080` and pad `0x04` on a runway, 0 in the air | per case |
| `+0x14`, `+0x18`, `+0x1C`, `+0x66`, `+0x68`, `+0x6A` | per case | position and attitude | per case |
| `+0x80`..`+0x91`, `+0x92`..`+0xA3` | `fx_matrix_a(pitch, heading, roll)`; `fx_matrix_b` of `28800 - angle` for each angle (0 stays 0) | matrices consistent with the angles (the step uses `+0x96`, `+0x9C`, `+0xA2` and matrix A) | yes |
| `+0x2B` | 9 (or per case) | throttle | yes |
| `+0x39` | 1 | repeat counter, low nibble: the value the no-key rule leaves; 0 makes a held key step faster at once (E4 differs from step 1) | yes (E4, E5) |
| `+0x62` | `0x11` | class F/A-18 (`0x10` gives identical traces) | yes |
| `+0x63` | `0x0D` | id as the start routine sets it | no (no rule reads it) |
| `+0x71` | `0xFF` | as the start routine sets it; read only outside the step | no |
| `+0x72` | `0x61A800` | fuel (any positive value; only the fuel column differs) | no |
| `+0x7B` | `0xFF` | negative: three-vertex ground test off (the cockpit-cruise value; 0 switches it on, 17.12) | no here (no example has a vertex below y = 0) |
| `+0x7D` | `0x53` | low nibble L = 3 (model unit shift); high nibble unused | the low nibble (E5, E6) |
| `+0x7E` | `0x1400` | turn limit of the coupling (0 removes the coupling) | yes |
| `V:4A7B2` | `0x800000` | altitude ceiling | yes |
| `V:4A83C`, `V:4A847` | 1, 1 | feature switches (gust, sink and stall; bank-turn coupling) | yes |
| `V:4A984` | `0x0840` | bit 6 flight simulation running (needed); bit `0x0800` as in the captured game states (no effect on any rule of the step) | bit 6 |
| `V:4A95E` | 1 | display mode cockpit (only the value 2 changes the step, and only with the pointer throttle on) | no |

### 2. Inputs

The step reads one **key byte** `+0x65` per step plus the throttle preset `V:4A928`; the key handlers keep them (set on a key
press, cleared on release), and a recorded demo replays exactly this byte per frame (`V:4A903` = 3 reads it from the stream;
`0x2A1A6` records it).

| bits of `+0x65` | meaning | key |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0-1 | 1 throttle up, 2 throttle down, 0 none | `=` (raw `0x0C`) up, `-` (raw `0x0B`) down |
| 2, 3 | `0x04` roll right, `0x08` roll left | RIGHT (`0x4E`), LEFT (`0x4F`) |
| 4, 5 | `0x10` stick forward (nose down), `0x20` stick back (nose up) | UP (`0x4C`), DOWN (`0x4D`) |
| 6, 7 | `0x40` rudder right, `0x80` rudder left | `.` (`0x39`), `,` (`0x38`) |

Other inputs, as events that change state words directly: **gear** (`G`, raw `0x24`) toggles bit 7 of `+0x7C`, refused while
`W2` bit `0x0080` is set (wheels down); **hook** (`A`, raw `0x20`) toggles `W2` bit `0x8000` for the F/A-18 only (`V:4B29E` = `0x11`),
and toggles bit 7 of the lamp byte `V:4A8FF`; **brake** (BACKSPACE `0x41` or `\` `0x0D`) toggles `W0` bit `0x0800` and clears the mouse
throttle; **eject** (SHIFT and `E`) sets the low nibble of `+0x7C` to 10; **F1-F10** (raw `0x50`-`0x59`) store a preset in `V:4A928`:
`12 * n` for F-key n (F1 12, F2 24, ..., F9 108, F10 121); F1 again while at 12 stores -1 (idle); a preset of 121 means "full power and
then afterburner".

Command bytes. With an ejection nibble in `+0x7C` the command bytes are left as they are; with B4 bit `0x10` set and `+0x4C`
not negative they are set to 0; otherwise, each step:
- the pitch command `+0x28` goes up by 1 per step while bit `0x10` is held (to at most 20), or down by 1 while `0x20` is held (to at
  least -20); when the sign flips it jumps to 1 (or -1) at once; with neither bit held it is 0 at once;
- the rudder command `+0x29` does the same with bits `0x40` (positive) and `0x80` (negative), limit 20;
- the roll command `+0x2A` moves by 3 per step with bits `0x04` (positive) and `0x08` (negative), limit 60, jumping to 3 (or -3) when
  the sign flips. So a held key ramps the demand over 20 steps for pitch and rudder and over 20 steps for roll.

Preset handling (live mode, not paused). With a preset P in `V:4A928` (a negative preset counts as 0) and R the repeat counter (low
nibble of `+0x39`), D = P - T (computed in 8 bits as the original does; this only matters for T below -7):
- D = 0: if T < 120 or the afterburner is on, the key bits are cleared and the preset cleared; if T = 120 with the detent `0x2000` set
  and the afterburner off nothing changes.
- D > 0: the throttle bits are set to "up" (1) when D > 8 or R < 2, otherwise cleared.
- D < 0: the throttle bits are set to "down" (2) when D < -8 or R < 2, otherwise cleared.
So F10 (preset 121) raises T to 120, where the detent stops it and clears the preset; a second F10 then engages the afterburner.

The **mouse** is a throttle when `V:4A890` is 1 (the default): the pointer height `V:4A830` (-960..960, limits set by `0x19254`, corrected 17.4; the full follow and fire rules are in 17.4) follows
`T * 8` after key changes, and moving the pointer (a change of more than 16, or a position of `0x3C0`; corrected, 17.4) sets the preset to
`V:4A830 >> 3` (below 0: preset -1). The pointer's x position is not read by the flight code. A port without a mouse can ignore it.

### 3. Throttle, afterburner and fuel (`0x15ED4`, first half)

State: T, `+0x39` low nibble R, W2 bits `0x2000` (detent), `0x0400` (idle detent), `0x0008` (afterburner), fuel F.

- **Fuel zero** (F = 0): T becomes 0, the afterburner is cut (W2 bit `0x0008` cleared), the mouse pointer is reset; the speed rules take the
  "idle" branch. No fuel is ever added in flight (only the landing-complete event refuels, section 8).
- **No throttle key held** (low bits of `+0x65` = 0): R becomes 1 (high nibble kept).
- **A key held**: n = R + 1; if n is 9 or more R stays at 8 and n = 8, otherwise R = n; the step size is `n >> 1` (0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 3,
  4 for n = 1 to 8). A step size greater than 0 with T = 0 and the player viewed raises the centre notice `0xFE`.
  - *Up*: W2 bit `0x0400` cleared; T increases by the step; when it reaches 120: if bit `0x2000` was already set, the afterburner switches on
    (W2 bit `0x0008` set, sound `V:4A8F6` = 2, notice `0xFD`, `V:4A84F` = 6) when it was off; otherwise bit `0x2000` is set, the throttle bits of
    `+0x65` are cleared (the key must be pressed again) and the preset is cleared; T is 120.
  - *Down*: bit `0x2000` cleared; with the afterburner on it switches off and T falls by 1 (sound 2, notice `0xFC`); otherwise T >= 0 falls by 4 and
    if it would go negative: with B4 bit `0x08` (catapult) T = 0; else with W2 bit `0x0400` clear the key bits are cleared, T = 0 and the idle
    detent `0x0400` is set; with the detent set T stays negative (reverse thrust starts). A negative T falls by the step size, to at least -32.
- **Fuel burn** (every step the speed rules run with a non-zero thrust command): afterburner `0x600` (1536); otherwise `|T| * 256 >> 9`, which is
  `|T| / 2`, 60 at T = 120 (4 at T = 9 as in the idle example).
- **Warning thresholds** (`0x1557C`, player only, flight simulation on): F <= 0 sets F = 0, flag `V:4AC08` `0x40` (fuel-exhausted warning), clears the
  afterburner and sets `V:4A8F6` = 2 (the last two only in the step in which the flag is newly set; the flags `0x43` are replaced by the one flag, 17.5); F < `0x70800` (460,800) flag `0x02` (critical-fuel warning); F < `0xBB800` (768,000) flag `0x01` (low-fuel warning); above that the low-fuel flags are cleared (a flag already shown is not set again).

### 4. Speed rules (`0x15ED4`, second half)

Quantities: S and W as above; C = 256 * T (the thrust command, as a word); "gear down" means `+0x7C` bits 4-6 are all 0 (a gear that is
moving or up counts as not down); "brake" is `W0` bit `0x0800`; "ground" is `W2` bit `0x0080`.

**Drag term G** (`0x16A32`), computed only when S >= `0x800` (else G = 0):
G = 2 * |E| + B - P + A, all in 16-bit words, where E is the pitch rate `+0x56`; P is the pitch taken as a signed angle (values from 14400 up
are reduced by 28800) divided by 2 (arithmetic), so a nose-up pitch makes P negative and G larger; B is the roll folded into 0..7200
(an angle from 14400 up is replaced by `28800 - angle`, then an angle from 7200 up by `14400 - angle`) divided by 8; and A is
`(V:4A7B2 - y) >> 13` (shift 11 with the afterburner on), the air-density term: the higher the aircraft, the smaller A.

**Gate.** The speed step is entered only for an aircraft class (high nibble of `+0x62` = 1), with `B20` bit `0x02` clear and `W0` bit `0x1000` set (section 1, item e). Within it, the speed rules change S only when `V:4A984` bit 6 is set and `V:4A986` bit 14 is clear. Otherwise S is unchanged by them (the
special cases of the last paragraph and the gust update still run).

**Branch.** With the gate open:
- C = 0 and no afterburner, or fuel = 0: the *idle rules*;
- afterburner on: the *afterburner rules*;
- otherwise the *normal rules*.

**Afterburner rules.** Fuel falls by `0x600`. Vmax = `0x3A98 - G`, at most `0x4650`; with the gear down Vmax falls by `Vmax >> 2`; with the brake
on it falls again by `Vmax >> 2`.
- S < Vmax: if S is negative S rises by `0x78`; else in the air S rises by `0x2D` when S > `0x1D4C` and by `0x36` otherwise; on the ground
  the ground term `+0x26` relaxes toward -37 and S rises by `-(+0x26)` (new value).
- S > Vmax + `0x3C`: S falls by `0x3C`. Otherwise S is unchanged.

**Normal rules.** Fuel falls by `|C| >> 9`.
- *Air, C >= 0.* a = (C >> 1) - (C >> 3); Vt = a - (a >> 2); with `B20` bit `0x01` (skid) Vt >>= 2; Vt = min(Vt, `0x1FEF`); Vt = Vt - G; with the brake
  on Vt falls by `Vt >> 2`; with the gear down Vt falls by `Vt >> 2` again. (At T = 120: a = 11520, Vt = 8640 before the cap, 8175 after.)
  - S < Vt: if S is negative S rises by `0x78`; else S rises by 5 when `Vt - S < 0x150` and by `0x2A` otherwise; if S now exceeds Vt it is set to Vt.
  - S > Vt + `0x3C`: S falls by `0x52` when S > `0x1D4C`; else by 7 when `S - Vt < 0x1E0`; else by `0x3C`.
- *Ground, C >= 0.* Vt = C >> 3, then `Vt - (Vt >> 2)` (= `3C/32`: 2880 at T = 120, i.e. 240 knots); Vt = 0 when B4 bit `0x08` is clear and T < 12;
  then when B4 has neither bit `0x04` (pad) nor bit `0x40` (deck) Vt is halved (rough ground), and when it has one of them and `B20` bit `0x01` is
  set Vt = 0. The target rate r is 0; S < Vt: r = -`0x78` when S is negative, else -(`((Vt - S) >> 8)` + `0x42`) with B4 bit `0x08` (catapult) and
  -(`((Vt - S) >> 8)` + `0x16`) without; S > Vt + 15: r = 15. The ground term `+0x26` relaxes toward r and, when r is not 0, S falls by the new `+0x26`
  (a negative `+0x26` accelerates).
- *Air, C < 0 (reverse).* S > 0: S falls by `0x57`. S <= 0: Vt = |C| - (|C| >> 2); when -S > Vt, S rises by `0x2A`; when -S < Vt - `0x2A`, S falls by `0x2A`.
- *Ground, C < 0.* r = 0; S > 0: r = `0x3C`; else Vt = (|C| >> 4) - ((|C| >> 4) >> 2), halved without B4 bit `0x04`; r = -22 when -S > Vt, r = 22
  when -S < Vt - 22. The ground term relaxes toward r and, when r is not 0, S falls by the new `+0x26`.

**Idle rules.**
- *Air.* S < 0: |S| falls by `0x52` when it is above `0x90`; otherwise by `0x3C` when it is above `0x3C`; otherwise it becomes 0. S >= 0: first S falls by `0x52` when S > `0x1D4C`;
  then, judging by the old S: when S < `0x5DC`, S falls by `0x1E` if S > `0x1E`; when S >= `0x5DC`, S falls by `0x3C` if S > `0x3C`, else S becomes 0
  (so the two fall-offs add above `0x1D4C`, and a slow aircraft keeps about 30 speed units).
- *Ground.* The ground term relaxes toward 0; |S| falls by 15 (to 0 below that).

**After the rules** (also when the gate was closed). For the player, with the new S: when S >= `0x20D0` (700 knots) and `V:4A98A` bit 14 was clear it is set, with sound `V:4A878` = 3, notice `V:4A968` = `0xFA`,
`V:4A84F` = 2; below `0x20D0` the bit is cleared.

**Final selection of S**, using the **old** S unless it is the plain case:
1. hook down and engaged (`W2` & `0xC000` = `0xC000`): slew(S old, 7, 3): the arrestor;
2. on the ground, brake on, S old > `0x40`: slew(S old, 15, 4): wheel brakes;
3. on the ground and gear retract commanded (`+0x7C` bit 7): slew(S old, 31, 5): belly drag;
4. `B20` bit `0x01`: slew(S old, 3, 2): skid or ditching;
5. B4 bit `0x08` (catapult armed): when `|T| < 0x6C` slew(S old, 7, 3) (the shuttle holds the aircraft), otherwise the new S (so the catapult
   releases only with the throttle at 108 or more);
6. otherwise S = the new S.
Then the gust update below, and W = S + `+0x78`.

**Gust and sink** (`0x2AB64`). With `V:4A83C` = 0 both `+0x76` and `+0x78` are 0. Otherwise, with the new S: c = `0x2AB18`[min(|S| >> 7, 30)];
p = pitch >> 3 (0..3599 tenths of a degree); k = `256 - (sin(900 - p) >> 6)` for p <= 1800 and `(sin(p - 2700) >> 6) - 256` above 1800, where
`sin(i)` is the word at `0x436E8 + 2*i` (a negative i reads the 16 bits before the table; only an attitude beyond the vertical does that), so k is 0
in level flight, positive in a dive (for example 35 at 30 degrees nose down) and negative in a climb. The target is `(c * k) >> 3`, reduced by a quarter
(`target - (target >> 2)`) while the gear is not commanded up (`+0x7C` bit 7 clear), and the gust `+0x78` is 0 on the ground. The gust moves toward the
target: when `+0x78` is above the target, it falls by `(+0x78 - target) >> 8` if that difference is above 255, otherwise by 1 (unchanged at 0, set to 0 when
negative); when it is not above the target it becomes `+0x78 - ((+0x78 - target) >> 5)` (arithmetic shift: it rises by ceil((target - gust) / 32), so by at least 1 while below the target and not at all when equal to it). The **sink term** is
`+0x76 = 0x2AAD8[min(|W old| >> 7, 30)]` with the **old** W (before this step's update): 512 at rest, 144 at 1280, 1 at 3840 and
above. It is a vertical velocity added in the motion step (4 times it is the altitude lost per step): this is how a slow aircraft sinks. There is no
other gravity or lift in the model. With `V:4A83C` = 0 there is no sink and no gust.


### 5. Control surfaces: stick to angular rates (`0x1557C`)

Runs from the attitude step when `V:4A984` bit 6 is set; without it the routine is not called and the flags `V:4AC08` are untouched (corrected, 17.7: the clearing branch of the routine is unreachable). `W2` bit `0x0040` is cleared at the
start of each pass. Notation: pc, rc, qc are the pitch, rudder and roll command bytes (`+0x28`, `+0x29`, `+0x2A`); E, Y, Q are the three rate words
`+0x56`, `+0x58`, `+0x5A`; the response table is `0x425D8` for the player (`0x42654` for other entities unless their `W2` bit `0x0100` is set); with B4 bit `0x08` (catapult)
rc and qc count as 0. A target rate is `+table[|command|]` for a positive command and `-table[|command|]` for a negative one.

Helper rules (all word arithmetic):
- **decay(x, k)**: x < -15 becomes `x + ((-x) >> k)`; x > 15 becomes `x - (x >> k)`; otherwise x moves one unit toward 0, **0 included: 0 becomes -1** (corrected, 17.7).
- **boost(x)**: s = `S >> 7`; a rate x >= 0 gains s per step when x >= `0x500`; a negative rate x loses s when x <= -`0x500`; inside that band nothing is added.
- **pitch-rate(a)**: skipped in the air when `+0x26` is non-zero and a <= 0; with the stall flag (`B20` bit `0x04`) a is halved (arithmetic); then E becomes
  `E - ((E - a) >> 2)` and boost(E) follows.
- **yaw-rate(a)**: a' = `(a >> 1) + (a >> 3)`, halved when stalled; Y becomes `Y - ((Y - a') >> 2)`, then boost(Y).
- **roll-rate(a)**: a' as for yaw; Q becomes `Q - ((Q - a') >> m)` with m = 1 (2 for class 0x14 aircraft), then boost(Q).
- **nose settle** (on the ground): nothing when pitch is 0. Below pitch `0x190` (5 degrees): `W2` bit `0x0040` is set (the first time, with a cosmetic bump) and E becomes 0. Otherwise
  E moves toward -`0x40` (pitch below `0x3840`) or `+0x40` by `E - ((E - c) >> m)` with m = 1 when S >= `0x6C0`, else 0 (immediately).
- **roll reset** (on the ground): Q becomes 0 when the roll is 0, `+0x20` when the roll is above `0x3840`, else -`0x20`.

Pitch:
- pc != 0: a = target(pc). In the air: pitch-rate(a). On the ground: with `B20` bit `0x01` set, or S <= `0x300`, or pitch non-zero and not above `0x6D60`: nose settle and roll
  reset; otherwise (fast enough and level or nose-up past `0x6D60`): a stick-back command (pc < 0) calls pitch-rate with the **half-index** target `-table[|pc| >> 1]`, a stick-forward command does the
  nose settle and roll reset.
- pc = 0: on the ground nose settle; in the air, when E != 0 and `+0x26` = 0, decay(E, 2) for the player (1 for others).

Rudder:
- rc != 0: with B4 bit `0x02` clear (not on water) and W != 0: yaw-rate(target(rc)); otherwise decay(Y, 1).
- rc = 0: unless qc is non-zero on the ground, decay(Y, 1) when Y != 0.

Roll:
- qc != 0: on the ground qc is first limited to +-10. In the air roll-rate(-target(qc)). On the ground, with B4 bit `0x02` clear and W != 0: yaw-rate(target(qc)) (steering with the roll keys), then if the
  roll angle is below `0x50` or above `0x7030` and W > `0x360`: roll-rate(-target); otherwise with a roll below `0x3840`: roll-rate(-target) when qc >= 0 else Q = 0, and with a roll from
  `0x3840` up: roll-rate(-target) when qc <= 0 else Q = 0. With B4 bit `0x02` set or W = 0: decay(Y, 1) and decay(Q, 1).
- qc = 0: on the ground roll reset; then decay(Q, 1) when Q != 0.

Last: for the player, with the flight simulation on and fuel not 0: the stall flag `B20` bit `0x04` with the wheels up sets `V:4AC0B` bit `0x80` (stall warning, message index 1; lamp `V:4AC0F` bit `0x20`) the first time and then
resets the lamp counter `V:4A9A8` to 2 when it is negative; without the stall the bit is cleared.

### 6. Attitude (`0x31AE0`, `0x32426`, `0x325B8`)

Run in this order (player; `V:4A866` set means all three rates are 0):
1. rates: (p_r, y_r, q_r) = (E, Y, Q) from section 5, or 0 when `V:4A867` is set.
2. **Bank-turn coupling** (`0x32426`, called only with `V:4A847` set and not at all with `V:4A867`/`V:4A866`; with `W0` bit `0x8000` set it runs to its end with zero contributions and `V:4A9FE` = 0, 17.8): let r = roll >> 3 (tenths of a degree, arithmetic). Sector by r: r < 900: angle a = r, state 0;
   900 <= r < 1800: a = 1800 - r, state 1, cosine negated; 1800 <= r < 2700: a = 3600 - r, state 1, cosine negated; r >= 2700: a = r - 1800, state 0. (s, c) = sine and cosine of a in Q14 (table folding of `0x32DB2`;
   the same folding and out-of-range reads as the matrix builder). `+0x22` = s, `+0x24` = c. State 0: `+0x50` = 0 and, if it was not 0, `+0x54` is negated; state 1: `+0x50` = -1 and, if it was 0, `+0x54` is negated.
   m = |s|, negated when c < 0. `+0x54` becomes `+0x54 - step` with step = (d >> 3 when |d| > 7, else +-1; 0 if d = 0) for d = `+0x54 - m` (a rate-limited follower of m). Let mag = |`+0x54`|: the
   sign bookkeeping sets `+0x52 = +0x50` when (`+0x54` < 0 and c < 0) or (`+0x54` >= 0 and c > 0), and negates (s, c) when c is outside +-256 on the "wrong" side and `+0x50`, `+0x52` agree
   in sign (read from the code, the case is rare). mag = min(mag, `+0x7E`), then `mag - (mag >> 2)`. Pitch contribution d3 = `((-(s << 7)) >> 13) * mag >> 13`, heading contribution
   d5 = `((-(c << 7)) >> 13) * mag >> 13`; both are scaled by `0x3259A[f >> 8]` (f = pitch, or `28800 - pitch` when pitch >= 14400; no contribution when f > 3600) and `>> 8`. They are added to p_r and y_r, and
   d3 is stored in `V:4A9FE`. (Coupling is a lagged bank: the more bank, the faster the heading changes, and the pitch rate follows.)
3. **Composition** (`0x325B8`, a pure function: input the matrix A at `+0x80` and three rate words; output three angles): negative rates get +28800; B = `fx_matrix_a(p_r, y_r, q_r)` (the same builder
   as the attitude matrix: the rates act as small angles); the product P (nine longs, Q28) has `P[3j + i] = A[i] * B[3j] + A[3 + i] * B[3j + 1] + A[6 + i] * B[3j + 2]` for i, j = 0..2 (A, B as stored). The new angles come
   from P with the inverse tables:
   - *pitch*: s = -P[7]; for |s| < `0xE210000`: i = (high word of s) >> 4, plus 1 when the last bit shifted out was 1, table `0x42E92`; for |s| below `0xFF60000`: j = (|s| >> 16 rounded the same way) - `0xDDB`
     (at most `0x225`), table `0x43098`; above: j = (|s| >> 12 rounded) - `0xFF08` (at most `0xF8`), table `0x434E8`; for the two fine tables i = +-j with the sign of s. With T the table: i >= 0 gives pitch' = T[i]
     and complement k = 900 - T[i]; i < 0 gives pitch' = 3600 - T[-i] and k = 900 - T[-i]. cs = the sine-table word at index k (= cos of the pitch); if cs < `0x8F` then pitch' = 910 (i >= 0) or 2690 and cs = -286.
   - *heading*: x = quotient(-P[6]); t = 2x. t >= 0: when t <= `0x180` the angle is `0x42E92`[x]; otherwise x2 = quotient(P[8]) and the angle is `900 - 0x42E92`[|x2|]. t < 0: with |t| <= `0x180` d = `0x42E92`[|x|],
     otherwise x2 as above and d = `900 - 0x42E92`[|x2|]; the angle is `3600 - d`. Quadrant fix with the signs of P[6] and P[8]: P[6] >= 0 and P[8] >= 0: unchanged; P[6] >= 0 and P[8] < 0: A = (5400 if A > 1800 else 1800) - A;
     P[6] < 0 and P[8] < 0: A = 1800 - A; P[6] < 0 and P[8] >= 0: unchanged. heading' = |A|.
   - *roll*: the same with P[1] and P[4] in place of P[6] and P[8].
   - *quotient(v)* (v one of the signed 32-bit values of P above; cs the cosine word found for the new pitch). The original works with a divisor d that starts at cs for each composition and is **shared by all the quotients of that composition**, heading first (one or two quotients) and then roll. A quotient has two forms, chosen by the current d (signed word compare):
     d <= `0x147`: d becomes 64 d (16-bit wrap) and the quotient is v / d truncated toward zero; d > `0x147`: the quotient is (v >> 6, arithmetic) / d truncated toward zero, then rounded: when the remainder's magnitude is at least floor(|d| / 2) (the remainder is compared with |d| >> 1, so for odd d the threshold rounds down) the quotient moves one unit up if it is zero or positive and one unit down if it is negative
     (so a zero truncated quotient rounds to +1 whatever the sign of v). Consequence: for cs in `0x8F`..`0x147` the first quotient is v / (64 cs) and every later quotient of the same composition uses the second form with d = 64 cs; for cs above `0x147` only the second form occurs with d = cs; the pole value cs = -286 gives the divisors -18304 and 8192 in the first form and then the second form with 8192.
     A quotient that does not fit a signed 16-bit result is replaced by the low 16 bits of its numerator (v, or v >> 6 in the second form), and the remainder used by the rounding is then the high 16 bits of that numerator. Rig-checked on 2000 random matrices.
   - The three results are multiplied by 8 (angle units 28800 per circle).
4. **Roll recentre** (`W2` bit `0x0010`, cleared when seen): with the old roll r, a step of min(distance/2, `0x50`) (1 when the distance is 1) toward 0 or `0x3840` (the nearer upright or inverted
   attitude by the quarter ranges 0..`0x1C20`, `0x1C20`..`0x3840`, `0x3840`..`0x5460`, `0x5460`..`0x7080`) replaces the composed roll.
5. **Pitch override**: state byte `+0x05` = `0x0A`: pitch moves toward level by `0x50` per step (up past `0x3840` it increases to `0x7080`, which becomes 0); `W2` bit `0x0040` (nose settled): the same with `0xA0`.
6. **Stall** (`V:4A83C` set, otherwise skipped): a nose drop happens when `B20` bit `0x02` is set, or when the aircraft is flying (`W2` bit `0x0080` clear) with an aircraft class (high nibble 1, so `+0x62` = `0x10`, `0x11`, `0x14`, `0x1F` all qualify; verified in the rig, the same state with class `0x00`, `0x20`, `0x30` never stalls) and indicated speed W is at or below the threshold
   Th: p2 = pitch >> 1; when p2 < `0x1C20` then p2 = `min(p2, 0xE10) + 0x3840`; u = (`0x4650 - p2`) as an unsigned word times 108 >> 8; Th = u + (((`+0x22 >> 8`) as unsigned word * u as unsigned word) >> 6)
   (the roll sine enters the product as an unsigned 16-bit number, so for a negative sine the sum wraps modulo 65536; an original quirk the port must copy). If Th >= W: `B20` bit `0x04` set; the nose drops when E, Y and Q are all 0 or when `Th >> 1` >= W;
   if Th < W: `+0x26` = 0 and `B20` bit `0x04` cleared. *Nose drop*: E = 0; `+0x26` becomes `+0x26 + ((0x50 - +0x26) >> 2)`; pitch = pitch + `+0x26` (>= `0x7080` gives 0; from `0x3840` up kept; at or below `0x12C0`
   kept; between them reduced by `0x50`). The stall threshold at level flight is 1518 (126 knots); at 30 degrees nose up it is about 2025.
7. **Gimbal guard** (when no override applied and the old pitch is steeper than 75 degrees, that is strictly between `0x1770` and `0x5910`, a signed 16-bit compare: an old pitch word of `0x8000` or more skips the guard): let dh = |new heading - old heading| and dr = |new roll - old roll| (words; for words outside 0..28799 the "absolute value" has the wrapping rule of 17.9, "The guard with angle words outside 0..28799" [rig]).
   The heading has "flipped" when `0x4B0` < dh < `0x6BD0` (then dh becomes |dh - `0x3840`|), the roll when `0x4B0` < dr < `0x6BD0` (then dr becomes |dr - `0x3840`|). If the roll has not flipped the new angles are used unless the heading
   flipped; if it has flipped they are used when ||dr - dh|| < `0x320` (dh as reduced when it flipped, else unreduced). Otherwise the step is abandoned and **no angle is stored**: when `W2` bit `0x0004` is clear it is set and, when
   the pitch command `+0x28` is non-zero, E gains `0x90` in the direction of its own sign; when the bit was already set Y gains `0x90` in the direction of its sign.
8. The angles are stored (`W2` bit `0x0004` cleared) and the matrices rebuilt: `+0x80` = `fx_matrix_a(pitch, heading, roll)`, `+0x92` = `fx_matrix_b` of the angles `28800 - angle` (0 stays 0).

### 7. Motion: velocity, floor, altitude and position (`0x16B0E`, `0x29C18`, `0x17288`, `0x2A2A2`)

**Target velocity.** n = (`+0x96`, `+0x9C`, `+0xA2`) is the nose direction (Q14 words). With sp = -W (word): if sp = 0 the target vector is 0; otherwise the target
components are `n_i * sp / len` with these truncations (the routine `0x29C18`; `0x20884` gives len):
1. len: a is the larger and b the smaller of |n_x| and |n_y|; i = floor(256 b / a) (0 when b = 0); h = a * `0x208E8`[i] (unsigned 32-bit, about the xy length times 16384); w = |n_z| * 16384;
   A = max(h, w) >> 14 (as a word) and B = min(h, w); j = floor(B / A) >> 6 (0 when A = 0); len = (`0x208E8`[j] * A) >> 14, at most `0x7FFF`. A zero len gives a zero vector.
2. Normalise |sp| by a power of four against len. Case |sp| <= len: let k >= 1 be the least integer with 4^k |sp| > len; then m = 4^k |sp| and e = 8 + 2k. Case |sp| > len: let j = min(J, 4) with J >= 1 the least integer
   such that (|sp| >> 2J) <= len (j = 1 for any unit nose vector); then m = 4 (|sp| >> 2j) and e = 10 - 2j. Either way q = floor(256 m / len) (an unsigned 16-bit quotient; for a unit nose vector q is at most 1024 and always fits; a quotient that does not fit is replaced by the low 16 bits of 256 m).
   (This closed form equals a direct step-by-step transcription of the original's scaling on 200,000 random pairs; the routine itself was compared with the rig in 3000 calls, see "Verified by".)
3. component i = floor(n_i * q / 2^e), negated when sp is negative (that is, when W is positive: the target velocity in this routine's sign convention is the negative of the nose displacement).

**Smoothing.** Define the velocity v as the negative of the stored displacement over 4 (`v = -(+0x3E)/4` and likewise `+0x42`, `+0x46`). Each step, for x and z (only when the wheels are not down) and for y (always):
v_new = v + ((t - v) >> s), t the target component, s = 2 when |t - v| > 4, 1 when it is 3 or 4, 0 when it is 2 or less (arithmetic shift: a negative difference rounds down). On the ground x and z take the target
unsmoothed. For y, when `V:4A857` bit 0 is clear the sink term `+0x76` is then added to v (positive = down). The displacement is D = (4 v_x, 4 v_y, 4 v_z) with the signs stored: `+0x3E` = -4 v_x, `+0x42` = -4 v_y, `+0x46` = -4 v_z.
With `V:4A866` set the vertical displacement is 0 for this step. (`V:4A857` = 1 or 3 or `V:4A858` != 0 are special scalings never selected in normal play; with 3 or 1 a constant `0x6000` is
subtracted from or added to the vertical term.) So an aircraft turns with its velocity lagging the nose by a quarter per step and the sink term pulls a slow aircraft down.

**Altitude and floor.** y becomes `y + (+0x42)`. The floor F (the height of the aircraft's reference point when its wheels are on the surface):
- with B4 bit `0x40` or `0x80` set (over the deck): F = `(+0x4E << 8) + c`, at least c, where c = `0x2EE` when the gear is not fully down, else `0xA8C` for class `0x14` and `0x708` otherwise;
- else: -`0x200` for class 0 (ground objects), `0x2EE` when the gear is not fully down, else `0x708` (`0xA8C` for class `0x14`).
(The runway and the sea are at y = 0, so a gear-down aircraft rests at y = 1800 = about 9 ft, gear up at 750; the terrain has no other height. The deck of the carrier lies at `+0x4E` = 112, i.e. 28672, plus the same offset: 30472.)
- **y above F**: airborne. If the wheels were down (`W2` bit `0x80`) this is the **lift-off**: that bit is cleared, B4 bits `0x08` and `0x02` are cleared (sound `V:4A878` = 4 when the catapult bit was set),
  `+0x21` bit 0 is set, `W2` bit `0x1000` and `V:4A98A` bit 1 are cleared, `V:4A982` bit 8 is set (armed for landing complete), and the clock statistics are updated (needs the system clock: patched in the rig). Otherwise y is limited to `V:4A7B2`.
- **y at or below F**: `B20` bit `0x04` cleared; y = F (for class != 0). With B4 & `0xC0` = 0 and either the wheels up or `(+0x4C & 7)` = 0, the terrain query (section 10) runs; over water with the gear fully down `+0x7C` bit 7 is set (the gear is lost) and
  `B20` bit `0x01` (skid) is set. Then, for the player:
  - `W0` bit `0x0200` already set: only the vertical velocity is zeroed.
  - **Touchdown** (wheels not yet down): `W2` bit `0x80` set, `+0x26` = 0, `V:4A857` = 0, sound 1, statistics. When the vertical v_y **of this very step** (the smoothed word the motion step has just produced, descent per step over 4 including the sink term, before the step zeroes `+0x42`) is **at least `0x240`** (576, 2304 position units
    per step) and `V:4A952` is 0: crash (`W0` bit `0x0200`, `V:4A87D` = 1, `V:4A850` = 4). Otherwise (v_y below `0x240`, soft touchdown only, 17.10), with W != 0: S = W, notice `0xFE`; when over water or the gear is not fully down and W >= `0x3C0` (80 knots) `W2` bit `0x1000` is set and the camera follow `V:54E72` is
    set; a jolt effect depends on v_y > `0x180`. The vertical velocity `+0x42` is zeroed.
  - **Rolling** (wheels down): when W != 0 and `V:4A98A` bit 1 is set it is cleared (sound 3, `V:4AC0E` bit 1). When W = 0 and that bit is clear: sound 4; when `V:4A982` bit 8 is set it is cleared and, if B4 bit `0x04` (airfield pad) or `W2` & `0xC000` = `0xC000`
    (arrested), the **landing-complete** event runs (`0x1F11E`: message `0x4005`, fuel = `0x61A800`, `+0x5F` = `0x24`, `+0x60` = 500, `V:4A8FB` = `V:4A8FC` = 3, `V:4A904` = `V:4A905` = 16, weapon bookkeeping cleared, records 1 to 3 cleared); then `V:4A98A` bit 1 is set. With W = 0 and fuel 0, `V:4A986` bit 14 is set (checked each step). The vertical velocity is zeroed.
  - Finally, when W < `0x3C0`, `W2` bit `0x1000` is cleared.

**Position** (only with `W0` bit `0x1000`): x = x + `+0x3E`; if the sum is negative z is set to 0 first (so the new z is just `+0x46`; an original quirk) and x is left negative; a sum above `0x1FFFFFFF` is limited to it;
z = z + `+0x46`, limited to 0 below and `0x1FFFFFFF` above. A class `0x15` entity gets y = `0xB000`. Then the cell fields: `+0x06` = `((x >> 16) as a signed word) >> 6` (also `+0x08` from z; a change sets `V:4A910` = `0xFF`), `+0x0C` = `(x & 0x3FFFFF) >> 8`,
`+0x0E` = `(z & 0x3FFFFF) >> 8`, `+0x10` = y >> 8, `+0x0A` = 4 * (3 - (`+0x0E >> 12`)) + (3 - (`+0x0C >> 12`)) (low bytes; when it changes and the cell is within 2 of the viewed cell `V:4AA04`/`V:4AA06` the neighbour index `+0x0B` is set to `0x0F`; corrected, 17.10).

### 8. Gear, hook, brake, ejection and the lamp counters

- **Gear** (`+0x7C`): the key toggles bit 7 only while the wheels are not down. Each step the byte is processed in two parts. The high part: when bit 7 is set and the progress (bits 4-6) is below 6 it rises by 1; when bit 7 is clear and the
  progress is above 0 it falls by 1; either change requests the sound `V:4A8FD` = 3 (corrected, 17.10); this is the retract and extend animation (6 steps). While the progress is non-zero the gear counts as "not fully down" for the floor (750 instead of 1800), the speed targets and the gust. The low nibble (ejection cycle): when it
  is non-zero it counts up from 1 to 6 and holds at 6 until `W2` bit `0x0020` is set (then it returns to 0 and that bit is cleared); a value above 6 first counts down to 7 and restarts at 1. The ejection key starts it at 10. (When the high part changes in the same step the byte is stored with the low nibble cleared.)
- **Hook** (`W2` bit `0x8000`) has no motion of its own; the lamp byte `V:4A8FF` counts 0..5 per step toward its target (bit 7 = lit, count up; clear = count down).
- **Brake** (`W0` bit `0x0800`): see the speed rules (target speed minus a quarter in the air, wheel braking on the ground). The same toggle is used for both.
- **Camera trail** (`0x2AC5A`): when `V:54E72` equals the entity offset and the game is not paused, the position is appended to a ring of six samples at `V:54E74` (12 bytes each, the first sample doubled while the
  ring has fewer than 5), the count `V:54E70` and index `V:54E71` advance, and `+0x3D` follows the count while the aircraft moves (`W2` bit `0x1000`); this feeds the crash and ejection cameras only.

### 9. The carrier: catapult, deck, arrestor

**Start on the carrier** (`0x1D730`): B4 is set to `0xC8` (deck bits and catapult armed), y = `(deck_y << 8) + 0x708`, `W2` = wheels down only, `+0x2B` = 9, `+0x7C` = 0; the position comes from the start blocks (note 02, section 9).
The carrier is a class `0x20` entity (record 14 in the captures, id `0x3D`, group `0x0E`, `+0x7D` = 3, stationary in free flight: speed 0) whose model is **model 8** (model 9 is its variant): its record holds, from `+0xA4`, the model's near-level
vertex array rotated by the carrier's matrix (slot k at `+0xA4 + 6k`, `(M . v_k) >> 8`, `M` = carrier `+0x92` matrix `>> 6`; the first 58 slots fit the record). This was checked exactly against a running game: all 58 slots equal the extractor's
model 8 level 0 vertices rotated that way. The slots are written by the draw pass, so the carrier must have been drawn before the first step, and a different level of detail drawn last would put that level's vertices there (open question 9).
The carrier's fine position words `+0x0C`, `+0x0E` and `+0x10` are written by its own step.

**Catapult.** While B4 bit `0x08` is set: S is held at 0 unless the throttle is `0x6C` (108) or more (final selection rule 5); the ground acceleration uses the constant `0x42` (about three times the runway's `0x16`) and the ground target is
`3C/32` even below T = 12; the rudder and roll commands are ignored. The bit is cleared at lift-off. With T = 120 and the afterburner the aircraft leaves the bow about 40 steps after the release (example E5). Nothing else models the shuttle.

**Deck contact** (inside `0x2B5E2`, before the landmarks). For each other entity with `W0` bit `0x0040`, none of `0x0600`, a different group byte `+0x5E`, and `W0` bit `0x0008` set on either record: class `0x20` entities are tested with a box of
half-size `0xA0000` around the carrier position against the player's **previous** position (position minus the displacement). On a hit the carrier's `W2` bit 0 and the flag `V:4A957` are set and the deck test runs in two passes (pass 0: the player's
model vertex 0 at `+0xA4`, pass 1: vertex 3 at `+0xB6`; for the F/A-18 these are the nose (0, -10, 384) and a point behind the main gear (32, 0, -184) in model units, rotated by the attitude).

For a pass: P = ((x_prev & `0x3FFFFF`) >> 8, y_prev >> 8, (z_prev & `0x3FFFFF`) >> 8) plus the rotated vertex `>> L_p` (L_p = `+0x7D` & 15 of the player). Let L_c be the carrier's L, list T1 = `0x3E0F8` (`0x3EDCC` for another class)
and S0 the first structure it names (a pointer list at `0x3E0F8`: longs, with a word `0xFFFF` ending each group). The first word of S0 is the byte offset of the **reference vertex** r; refy = (the y word of vertex r) `>> L_c`.
- **P.y < refy** (below deck level): the volumes named by the **remaining** pointers of the first group, then the following groups, are tested (a *volume* is a group of plane structures; plane = three vertex offsets in words 1 to 3 of its structure,
  word 3 masked with `0xFFF`; normal n = `(V[w2] - V[w1]) x (V[w3] - V[w1]) >> (7 + L_c)` (the cross product with 32-bit products, each component then taken as a 16-bit word); the point is inside the volume when `(P - V0) . n < 0` for every plane, with
  V0 = V[w1] converted as `(x >> L_c) + xc`, `(y >> L_c) + (+0x10 of the carrier)`, `(z >> L_c) + zc` (xc, zc the carrier's `+0x0C`, `+0x0E`); a plane with `(P - V0) . n >= 0` skips the rest of its group). Inside any volume: the result is `0x20` (hull hit). No hit: no further test (corrected, 17.11: the scan of this entity ends, with no polygon test, no deck bit and no second pass).
- **P.y >= refy**: S0 holds deck polygons: each is a list of vertex byte offsets (bit 15 on the last one; where the list starts and ends: 17.11), edges from each vertex to the next and the last to the first; with A the edge start and B its end (model units, words) and
  P - A in 256-unit steps, the point is inside when `(P - A) . (-(B.z - A.z), B.x - A.x) < 0` for every edge. The first polygon that contains P is the deck. Then: B4 bit `0x40` (pass 0) or `0x80` (pass 1) is set and `+0x4E` = refy.
  In pass 1 with the wheels down (`W2` bit `0x80`) the **arrestor corridor** is tested with three vertices a, b, c of the ship's record, at byte offsets from the record start: for the **carrier (class `0x20`)** `0x1E8`, `0x1EE`, `0x1F4` (slots 54, 55, 56: the aft deck, z about -3400 to -4700 model units; the offsets used by E6); for **any other class** (the second ship type, lists `0x3EDCC`/`0x3EDEC`) `0x1AC`, `0x1B2`, `0x1B8` (slots 44, 45, 46). For a vertex u let U = (`(u.x >> L_c) + xc - P.x`, `(u.z >> L_c) + zc - P.z`) be the vector from P to u in 256-unit steps (16-bit words), and for two vertices u, w let
  E(u, w) = (`u.x - w.x`, `-(u.z - w.z)`) in model units; define t(E, U) = `E.z * U.x + E.x * U.z`. The tests, all of which must pass: t(E(a, c), U_c) >= 0; t(E(a, c), U_b) < 0; t(E(b, c), U_c) < 0; t(E(b, c), U_q) >= 0 with q = a + ((a - c) >> 1) (a point beyond a); and the
  nose direction agrees with the carrier's: `(+0x96 of the player) * (+0x96 of the carrier) + (+0xA2 of the player) * (+0xA2 of the carrier)` is at least `0xE000000` (cosine 0.875, about 29 degrees). All true: `W2` bit `0x4000` is set; otherwise it is cleared unless the hook is down
  (`0x8000`) and the bit was already set (once caught, the wire keeps the aircraft); with the wheels up in pass 1 the tests count as failed (corrected, 17.11). Then the second list T2 (`0x3E174`; `0x3EDEC` for another class): let y1 = (the y word of the vertex whose offset is word 1 of the first structure of its first group) `>> L_c` and y_p the player's
  **current** `+0x10`; when y_p < y1 the remaining pointers of that group are tested as one volume (as above), otherwise the same is done for the second group with y2 = (the same quantity from the second group) + 7 and y_p < y2. A volume hit returns `0x20`.
- After pass 0 comes pass 1 (the viewed entity only). A hull hit returns `0x20` at once; otherwise the scan goes on. (The vertex term and pass 1 apply only when the mover is the viewed record; any other mover is tested once at its bare previous position: note 07 section 10.2; rig-confirmed by `tests/rig/test_ship_diff.c` `ships`: the viewed player, a non-viewed missile and a viewed missile against ship records, 3,000 cases, 0 differences in the result `0x20`, `V:4A957`, B4 bits 6 and 7, `+0x4E` and W2 bits `0x4000`/`0x8000`.)

**Arrestor.** The wire is not a separate object: with the hook down (`W2` `0x8000`) and the corridor flag (`0x4000`) set, rule 1 of the final speed selection slews S to 0 (an eighth of the speed is lost per step; from 144 knots the aircraft stops in about 40 steps,
example E6). Landing complete (refuel and re-arm) follows when the aircraft is stopped (`W` = 0) and `W2` & `0xC000` = `0xC000`.

**Leaving the deck.** When both deck bits (0x40, 0x80) are clear the floor is the plain 1800 (gear down); an aircraft still at deck height is then airborne: lift-off event, then it sinks by the sink term (large at low speed) until the speed
or the pitch holds it; example E5 shows y falling from 30472 to 14600 at the end of the shot.

### 10. Terrain class: water and airfield pads (`0x2F75A`)

The step asks the terrain only on touchdown or while rolling on a surface (section 7). The routine is a pure function of x and z and sets or clears two bits of B4; both parts were reproduced bit for bit and compared with the original on more than 7000 random positions
(including 276 inside pads and several thousand on both sides of the coast).

**Water** (B4 bit `0x02`). cell column = the unsigned byte `(x >> 24) & 0xFF`, row = the unsigned byte `(z >> 24) & 0xFF` (0..31 for a position inside the world; a negative x reads cells 128 to 255 of the grid, never a word in front of it; corrected, 17.10) [rig]; the grid word at `0x47F40 + 2 * (column + 32 * row)` is the byte offset of the cell record from `0x47F40`; a value of 0 or less means "no polygon" and clears the bit. A record starts
with a word that must not be negative (else the bit is cleared) and one more header word; then comes a list of polygons: a count word n > 0 followed by n vertices (x, z) of two words each in units of 1/4096 cell (a vertex word times 4096 is a cell-relative position in position units); the word `0xFFFF` ends the list (bit cleared); a non-positive count other than `0xFFFF` is followed by a word that replaces it. The point is (xc, zc) = (`x & 0xFFFFFF`, `z & 0xFFFFFF`).
For each polygon, for each edge from vertex i to vertex i+1 (the last to the first) with the vertices times 4096 as X1, Z1, X2, Z2 (longs):
- dx = X2 - X1, dz = Z2 - Z1; an edge with `dx >> 8` = 0 is skipped. If |dx >> 8| exceeds `0x7FFF`, dx and dz are both halved (arithmetic shifts) the least number of times n that brings |dx >> 8| >> n to `0x7FFF` or less (the magnitude is tested, the signed values are shifted; the original also tests for a zero magnitude after a halving, but that test can never fire because the value is above `0x7FFF` before every halving).
- Slope ladder (|dx|, |dz| after the halving): |dx| >= 4|dz|: num = dz * 64, den = dx >> 10, e = -16; |dx| >= 2|dz|: dz * 64, dx >> 9, e = -15; |dx| >= |dz|: dz * 64, dx >> 8, e = -14; 2|dx| >= |dz|: dz * 32, dx >> 8, e = -13; 4|dx| >= |dz|:
  dz * 16, e = -12; 8|dx| >= |dz|: dz * 8, e = -11; 16|dx| >= |dz|: dz * 4, e = -10; else dz, dx >> 8, e = -8. slope = num / den as a signed 16-bit division (an overflowing quotient leaves the low word of num).
- u = xc - X1 (a long). The original tests the **magnitude** and shifts the **signed value**: m is the least number of places with |u| >> m <= `0x7FFF` (magnitude shifted, then compared; it is not "halve the signed value until its magnitude fits"), u becomes u >> m (arithmetic, rounding toward minus infinity) and e is increased by m. The two readings differ for a negative u that lies just beyond a power of two times `0x7FFF`: u = -65535 gives m = 1 and u' = -32768, where halving the signed value and re-testing its magnitude would give m = 2. (Neither reading of the pin is observable: both give identical edge heights for every u and slope, 17.15 C16.) The edge height at the point is ez = Z1 + ((low word of u * slope) >> -e) (a left shift by e when e >= 0).
- If ez < zc nothing is counted. Otherwise: if |xc - X1| < `0x300` or |xc - X2| < `0x300` the whole query ends with the bit **cleared** (a point within 768 position units of a vertex column is never "in the water"); else the edge counts when Z1 = Z2 and
  X1 < xc < X2 (the two ordered), or when Z1 differs from Z2 and ez lies strictly between Z1 and Z2 (ordered).
After the polygon's edges: an odd count sets the bit and ends; an even count goes on to the next polygon of the cell. (A vertical-edge test thus casts a ray toward +z.)

**Pads** (B4 bit `0x04`). The table `0x47B72` has 16-byte entries (word 0: the offset of an object record from `0x2600C`, then five words): cell x, cell z, a direction index d, offsets ox and oz; a first word of -1 (word at +2) ends it, a negative cell x skips the entry.
R = (cell x * 2^24 + dirx * 1024 + ox * 1024, cell z * 2^24 + dirz * 1024 + oz * 1024) with (dirx, dirz) the words at `0x206F2 + 4 d`. An entry is a candidate when |R.x - x| <= `0x180000` and |(R.z - z) >> 8| <= `0x180000` (the second test is weaker than it looks: an
original quirk). The long at `0x2600C + word0 + 0x10` is a pointer to a list of 16-byte quads (four (x, z) word pairs in units of 256 position units), ended by `0xFFFF`; the player is on the pad when for some quad all four edges satisfy
