`e.z * v.x + e.x * v.z >= 0`, with e = (x_{k+1} - x_k, -(z_{k+1} - z_k)) and v = (((x_k << 8) + R.x - x) >> 8, ((z_k << 8) + R.z - z) >> 8). The four entries are the airfields of note 02 section 9 (object ids 46, 72, 73, 95 of note 02, the descriptors above).

### 11. Collisions, crash conditions and the hand-over

All crash causes of the step, for the player (flight constants as in the sections above):
1. **Hard touchdown**: wheels reach the floor with the v_y of the touchdown step itself >= `0x240` (descent of 2304 position units per step, about 11 ft per step) and `V:4A952` = 0 (section 7).
2. **Hull hit** on the carrier (result `0x20`, section 9) or a **landmark hit** (result `0x20`, section 11b) at indicated speed above `0x3C0` (80 knots) with `V:4A952` = 0 and `V:4A844` set; at or below 80 knots the contact sets `W0` bit `0x8000` and, the first time, the bump effect `0x1A0DC` (corrected, 17.12: a bump sound with the sound system running, the notice `0xFA` with `V:4A84F` = 2 without it).
3. **Aircraft collision** (result `0x40`): damage path (milestone 5, see below).
4. The **wheels-up / ditching** case is not a crash by itself: gear up or water at 80 knots or more sets only `W2` bit `0x1000` (skid trail and camera follow) and the skid slows the aircraft (final speed rule 4).
5. No structural limit crashes the aircraft: overspeed (700 knots), high turn rate and gear-down speed above 384 knots only raise warnings and sounds; fuel exhaustion stops the engine (S decays by the idle rules and the aircraft sinks by the sink term; it then ends in cause 1 unless it lands gently).
There is no terrain relief: the only surfaces are y = 0 (sea and land alike), the carrier deck and landmark volumes.

*Result handling* (`0x2A758`-`0x2A9C0`, player): a result 0x20 zeroes E, Y and Q and, for class != 0, undoes the displacement (x and z, and y when airborne); the cell fields are refreshed; with an ejection nibble the dying path is taken; result 0x10 (the three-vertex ground test, disabled for
the player while `+0x7B` is negative: the cockpit block is drawn, 17.12 and note 04a section 8a) sets the wheels-down bit and does not undo the displacement. Both set `W0` bit `0x8000`, and when the speed word after this step's speed step exceeds `0x3C0` (and `V:4A952` is 0) `W0` bit `0x0200`, the statistics counter, `V:4A87D` = 1 and `V:4A850` = 4. In the external view the finalisation follows at once: `B20` bit `0x02`
(out of control), `W0` bit `0x1000` cleared (the entity stops being simulated); then `W0` bit `0x0400` and `+0x4C` = 15 (dying countdown), `W0` bit `0x0200`; the finalisation does not depend on `W0` bits `0x1000` and `0x0100` and, with an ejection nibble, is made in the cockpit view and without the crash conditions as well (17.12). (Corrected, 17.12: an explosion record, id `0x11` for a hull or landmark hit and `0x12` or `0x13` by slot parity for a ground result on land, none on water, is copied into the first free slot of the effect table at `0x4D23C` and the position is projected to the surface for a ground result **only for a class-0 entity (ground objects), never for the player or another aircraft**; an earlier version of this paragraph listed both as part of the player's finalisation.) In the cockpit view this result handler does **not** finalise (read from the code: after setting `0x0200` and the statistics the handler tests the view selector `V:4A83D` and, for the cockpit, goes straight to the camera trail at the end of the step; no `0x0400`, no `+0x4C` = 15, no explosion record, `0x1000` kept). The finalisation is reached only by the tail of a step with **no** collision result while `0x0200` is set (that tail also clears `0x8000`). The earlier statement that the cockpit finalisation happens at the tail of the next step is **not observed**: in `stall2.txt` word 0 reads 0x93C8 after the impact step, 0x91C8 in the hand-over pass (at the two later counter turns, 17.17), so through the hand-over pass `0x0400` is never set, `0x1000` stays set and `0x8000` stays set. Order, from the code and the log: in every pass of the frame routine the mission state routine (the call of the current state routine at `0x11956`, the last act of the routine `0x11748`, which the frame routine `0x11124` calls at `0x11134`, so before the entity update; 17.1) runs **before** the flight step; the flight state `0x12EFE` (its hand-over branch) therefore clears `0x0200` at the start of the pass that follows the impact pass, so the step of that pass no longer sees `0x0200`. That `0x8000` is still set at the end of the hand-over pass means the collision routine returned a result again in it (the no-result tail would clear it; the restart pass A shows `0x8000` once more, for another reason, 17.17 rule 2): consistent with the aircraft lying still with its nose vertex below the ground, the result `0x10` recurring (section 14 and 17.12). A result 0x40 (aircraft
collision) at speed above `0x3C0` is the damage path: first hit sets `B20` bit `0x02` and posts the engine-failure warning (message `0x9014`), later hits (state `V:4A993`) post the minor hydraulic or structure damage messages (`0xD00B`, `0xD00C`), with a cool-down `V:4A907`/`V:4A906`;
the full damage model belongs to milestone 5.

*Hand-over to the mission flow* (the crash branch of the flight state `0x12EFE`; verified on the reference emulator, 17.17): with `W0` bit `0x0200` set, no ejection nibble **at the test** (the crash pass itself clears a non-zero nibble first, so an ejection that is under way does not prevent the hand-over: end code 4, the 40-pass hold, the allowance 5 to 4, the restart that frees the seat, no request 2 [emu: note 07 6.4 and 6.5, `followup-combat-crash-after-eject.txt`, and the crash in the third pass of `followup-combat-batch-b.txt`]) and collisions enabled, the crash statistic is incremented, `V:4A87D` = 1, `V:4A982` bit 8 and `V:4A984` bit 6 are cleared (the simulation stops), `W0` bit `0x0200` is cleared, and the state becomes the crash wait state `0x138D8`;
in the cockpit view only (`V:4A83D` = 0, tested at `0x12F80`) it also sets `V:4A952` = 1 (immune), zeroes the vertical velocity, sets `V:4A951` = 40 (`0x12F98`), stops the four sound channels and starts the crash sound `0x1A0DC`; in the external view the branch at `0x12F88` skips all of that, so `V:4A951` stays 0 and **the crash freeze of section 14 does not occur** (captured, 17.17). The wait state `0x138D8` tests the view first: in the cockpit it restarts when `V:4A951` is 0, in the external view when the player's `W0` bit `0x0400` is clear; it then calls `0x1D538` (the start of section 15, entered after the lines that reset the view, so the view stays), decrements the crash allowance `V:4A94F` (3 by default, 5 in free flight, 1 in game mode 0x7D: set at `0x120BC`, `0x1217A`, `0x12202`) and, while it is still positive, goes on through the resume states `0x13980` and `0x139C2` to the flight state again (17.17: seven passes without simulation); at 0 it takes the game-over path `0x139F0` instead, which shows the screen with text id `V:4A802` = `0x62` (F-16) or `0x63` (F/A-18);
the two game-over text blocks at `0x44157` and `0x441A3` are those texts (the first for the F-16, the second for the F/A-18, in the order of the ids `0x62`, `0x63`); the id-to-block table is in note 08 section 2 (ids `0x62` and `0x63` name the records at `0x44153` and `0x4419F`). With collisions disabled (`V:4A844` = 0, training) the crash is only cleared and the message `0x4021` (the collisions-off notice) is posted. (Corrected, 17.17: the earlier text called `0x12EFE` the hand-over state and named a state `0x12FB8`, which is only the operand of the instruction that stores `0x138D8`.)

*11b. Landmarks* (`0x2BC28`-`0x2C08C`, the second half of `0x2B5E2`; verified against the rig with synthetic slot data, 5700 random cases, about 400 hits, 0 differences). After the entity scan an aircraft (class high nibble 1; the player is `0x10` or `0x11`; this class test was read from the code, the rig comparison used `0x11` only) is tested against the slots of the last tier C build (note 04a section 7), when `+0x10` (y >> 8) is at most `0x7FFF`.
The data is produced **at build time** by the tail of the display-list builder (tier C pass 2, `0x210A2`-`0x211E4`; note 04a section 7, which settles this: it is not a per-frame post-process, and when tier C is not rebuilt the slots keep their contents): it walks queue C backwards from `V:4AA7E` (the last emitted entry, in generated order before the per-frame sort and reorder), over at most `V:4A916` (emitted count) entries, skips entity and secondary entries (flag bits `0x10` and `0x40`) and fills at most 11 slots, into two tables in hunk 72: the pointer table `0x5283C` (11 longs: the address of the model's collision data, or -1) and 11 slot records of 256 bytes at `0x52868`.
A slot record is the model's collision data translated by the instance's offset (x and z fine offsets, shifted by the instance scale and limited to 14 bits): bytes `+2` and `+3` are the landmark's cell bytes (compared with the low bytes of the player's cell words `+0x06`, `+0x08`, that is `+0x07` and `+0x09`); at `+4` the outer box (five words: maximum height in y >> 8 units, minimum x, maximum x, minimum z, maximum z, in fine units relative to the landmark's cell);
at `+0x0E` the first inner node: the same five words, a long that is the address of the next inner node (negative at the end), and then, from `+0x1C`, one reference point (x, y, z words, the x and z offset by the instance) per plane. The model's own collision data (immutable, in the image; the pointer table entry) has the same node structure with, per node, a count word at `+0x0E` and from `+0x10` the planes
as 12 bytes each: a 6-byte reference point and a 6-byte normal (three words); its node header (10 bytes of box and a long next pointer) is 14 bytes.
The test for slot s (the collision code re-walks queue C backwards from `V:4AA7E` every frame and numbers the non-entity entries 0 to 10 in the **current** queue order, which can differ from the builder's order after the sort and reorder in rare cases (3 of 400 random states in note 04a's rig run); this matters only for which hit is found first; a skipped record uses no slot): let dx and dz be the byte differences between the player's cell bytes and the slot's; |dx| and |dz| must be at most 1. The player's fine position relative to the slot is
X = `+0x0C` + dx * 16384, Z = `+0x0E` + dz * 16384 + **z**, with z = (the high word of the pointer-table entry) >> 2, the entry being the address of the model's collision data. This is an artefact of the original's address arithmetic (the high word of an address leaks into the position), so z depends on where the loader put the model data. **On the reference machine** (Kickstart 1.2, program in slow RAM from `0xC00000`) the entries read from four captures (airborne, cockpit-ground, cockpit-carrier and demo snapshots, `peek F:5283C 44`) are all in `0xC375B4`-`0xC38BD0`, high word `0x00C3`, so z = **48** (any address in `0xC00000`-`0xC3FFFF` gives 48; `0xC40000` and up gives 49); a stock machine with the program below `0x80000` would give 0 or 1; the rig image at `0x10000` gives 1 for data from `0x40000` up and 0 below. Effect: the player's fine z is treated as 48 fine units (12288 position units, 60 ft) larger, so every landmark collision volume acts as if shifted 12288 position units toward -z (south). **Port rule** (the port reproduces the reference machine): z is a named constant of 48 fine units; the rig differential test takes z as a parameter (1 for the rig image).
The player's Y = `+0x10`. The outer box contains the player when Y is not above the maximum height, X is strictly between the x limits and Z strictly between the z limits (an equal bound rejects). Then `V:4A957` is set to 1 and the chain of inner nodes is walked (the box test again; a miss goes to the next node through the long at node `+0x0A`, in the slot copy and in the model data in step; a negative
next pointer abandons the slot). When an inner node contains the player and `W2` bit `0x0002` is set (the contact flag of the previous step; otherwise no result) its volume is tested with three points, in this order, until one is inside: model vertex 0 of the player (`+0xA4`), 1 (`+0xAA`) and 2 (`+0xB0`), each rotated vertex shifted right by L (`+0x7D` & 15) and added to
(X, Y, Z): vertex 0 as longs (the y as a word sum, then sign-extended), vertices 1 and 2 with the x and z sums as words first. A point is inside the volume when for every plane `n . (point - reference point)` is negative (word products, 32-bit sum; the y difference is a word). Inside: the result `0x20`. If all three points are outside, the whole landmark phase ends (later nodes and slots are not tried). The phase also ends when the walked records reach `V:4A916` or 11 slots were used.
The last test (`0x2C08C`) is the three-vertex ground test of the model (slots 0 to 2, y words `>> L` plus `y >> 8` below 0, only when `+0x7B` is non-negative, an aircraft class (high nibble 1) and `(+0x7B & 15)` = 0): result `0x10`; (it, the landmark phase and the ship test read the **mover record's own** `+0xA4` slots for every record, not only the player's: note 07 4.12, rig-confirmed;) for the player it is live whenever the renderer's part id `+0x7B` is non-negative with a zero low nibble, which is the band-and-eye condition of 17.12 and not a height rule (corrected, 17.12).

### 12. Warnings, notices and the HUD figures

Warning flags the step sets (read by `0x13D4C`, which maps them to messages, and by the panel lamps; note 06):
- `V:4AC08` low byte: bit 0 low fuel (message index 9), bit 1 critical (index 2), bit 6 exhausted (index 4), bit 7 stall (index 1); the chooser prefers exhausted over stall over critical over low fuel.
- Direct posts: `0x9C06` (crash-imminent warning) when the descent will reach the ground within 16 steps in a nose-down attitude between `0x1E0` and `0x3840` (the product `|+0x42| * 16 >= y` with a descending `+0x42`), or, in the air, when the roll is beyond 15 degrees (between `0x4B0` and `0x6BD0`, so banked or inverted) and y <= `0x1800`; `0xD02A` (eject warning) when out of control (`B20` bit `0x02`) with `|+0x42| * 64 >= y` (no ejection nibble); `0xD00A` (proximity warning) when another entity's scan set `W2` bit 0 and the cockpit is shown. All three set
  `V:4AC0C` bit `0x80` (or `0x40` for eject) and the first two skip the speed step in the step that posts; a post is made only when `V:4AB98` differs from the code, a refused post sets and skips nothing (corrected, 17.13: the acceptance rule, the order and the cue bits are there).
- Message `0x4005` (landing complete), `0x4023` (hook down), `0x4021` (collisions off) as above. Index 8 (the maximum-altitude message) has no poster in the code searched (the ceiling clamp is silent).
- Notices `V:4A968`: `0xFE`, `0xFD`, `0xFC`, `0xFA` (sections 3, 4, 7); sounds `V:4A878`.

HUD figures derived from the state (the figures of the cockpit and of the external-view text line):

Status column: "verified" means the formula was confirmed by running the real HUD or panel routine (note 06, "Readout formulas" and the in-flight reconciliation) or by a capture value; "read only" means read from the code and not exercised.

| figure | formula | check | status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| speed, knots | `abs(W) / 12`, **truncated** (0 when `W0` bit `0x8000` is set) | 352 for 4231, 355 for 4271 (the running routine, note 06) | verified |
| altitude, feet | `5 * (y >> 10)` (arithmetic shift) | 5 ft at y = 1800; 145 ft at y = 30472 (`demo-deck.ppm`); the panel digits are a cache refreshed every 4 steps, at most 5 steps stale (01b, note 06) | verified |
| heading | `(+0x68 >> 3) / 10` | 028 at 2240 (the runway frame) | verified at that frame, other values read only |
| thrust, percent | `abs(T) * 256 / 307` | 7 at T = 9, 100 at T = 120 | verified (note 06 sweep) |
| fuel | `+0x72 >> 8` | 25000 full | verified (note 06 sweep) |
| G meter (tenths) | `abs(v)` for `v = ((E + (E >> 3) + c) >> 3) + (10 if `V:4A982` bit 1 else -10)`, c = `V:4A9FE` when its magnitude exceeds 1 else 0; printed with a minus sign when v >= 0, blank otherwise; level flight (v = -10) prints 1.0 | 1.0 at rest (01b); the routine sweep is in note 06, the other figures of 01b are not reproduced from the state (open question 11) | verified as a readout of E, c and the bit; the relation to the physical state beyond 1.0 G is open |

The other HUD items that depend on the attitude words (the heading tape and the ball bank, and the 3D horizon, which is the world itself) use `+0x66`, `+0x68`, `+0x6A` directly (note 06); the original draws no pitch ladder (next paragraph).

**There is no vertical-speed readout** in the original: note 06 (section "HUD ... elements") establishes that the cockpit code draws no vertical speed, no pitch ladder, no velocity vector and no ILS cue (verified on every cockpit capture), and no HUD routine in `0x35000`-`0x39000` reads the vertical displacement `+0x42` for a figure. The figures above are the whole set. **The port must not show a vertical-speed figure** (a `vspeed_fpm` derived from the altitude change per step is a port invention and breaks the replica; Task 5 report, question 5) [read; note 06].

### 13. F/A-18 and F-16

The step has **no per-type constants**: with the same initial block the two classes (`+0x62` = `0x11` and `0x10`) gave identical traces for 200 steps of the takeoff and 40 steps of the manoeuvre examples. The type enters through:
- the hook: only class `0x11` (`V:4B29E`) toggles it; the F-16 has none;
- the model: the aircraft models are ids 4 (F/A-18) and 3 (F-16); the deck test uses the rotated model vertices 0 and 3 (F/A-18 (0, -10, 384) and (32, 0, -184); F-16 (0, 0, 304) and (24, 0, -160)), the shift L = 3 for both;
- the cockpit eye point (`0x1F45E`): the F/A-18 (0, 5, 20), the F-16 (0, 4, 18) in model units;
- the game-over text id (`0x62` F-16, `0x63` F/A-18) and the menu choice `V:4A901` (`0x11` F/A-18 by menu key 1, `0x10` F-16 by key 2).
Class `0x14` entities (other aircraft) have a higher floor (`0xA8C`) and slower roll lag; they are not the player's types.

### 14. Time base

**The original's rule.** One simulation step per displayed frame; no flight rule reads the clock or scales by elapsed time (note 04). The frame rate comes from two things: the frame-rate limiter (`h20+0x562`, `0x297D6`, note 04), which after each frame waits until the average frame time over the current window of 1 to 8 frames reaches the limit-table entry for the setting `V:4A976` (the table holds 1000 / n milliseconds for setting n; the start setting 15 gives 67 ms, a ceiling of 15 frames per second), and the display flip, which waits for the vertical blank (50 Hz on a PAL machine), so every frame lasts a whole number of 20 ms blanks. Between those, the frame rate is whatever the scene costs to draw.

**Measured on the reference machine** (note 01b, "Simulation step rate", the step counter `V:4A992` against the blank counter `V:4A82C`, 64 s per scene): open sea 9.27 steps per second, bay area and city in flight 7.24 (samples b000 to b023), external view 6.64, carrier deck idle 8.25; the **flight-only mean over these four scenes is 7.97 steps per second** (230 samples, standard deviation 1.30, 10th to 90th percentile 6.3 to 9.8; 1698 steps in 10,654 blanks). The fifth row of 01b, 5.60 steps per second, is the aircraft **parked** on the runway after a crash (samples b027 to b069), not a flight. The one-second sample that read zero steps in the city run was a crash one cell short of the airfield (the player y reaches 1800, the gear-down floor; the terrain bits of the record say sea, see item 1 below and 01b) followed by a freeze of the step counter and a mission restart (next paragraph), not a slow scene. The 15 Hz ceiling is never reached; the machine is the limit.

**The step freeze after a crash** (verified on the reference emulator for the cockpit view; the external view is read only). 01b logged the step counter standing still for 93 and 95 blanks (about 1.9 s) after the impact, then resuming after the restart. Mechanism (listing, `0x12F98`, `0x1827C`): the hand-over code sets the counter `V:4A951` to 40 at the crash, in the cockpit view only (section 11); the display flip routine at `0x1827C`, called once per frame by the main loop after the frame routine (`0x17EE6`-`0x17EFE`), runs a loop at `0x182EC`-`0x18372` for as long as `V:4A951` is positive, decrementing it once per pass and drawing in each pass; the loop is inside one call, so the frame routine, which increments the step counter (the update starts with the read of the counter at `0x114B0`, the increment is at `0x114B6` and the write back at `0x114B8`; the same sequence is meant wherever another note gives one of these addresses), is not entered for the 40 passes. The draw calls inside a pass (`0x58E9C`, `0x58F64`, library glue) were not identified and need not be: the pass length was measured.

*Pass length, measured.* The stall script (`openers-steprate-stall.txt`, cut to the crash: one blank per command, the restored airborne state, the same UP hold) was rerun with `V:4A951` and the step counter peeked after every blank from blank 1080 on (one run, reproducing the 01b crash; log `extracted/captures/logs/crash-hold.txt` (git-ignored with `extracted/`), script `tools/vamiga-ref/scripts/openers-crash-hold.txt`; the blank numbers below count from that run's first peek, called blank 0 at the first sample). Sequence in that run: the step counter reached its value 418 and `V:4A951` was first seen as 40 in the same sample (blank 30), so that step is the **hand-over step** (the one after the impact step, see the next paragraph, not the impact step itself); the next step (419, blank 38) is a normal step, 8 blanks later, with `V:4A951` still 40; this is the **last step before the freeze** (corrected, 17.17: the counter turns at the end of a pass, so the turns at blanks 30 and 38 here are the end of the impact pass and the end of the hand-over pass; the hand-over pass is the last pass before the hold, and no further pass runs before it). `V:4A951` first reads 39 at blank 40, **2 blanks after that last step**, and then falls by exactly one every **2 blanks** down to 0 at blank 118 (40 decrements, 39 intervals of 2 blanks in 78 blanks, no iteration of any other length in this run; the seven runs of 17.17 saw one 3-blank iteration in four of them, so the first fall to 0 took 78 or 79 blanks and the last turn to 0 took 80 or 81, rule 3). So the 40 passes last **80 blanks** (1.6 s at 50 Hz, a 25 Hz flip) counted from the last step before the freeze (blank 38) to the counter reaching 0 (blank 118), the first pass ending 2 blanks after that step. The step counter turned to 420 at blank 132, **14 blanks after `V:4A951` reached 0**: that turn is the end of the restart pass A (record rewrite, world update without simulation, display-list rebuild), not a step that moves anything (corrected, 17.17: A is the first of seven passes without simulation, the first simulated step is the eighth pass counted from A, about 77 blanks after the hold ended (blank 118) and about 63 blanks after this turn at 132). Total from the end of the hand-over pass (blank 38) to the end of pass A (blank 132): 94 blanks (80 + 14), inside the 93 to 95 of 01b, which therefore measured the hold plus pass A, not a stall that ends with a step. The two 01b runs give, by subtracting the 80, tails of 13 and 15 blanks (pass A) from the same anchor (the counter reaching 0); the 13 that 01b counts from the rewrite of the player record to the next counter turn is a different anchor, and in the second 01b run the two coincide (the last step at blank 1108 plus 80 is blank 1188, the rewrite), which is consistent with the 80 but was not checked there, since that run peeked no `V:4A951`. The earlier figure of "2.3 blanks per pass" divided the whole stall, restart included, by 40 and is withdrawn.

*Impact pass and hand-over pass (from `stall2.txt`, one sample per blank; verified unless marked; corrected, 17.17: there is no extra step. **Counter convention, one for this section and 17.17:** a pass is named by the value of the step counter `V:4A992` while it runs, up to its own turn at its end; a value read in a sample names the pass that is running in that sample, and the sample of a turn is the first sample of the pass the new value names, not the pass that just ended. The impact pass is 0x19E here, the hand-over pass 0x19F, and the value 0x1A0 from blank 1108 names the restart pass A, which runs after the hold; "the step" of a pass is its player step.)* Step numbers are the step counter `V:4A992` in hexadecimal; a step's work is spread over a few blanks after the counter turns, so a sample can show the counter already advanced and the record not yet changed.

| blank | step counter | what the log shows |
|---|---|---|
| 1085 to 1091 | 0x19D | y = 5588 (0x15D4), word 0 of the player record 0x11C8 |
| 1092 | 0x19E (**impact step**) | y = 1800 (0x708), the floor of section 7 for gear down |
| 1093 | 0x19E | word 0: 0x11C8 to 0x93C8, i.e. the bits 0x8000 and 0x0200 (crashed) are set **in the same step that brings y to 1800**; the neighbouring record words change as well (not decoded) |
| 1098 | 0x19E | the panel speed cache `V:4A9B0` (the "s" column of the log; it is not the speed word and lags it) goes to 0x8000 |
| 1100 | 0x19F (the end of the impact pass; the **hand-over pass** begins) | word 0: 0x93C8 to 0x91C8, bit 0x0200 cleared |
| 1108 | 0x1A0 (the turn at the end of the hand-over pass; the hold begins; 0x1A0 names pass A) | nothing further changes; the hand-over pass is the last pass before the freeze (17.17: there is no extra pass) |
| 1109 to 1200 | 0x1A0 | no step; at 1188 the player record is rewritten (restart pass A, cell words 0x40/0x43 to 0x41/0x42), at 1201 the counter turns 0x1A1 (the end of A, not a step that moves anything) |

What this settles. (1) The crash is **flagged in the impact step itself** (verified): the position that gives y = 1800 and the crashed bit 0x0200 appear in the same step, 0x19E. The earlier reading that the collision test sees only the position left by the previous step, so that detection comes a step after the y = 1800 step, is **withdrawn**. The earlier **inference** that the floor clamp is what raises the crash flag is **dropped**: the record word contradicts it. The touchdown of section 7 (the rig's E8 trace) gives word 0 = 0x17C8 (0x0200 from the touchdown, then 0x0400 from the no-result tail), while the reference shows 0x93C8: `0x8000` set and `0x0400` never, which is the collision-result handling of section 11 (`0x8000`, plus `0x0200` above 80 knots, the cockpit finalisation deferred).

*Reproduction on the rig (verified for what it reproduces).* The memory of the airborne snapshot (every hunk of the loaded program) was placed in the rig at its real Amiga addresses, so that the pointers and the landmark z artefact of section 11b are those of the reference machine, with the patches of the rig convention; the step was run with the key byte (UP for 17 steps, then none). The descent reaches the logged displacement exactly (-10632, -4376, 19820 position units per step, speed word 5727; the panel cache of the log reads 0x1663 = 5731), at a position a few hundred fine units from the logged track (the hold of the reference script is not reproducible to the step), so the logged state of step 0x19D (cell 0x40/0x43, fine x 0x2D94, fine z 0x2C70, y 5588; the low bytes of the long positions are not logged) was written into the record and one step run. Result: y 1800, fine x 0x2D6A, fine z 0x2CBD or 0x2CBE (the log has 0x2D6A and 0x2CBE), B4 = 0x02 and W2 = 0x2080 as in the log, but word 0 = 0x17C8 and no collision result. The log's B4 (byte +4 of the record) reads 0x04 until the impact (the stale airfield-pad bit of the take-off) and 0x02 from the sample after it: the terrain query of section 10 ran at the touchdown and answered **water**. So the cause of this run is cause 1 of section 11 (hard touchdown: v_y 0x446 = 1094 per step, above the 0x240 limit) **on the sea**, one cell short of the airfield (01b's earlier "ground, not the sea" is corrected there); the terrain itself returns no collision result (it only sets B4).

*The extra `0x8000` (a collision result in the same step): settled in 17.12, it is result `0x10` of the three-vertex ground test; the candidates examined before that were:* Candidates and evidence: (a) result 0x20, landmark or carrier hull: the rig gives exactly 0x93C8 for it (157 positions of a three-by-three-cell scan with the contact flag forced), but the result handler then **restores x and z to the pre-step values** (verified in the same scan), whereas the log shows the advance 0x2D94 to 0x2D6A kept in the following samples: contradicted; moreover no landmark outer box of the snapshot's slots (rebuilt for the crash cell in the rig: the same set) contains the logged positions even with the contact flag forced, and the carrier is at cell 70/65, six cells away; (b) result 0x10 (the three-vertex ground test): judged inactive for the player because `+0x7B` was read as negative (corrected, 17.12: `+0x7B` had turned 0, so this is the cause); (c) result 0x40 (another aircraft): sets `0x8000`, keeps the position and the touchdown's `0x0200`, skips the cockpit tail (so no `0x0400`) and, for an aircraft lying still, recurs in the hand-over pass (the extra step this candidate also named was withdrawn, 17.17) (which keeps `0x8000` set, section 11): fits every logged word, but needs an active second record, and the snapshot has none (only the carrier) and the stall logs recorded no record but the player. **Settled by the rerun** of the stall script peeking `W0` of records 1 to 14 and `+0x7B` (17.12, open question 14): records 1 to 13 invalid, the carrier six cells away, `+0x7B` turned 0 in the step before the impact: (b). (2) The hand-over of section 11 (it clears bit 0x0200 and sets `V:4A951` to 40) runs in the **next** step, 0x19F (bit cleared at blank 1100, verified); `V:4A951` itself was not peeked in this run, but the pass-length run (`crash-hold.txt`) sees it become 40 in the sample where the counter turns 418, which is the same position in the sequence (the hand-over step, N+1), so 0x19F here is step 418 there (inferred by the matching pattern; the pass run logged no y or record). (3) (Corrected, 17.17: the step counter turns at the **end** of a pass, so the turns at blanks 1100 and 1108 are the end of the impact pass and the end of the hand-over pass; no pass runs between the end of the hand-over pass and the hold (0x1A0 names the restart pass A, which runs after the hold). The hand-over pass runs its single step, the dispatcher having cleared the bit at its start, and the flip-routine loop of the 40 passes starts after it, so no step runs from blank 1108 to 1201 (93 blanks): the turn at 1108 is the end of the hand-over pass and the turn at 1201 the end of pass A. 01b's "the counter advances twice after the impact" counts the ends of the impact pass and of the hand-over pass; `+0x4C` falls by exactly one between the first `0x0200` sample and the hold.) (4) The first 01b run (`stall.txt`) logged no y and no record word, so its impact step is not identified; its freeze begins after step 0x1A3 at blank 1117 (verified from `stall.txt` and `stall2.txt`: its counter reads 0x101 at the first sample where the second run's reads 0xFF, 2 counts ahead, and 3 ahead at the freeze, 0x1A3 against 0x1A0), so whether it shows the same three-step pattern, or one more step, is **unverified**.

*Port rule (corrected, 17.17).* After a cockpit-view crash the port runs the hand-over in the dispatcher of the pass after the impact pass, runs that pass's step, and then stops stepping the flight for `CRASH_HOLD_PASSES` = 40 iterations of `CRASH_PASS_BLANKS` = 2 display blanks each (80 blanks = 1.6 s at 50 Hz; as a time `CRASH_HOLD_S` = 1.6), counted in blanks of the 50 Hz clock rather than in port frames; **there is no extra step after the hand-over pass**. Then the restart pass A (the dispatcher calls `0x1D538`; the pass also runs the world update without simulation, 17.17 rule 2, the display-list rebuild and the counter, 14 blanks), the resume pass, and five waiting passes follow, none of which simulates (`V:4A984` bit 6 is clear), and the eighth pass after the hold is the first simulated step (17.17: 76 to 77 blanks in the cockpit view, 91 in the external view, from the start of the restart pass to the start of that step). In the external view there is no hold (see section 11). This resolves the zero sample of 01b (a stall, not a slow scene): the counter does not turn for 93 to 95 blanks (the hold plus restart pass A: 1108 to 1201 = 80 + 13, and the cockpit flow capture 18 to 113 = 81 + 14), and then it turns in every pass; the seven passes from A on keep counting but nothing moves in them.

**Consistency checks** (rig and captures agree with a design rate near 7): the unit conversions give a step of 1/7.2 s (a speed unit is 1/12 knot, a knot is 1.6878 ft/s, a step moves 4 position units per speed unit and a foot is 204.8 position units: `dt = (12 / 51.2) / 1.6878` = 0.139 s); the 01a measurement "rest to about 240 knots in 22 s with F10" takes 134 steps in the rig, 6.1 steps per second;
the 01b pitch holds (UP for 1.0 s gave +1112 units, 2.2 s gave +4144) correspond in the rig to 11.7 and 21.4 steps, 11.7 and 9.7 steps per second in a sky-only scene, the lightest one.

**Ruling for the port.** The controller's ruling (plan and ledger, not a finding of this note) is that the port runs the flight step at a fixed rate of **8 steps per second by default**, a named constant (`FLIGHT_STEP_HZ`) whose comment cites this evidence (the flight-only mean of 7.97 over the four flight scenes of 01b, rounded to 8; the parked 5.60 and the crash freeze are not flight; the 15 Hz limiter ceiling is not the design rate). The step is a fixed time step, independent of how often the port draws. A scene-dependent rate (9.3 over open sea, 6.6 to 7.2 over land) may be offered as an option; it is not the default.

### 15. Start of a flight (`0x1D4E2` and neighbours)

Status: **verified in the rig** for all three routines (`0x1D4E2`, `0x1D8B8`, `0x1D858`): each was run on a block filled with `0xFF` bytes and the listed fields compared with the text; `0x1D4E2` with `V:4A900` = 0 and `V:4A901` = `0x11` also leaves the runway start position (273963296, 1800, 279184624, heading 2240, the E1 position), the view selector `V:4A83D` at 0, W2 `0x4EF7` and B4 `0x35` from a `0xFFFF` start, and `0x1D8B8` also clears bit 0 of `+0x21`. The carrier start and the mission-level setup (what sets `V:4A900`) are read only.

At the start `0x1D4E2` sets: `W0` bits `0x0600` cleared, `W2` bits `0x2100`, `0x8000`, `0x1000` and `0x0008` cleared and `0x0080` set, B4 `&= 0x3F` with bits 1 and 3 cleared, `B20` bits 0, 1, 2 cleared, `+0x7C` = 0, `V:4A8FF`, `V:4A928` = 0, the mouse pointer `V:4A830` = `V:4A834` = 72 and T = 9, `V:4A858` = 0,
`V:4A857` is cleared at touchdown. The record routine `0x1D8B8` sets `W0` = `0x148 | 0x1080` = `0x11C8`, `+0x7E` = `0x1400`, `+0x63` = `0x0D`, `V:4A890` = 1, `+0x71` = `0xFF`, `V:4A951` = 0, `V:4A952` = 0. `0x1D858` clears `+0x6C`, `+0x6E`, `+0x78`, the three displacement longs, E, Y, Q, `+0x50`, `+0x54`, `+0x65`, sets `+0x2B` = 9 and clears
`W0` bit `0x8000`, `V:4AC08`, `V:4AC0C`. On a runway the position is the start block (y = `0x708`, heading from the block); the carrier start is section 9. **The fuel is refilled here** (corrected, 17.17): `0x1D8B8` calls the refuel and re-arm routine `0x1F14A`, which writes the fuel `0x61A800` and the other values of the landing-complete event (`+0x5F` = `0x24`, `+0x60` = 500, `V:4A8FB` = `V:4A8FC` = 3, `V:4A904` = `V:4A905` = 16, `V:4A943`..`V:4A945` and `V:4A97A` cleared) and clears the first 164 bytes of records 1 to 3; the list of everything the routines write, and what survives, is in 17.17 (rule 4). The restart enters at `0x1D538`, after the lines of `0x1D4E2` that reset the view and the recorder (17.17, rule 11).

**The step counter `n` (`V:4A992`) is not part of the restart.** It is cleared by the mission-start code (`0x12EC0`, followed by a three-tick wait and the flight state `0x12EFE`) and kept across the crash restart: the restart path is the flight state `0x12EFE` (its hand-over branch, which stores `0x138D8`), the crash wait state `0x138D8` (which calls `0x1D538`), the resume states `0x13980` and `0x139C2` and back to `0x12EFE` (17.17), none of which writes `V:4A992` (read from the code), and the logs show it counting through (0x1A0 to 0x1A1 in `stall2.txt`, 419 to 420 in `crash-hold.txt`, one turn per pass through the whole restart in `crash-flow-cockpit.txt`; verified). The other clear, at `0x138B6`, belongs to a state routine at `0x138AC` that waits for the countdown `V:4AB8E` to go negative, clears `n` and enters `0x12EFE`; no reference to `0x138AC` was found in the image (no absolute address, pc-relative load or branch), so it is not on the crash path and may be unused (read only, not run). The game-over path clears `n` in its last state (captured, 17.17).

### 16. Worked examples (rig output of the real code)

Method: the rig convention of section 1 (`rig_run(0x26AF8)` per step, the four patches, key byte written to `+0x65` before each step, `V:4A83D` = 0). Initial state = the **base block** B0 plus the per-case changes. Position, attitude and speeds are decimal; W2 and B4 are hex. "Step 0" is the initial state.

Base block B0: the table of section 1 (its only definition); each case lists its changes to B0. The matrices of a case are built from the case's angles as B0 says.

| case | position (x, y, z) | pitch, heading, roll | other changes to B0 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E1, E4 (runway) | 273963296, 1800, 279184624 | 0, 2240, 0 | `W2` = `0x0080`, B4 = `0x04` |
| E2, E3 (air, gear extending) | 273272584, 409600, 280480032 | 0, 2240, 0 | `+0x6C` = `+0x6E` = 3000, `+0x2B` = 60, `+0x7C` = `0x60` (retract command clear, progress 6: the gear extends over steps 1 to 6) |
| E9 (air, gear up) | 273272584, 409600, 280480032 | 0, 2240, 0 | `+0x6C` = `+0x6E` = 3000, `+0x2B` = 60, `+0x7C` = `0xE0` (retract command set, progress 6: up and stays up) |
| E7 (air, gear down) | 273272584, 409600, 280480032 | 0, 2240, 0 | `+0x6C` = `+0x6E` = 1700, `+0x2B` = 0 |
| E8 (descent) | 273272584, 20000, 280480032 | 2000, 2240, 0 | `+0x6C` = `+0x6E` = 3000, `+0x2B` = 60 |
| E5, E6 (carrier) | see below | 0, 28600, 0 (28600 is the demonstration's heading, 17.14; free flight has 0) | carrier record, see below |

Reading E2 and E3: the start value `+0x7C` = `0x60` has the retract command (bit 7) clear and a progress of 6, which is a gear that is **extending**: the progress falls by 1 per step and reaches 0 (down and locked) at step 6. While the progress is non-zero the gear counts as "not fully down"; from step 7 it counts as down, so the normal-rule speed target falls by a quarter
(about 3346 to 2510 at this altitude and throttle) and S, which had been rising by 5 per step toward the higher target (3042, 3047, ..., 3067), falls by `0x3C` for two steps (3007, 2947) and then by 7 per step (2940, 2933, ..., 2863 at step 20) toward the lower target. The gust target is reduced by a quarter as well while the retract command is clear; it is 0 in level flight. In E3 the same decline is overlaid by the pull-up (the pitch rate and the nose-up pitch enlarge the drag term).

The crash test uses the v_y of the touchdown step itself, which the table cannot show because the step zeroes `+0x42`: run without the floor (the same state with y raised by 400) the step-6 displacement is -4316, v_y = 1079, above `0x240` (576), so the touchdown at step 6 crashes (`W0` bit `0x0200` and the dying bit `0x0400`: `0x11C8` becomes `0x17C8`). The harness does not run the mission state machine, so the aircraft goes on rolling.

#### Carrier set-up for E5 and E6

The carrier is record 14 (offset `0x4CE3C`; corrected from `0x4CC3C`): `+0x00` = `0x404C`, `+0x14` = 286785536, `+0x18` = 0, `+0x1C` = 286785536, `+0x0C` = `+0x0E` = 6144, `+0x10` = 0, `+0x5E` = `0x0E`, `+0x62` = `0x20`, `+0x63` = `0x3D`, `+0x68` = 28600 (the demonstration's heading, 17.14), `+0x7D` = 3, matrices of the angles (0, 28600, 0), and from `+0xA4` the slots 0 to 57: the
vertex k of the extractor's model 8, level 0 (the near level), rotated: component i = `(M[3i] * x + M[3i + 1] * y + M[3i + 2] * z) >> 8` with M = the carrier's matrix B `>> 6`. The player (E5): position (286801436, 30472, 286891436), pitch 0, heading 28600, roll 0, `W2` = `0x0082`, B4 = `0xC8`, `+0x4E` = 112, `+0x0C` and `+0x0E` = `(x & 0x3FFFFF) >> 8` and the same for z,
`+0x10` = `y >> 8`, and at `+0xA4` and `+0xB6` the vertices 0 and 3 of model 4 (level 0) rotated by the player's matrix B in the same way. E6 differs: the player is airborne at (carrier x, 37472, carrier z - 260000), `W2` = `0x8000` (hook down), B4 = 0, S = W = 1700, T = 40, `+0x7C` = 0.
A run built from this specification is identical for 100 steps to a run from a real game state of the demo (a captured memory image of the attract mode).
