step 66; at step 67 the aircraft is airborne (wheels bit lost, B4 = 0, catapult bit gone) and sinks from y = 30472 toward 14600 (the sink term, no pitch). Reading E6: the nose point touches the deck at step 8 (B4 = `0x40`, wheels down), the tail point at step 9; at step 20 the tail point enters the corridor (`W2` = `0xC082`) and
S loses an eighth per step: 1732, 1516, 1327, ..., 12 at step 60 and 0 a few steps later (the rule stops at |S| <= 7).

### 17. Addendum (Task 5 gaps): rules the port had to decide

The Task 5 port was written from this note and compared with the original code in the rig (`tests/rig/test_flight_diff.c`: the worked examples bit for bit, 3000 envelope sequences with 0 divergences, 3000 adversarial sequences with 55 divergences listed there as informational). Where the note was silent, ambiguous or wrong the port marks the place `NOTE-GAP`: 47 tagged lines at HEAD `1817830` (`flight.c` 40, one of them the header comment that defines the tag, `flight_coll.c` 6, `flight.h` 1). The first version of this section settled 39 of them from an earlier count and left four places without a rule (the ejection cycle, the external-view finalisation of a hull hit, the hull hit with an ejection nibble, the pause pre-steps); the fix round settled those four, re-derived the index (17.16, one row per tagged line) from the tags in the code, and corrected what the rig showed wrong (17.15, C19 onwards). Status tags: **[rig]** the rule was run against the original in the Musashi rig, either by the Task 5 test or by a run made for this addendum (a scratch driver, not committed; a count is the number of states compared); **[emu]** observed in the reference emulator; **[read]** read from the listing only. "Was" says what the earlier text said where it was ambiguous or wrong; every wrong statement is also listed in 17.15 and fixed where it stood.

#### 17.1 Order within a pass of the frame routine [read, emu]

In every pass the frame routine runs the current mission state routine (called through the pointer at `0x1A6BC` at `0x11956`, the last act of the routine `0x11748`, which the frame routine `0x11124` calls at `0x11134`; note 04 lists `0x11124` as the frame routine and `0x11748` as the elapsed-seconds accounting, both right: `0x11134` is the call site, not a routine, and the accounting routine also runs the state routine) **before** the entity update (`0x26AF8`, which contains the player step). The state routines (the flight state `0x12EFE` with its hand-over branch, the crash wait state `0x138D8`, the resume states `0x13980` and `0x139C2`; names and the rest of the pass in 17.17) therefore see the state the previous pass left and the player step of the same pass sees what they changed: the hand-over clears `W0` bit `0x0200` at the start of the pass that follows the impact pass, so the step of that pass no longer sees the crash bit (section 11, the table of section 14). A port that runs its flight step and then its state logic in one tick moves every transition one step early. The port's tick is: dispatcher, then world update (the player first, then records 1 to 14), then the flip routine.

#### 17.2 Which parts of the player step run (view and flags) [rig, read]

The note said (section 1, item a) that with neither `W0` bit `0x1000` nor `0x0100` set in the external view "only the bookkeeping runs"; the port read this as "displacement zero" and still ran the attitude step. In the original the whole of steps c to g is skipped. The player step runs these parts, by view (`V:4A83D`) and `W0`:

| view | W0 `0x1000` | W0 `0x0100` | input conversion, warnings | speed step | attitude step | motion step | position update and cell fields |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| any | set | any | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| any | clear | set | yes | no | yes | yes | no |
| cockpit | clear | clear | yes | no | yes | yes | no |
| external | clear | clear | **no** | no | **no** | **no** (D = 0 as a value; the stored `+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46` are **not** cleared, 17.18 b) | **cell fields only** (words `+0x06`, `+0x08`, `+0x0A`, `+0x0C`, `+0x0E`, `+0x10` from the unchanged position; the shortcut enters the cell-refresh code of the position update with D = 0) |

In every row the gear counters (section 8) run, and so do the collision phase and the tail unless `W0` bit `0x0400` is set. **Where the cell refresh comes from.** The cell fields are written by one block of code, the tail of the position update (item h of section 1); the last row reaches it by a shortcut that sets D = 0 and skips everything before it, so "cell fields only" is that block run without a position update, and item h's "cell bookkeeping only with `0x1000`" is the rule of the normal path. Rows 2 and 3 skip the block altogether: position and cell fields stay as they were. **Which previous position the collision phase uses.** Always the position minus D, whatever the position update did. Row 1: the position has advanced by D, so this is the true previous position. Rows 2 and 3: the motion step has produced a real D but the position has not advanced, so position minus D is a point one displacement *behind* the record, not the previous position (which equals the position), and a hull hit then undoes the displacement from the unmoved position as well: the aircraft ends a further D behind (rig: `W0` `0x01C8` and `0x00C8` in the cockpit view, hull hit at speed 1100: x changes by -48, at speed 500 by -24, the D of that step). Last row: D = 0, previous position = position, the undo moves nothing. Rows 2 and 3 do not occur for the player in play (its `W0` carries `0x1000` until the finalisation, after which the collision phase is skipped by `0x0400`); they are reachable only from a synthetic state. Run in the rig with `W0` = `0x00C8`, `0x01C8`, `0x10C8`, `0x11C8` in both views and the key DOWN held: the pitch command `+0x28`, the speed and the cell words follow the table. This is the cause of the two W2 divergences of the adversarial family (open divergence 2 of the Task 5 report): the nose-settle bit `0x0040` is set only by the attitude step, which the original does not run in the last row.

**Pause (`V:4A866` non-zero)** [rig, read]. Section 1's "returns at once" is true of the player step `0x2A2A2` only. The world update `0x26AF8` still does these things in a paused pass: it clears `W2` bit `0x0001` of records 0 to 14; it runs the AI tick of item 2 (every 16th frame) and the stale-lock drop `0x26F28` (neither is gated by the pause); it clears the player's `W0` bit `0x0002`; it runs the turn-rate cue `0x270A0` (from `+0x56` and `V:4A9FE` as they stand: sound 8 once, latch `V:4A940`) and the gear-down cue `0x2835A` (`V:4A8FE` = 1 and sound 4 once while the gear is down, `+0x7C & 0x70` = 0, and S is `0x1200` or more, else `V:4A8FE` = 0). It does **not** run: the countdown of the effect timers, the damage cool-downs `V:4A907` and `V:4A906`, the `+0x4C` decrement (so the counter and the lock of 17.4 do not advance), `0x27B1E`, the player step and the camera trail. Rig: one paused step from `W0` `0x11CA`, `W2` `0x0001`, S `0x1300`, `+0x4C` 50, an effect timer 30 and both cool-downs 5: the whole variable block differs only in `W0` (`0x11C8`), `W2` (0), `V:4A8FE` (1) and the last-entity words `V:4AA6C`/`E`. The port's pause branch does exactly this.

#### 17.3 Dying expiry (the external-view dying path) [rig, read]

This is open divergence 1 of the Task 5 report (53 of the 55 adversarial divergences). There is no effect record involved: the rule is in the entry of the entity step (`0x2A2A2`, `0x2A2E8`-`0x2A360`) and applies to the player **only in the external view** (`V:4A83D` non-zero; with the cockpit view the entry goes straight on and a dying player stays dying, which is why the cockpit touchdown crash of E8 stays `0x17C8` for as long as the run goes on).

With `W0` bit `0x0400` (dying) set, after the world update has decreased `+0x4C`:

- while `+0x4C` is greater than 0: `W0` bit `0x2000` is cleared and the step goes on as usual (no collision phase, section 1 item j);
- when `+0x4C` is 0 or less (it reaches 0 on the 15th step after the finalisation set it to 15): `W0` bit `0x0400` is cleared; a statistics counter `V:4A963` is incremented when the class is an aircraft (high nibble 1) and both `W0` bit `0x0008` and `W2` bit `0x0080` are clear (a class `0x15` or a class with high nibble 0 skips this); `W0` bit `0x0040` (valid) and bit `0x4000` (radar) are cleared, `W0` bit `0x2000` is cleared, `+0x4C` is set to 20; the rest of the step is skipped (no input, speed, attitude, motion or collision; only the camera trail runs). `W0` `0x15C8` becomes `0x1188` in one step; `+0x4C` then counts down from 20 and nothing further happens to the record. The `0x15C8` is the test word of the rig run; in play, **after a collision result** (the result handler sets `0x8000`, 17.12; the external finalisation has cleared `0x1000`, the hand-over `0x0200`) the word at the expiry is `0x85C8`, which becomes `0x8188` (captured: `0x87C8` at the finalisation, `0x85C8` after the hand-over, `0x8188` after the expiry, 17.17). An external hard touchdown keeps `0x1000` (the finalisation does not clear it for that result): `0x17C8` becomes `0x15C8` at the hand-over and `0x1188` at the expiry. An ejection hull hit keeps `0x0200`: `0x07C8` becomes `0x0388` [rig].

Task 6 needs two consequences. The external-view branch of the crash wait state (`0x138D8`, section 11 and 17.17) waits for `W0` bit `0x0400` of the player to be clear instead of waiting for `V:4A951` [read; verified on the emulator, 17.17], so the restart begins in the state routine of the pass **after** the expiry step: finalisation in step N (`+0x4C` = 15), expiry in step N+15, restart (`0x1D538`) in the pass N+16. The cockpit view has no expiry; its timing is the 40-iteration hold of section 14. A switch to the cockpit view while the external wait is running restarts at the next pass (17.17, rule 1). Verified in the rig for the counter values 15 down to 0 (22 steps from `W0` = `0x15C8`, both views), and for the statistic with `W0` `0x15C0` and `0x15C8`, `W2` 0 and `0x0080`.

#### 17.4 Inputs (section 2)

- **Both keys of a pair** [rig]. The conversion tests "only the positive key" against "anything else with the pair non-zero": stick forward `0x10` is positive, back `0x20` negative; rudder right `0x40` positive, left `0x80` negative; roll right `0x04` positive, left `0x08` negative. With both bits of a pair held the **negative key wins** and the command ramps negative (stick back, rudder left, roll left), with the same jump to -1 (-3 for roll) on a sign flip. Was: silent.
- **Both throttle bits** (key byte low bits = 3) [rig]. The throttle does not change (neither up nor down branch runs), but everything before the branches does: the repeat counter advances (R + 1, held at 8) and the centre notice `0xFE` is raised as for a single key (T = 0, step size above 0, player viewed). Was: silent.
- **Preset** [rig, 11 cases]. P is the preset byte (a negative preset counts as 0, the stored byte is not changed), T the throttle byte. The sign comes from the signed comparison of P with T: equal, P above T, P below T. The two thresholds then use the difference as a **wrapped signed byte**: D = P - T computed in 8 bits. P above T: key bits "up" when D (wrapped) is above 8 or R is below 2, else the bits are cleared. P below T: "down" when D (wrapped) is below -8 or R is below 2, else cleared. P = T: as section 2. Example: P = 121, T = -28: P is above T, the wrapped D is -107, which is not above 8, so the bits are set to "up" for R below 2 and cleared otherwise. Was: "D computed in 8 bits, this only matters for T below -7" without saying that the sign and the threshold come from different sources.
- **Ejection nibble** (`+0x7C` low nibble non-zero) [rig]. The whole conversion routine (`0x1DFAA`) returns at once: the commands, the preset handling and the recorder call (`0x2A1A6`) are all skipped; the key byte is left as it is. Was: "the command bytes are left as they are" (the preset handling was not mentioned).
- **Control lock** (B4 bit `0x10`) [rig]. The test follows the preset handling. With `+0x4C` zero or positive (the value after the world update's decrement) all three commands are set to 0 and the conversion ends; the key byte is untouched, so the throttle keys still act. When `+0x4C` is negative the bit is cleared and the conversion goes on in the same step (with `+0x4C` = 0 the lock still holds; at -1 it is dropped and a held key already moves the command).
- **Pointer throttle: the original's rules** [rig, about 45 cases in two runs; the mouse handler and the throttle code read]. State: `V:4A830` (the pointer height P), `V:4A834` (the reference Q, the value the throttle last agreed with), `V:4A890` (non-zero = pointer throttle on, set to 1 at every start), `V:4A95E` (display mode); at a start P = Q = 72 (T = 9, times 8). Three rules and their consequences:
  0. *Mouse to P* (`0x192DE`; the limits are stored by the call of `0x19254` at `0x1167E` with 960, 960, -960, -960) [rig: the handler run with the counter register injected; read]. Once per vertical blank (50 Hz; the handler is installed at `0x195BE` and also counts `V:4A82C`), dy is the change of the mouse's vertical counter since the previous blank, wrapped into -128..127 (a difference below -128 gets 256 added, above 127 gets 256 subtracted), and **P = clamp(P - dy, -960, 960)**. Pushing the mouse away from the user lowers the hardware counter (the machine's convention, [read]), so it raises P. With `V:4A890` = 0 a negative dy would be halved first (arithmetic shift); never in play. The horizontal counter accumulates in `V:4A82E` (also limited to ±960), which no flight rule reads. A step lasts 6 to 7 blanks, so P moves by the sum of the dy of those blanks. Rig samples (P = 0 before): dy +5 gives -5, -5 gives +5, +127 gives -127, -128 gives +128, a wrapped 200 gives +56; from 900 with dy -100 the result is 960, from -900 with +100 it is -960.
  1. *Follow, only in a step in which a throttle key was held* (the low two bits of the key byte `+0x65` **at entry** to the throttle routine `0x15ED4`, that is as the input conversion left them, which includes the bits the preset handling set; the routine's own clearing of the bits in the military-power detent comes after the test and does not undo the follow; corrected, 17.18 e): with U = max(T, 0) times 8, if P is 960 or more, or |P - Q| is **below 16** (15 or less), then Q = U and, when P is not negative, P = U. A key therefore drags the pointer along unless the user has moved it by 16 or more. Not gated by the display mode.
  2. *Fire, every step, only when the display mode `V:4A95E` is not 2* (mode 2 turns off this rule only): if P is 960 or more, or |P - Q| is **17 or more** (above 16; a difference of exactly 16 does neither 1 nor 2): if P is negative, P = 0 and the preset is set to -1 (idle); otherwise, with the afterburner on, the preset is P >> 3 only when P is below 864 (`0x360`; at 864 or more nothing changes), with the afterburner off nothing happens when P = 0 (the preset is not touched), else the notice `0xFE` is raised when T = 0 and the player is viewed and the preset is P >> 3 (120 at the top stop). Then Q = P. Rig, Q = 500, T = 9, key bits 0: P - Q = 14, 15, 16 fires nothing (P and Q keep their values), 17 and 18 set preset 64 and Q = P; the same on the other side (-14 to -16 nothing, -17 and -18 preset 60). With a key held, 15 follows, 16 does nothing, 17 fires. The earlier rig remark "+16 does not fire, -40 does" is consistent: the first firing difference is **17**.
  3. *Consequences.* (a) The preset goes through the preset handling of section 2 like an F-key, in the next step. (b) **The pointer held at the top stop does not stay there.** P = 960 fires in the step it is reached (preset 120); in the next step the preset drives the throttle keys, the follow rule sees a key and sets P = Q = 8 T, so the pointer leaves the stop after one step and then tracks 8 T while the throttle climbs (rig: T = 9, P = Q = 960, no key: step 1 preset 120, P = 960; step 2 P = Q = 80, T = 10; step 3 88, 11; step 4 104, 13). It stays at 960 only with the military-power detent set (`W2` `0x2000`) at T = 120 and the afterburner off (the preset 120 is re-made every step and the detent changes nothing), and with the afterburner on nothing happens at all. The earlier sentence "fires every step" held only in the detent case. (c) A pointer jump to 700 (preset 87) is followed by the same drag: P = Q = 8 T from the next step on, the throttle rising toward 87. (d) With the afterburner on, a height of 864 or more sets no preset (the afterburner stays on); below 864 the preset is P >> 3, at most 107, which leaves the afterburner through the preset handling (rig: P = 860, Q = 900 gives 107; P = 870 gives nothing, Q = P). (e) Display mode 2 with a key held: the follow still runs (rig).
  The F-key handler (`0x1EA64`) sets the preset to 12 n (121 for F10) and P = Q = min(8 × preset, 960); the brake key (`0x1E32E`) sets P = Q = 0 and clears the preset. Was: "the pointer height (0..960, limits set by `0x19254`)", wrong: the limits are ±960 and a negative height is the idle request; and "fires without any movement only at the top stop", see (b).
- **Port input** [ruling, not an original rule]. The port's absolute throttle axis keeps P and Q in the flight state, writes P = round(960 × axis) (clamped to -960..960; a bipolar axis below zero gives idle) when the axis value changes, and applies rules 1 to 3 above unchanged. The faithful input is relative mouse deltas accumulated into P as in rule 0, summed over the blanks of a port step; the axis mapping is a substitute for it. The original's P runs -960 to 960 with P below 0 the idle request, so a lever from 0 to 960 cannot send idle: map its bottom stop to -960 (17.17, rule 12).

#### 17.5 Throttle, afterburner and fuel (section 3)

- **Fuel zero** [rig]. The test is the fuel long equal to 0 at the top of `0x15ED4`, before the key logic: T = 0, the afterburner is cleared (`W2` bit `0x0008`), the pointer P and Q are reset to 0 (player, pointer on); the key logic including the repeat counter, the notice `0xFE` and the pointer rules 1 and 2 are skipped; the speed rules take the idle branch. With T = 50, R = 5 and the key `=` held: T becomes 0 and R stays 5.
- **Afterburner-off notice** [read]. Down-key with the afterburner on: sound `V:4A8F6` = 2, `W2` bit `0x0008` cleared, T - 1, and for the viewed player `V:4A968` = `0xFC`; `V:4A84F` is **not** touched (the afterburner-on branch sets `V:4A84F` = 6 together with `V:4A968` = `0xFD`).
- **Fuel warning flags** (`0x1557C`, player only, with `V:4A984` bit 6) [rig, 19 cases]. The fuel after this step's burn names exactly one flag X: `0x40` when the fuel is 0 or less, `0x02` when below `0x70800`, `0x01` when below `0xBB800`, none otherwise. When X is already set in the low byte of `V:4AC08` nothing changes (so `0x03` stays `0x03` at a critical fuel level). Otherwise the bits `0x43` are **replaced** by X (the other two are cleared); with no X they are cleared. For X = `0x40` the side effects (fuel set to 0, `W2` bit `0x0008` cleared, sound `V:4A8F6` = 2) are made **only in the step in which the flag is newly set**; on later steps the afterburner is cut by the speed step's own fuel-zero branch (above) and the sound is not requested again. Was: "above that the low-fuel flags are cleared (a flag already shown is not set again)", which left the replacement and the once-only side effects unsaid.

#### 17.6 Speed rules (section 4)

- **Normal rules, air, C >= 0** [read; the Task 5 test compares the branch]. The clamp after the rise (S set to Vt when it exceeds Vt) is reached from both rise branches, so it also applies after the negative-S rise (S below 0: S + `0x78`, then the clamp). Was: the order of "if S is negative S rises by `0x78`; else ...; if S now exceeds Vt" left this open.

#### 17.7 Control surfaces (section 5)

- **decay(0)** [rig]. decay(x, k) for x from -15 to -1 adds 1; for x from 0 to 15 subtracts 1, so **0 becomes -1**. It matters because two callers do not test for zero: the rudder with B4 bit `0x02` (water) or W = 0 (decay of Y), and the roll with water or W = 0 (decay of Y and of Q). Was: "otherwise x moves one unit toward 0".
- **Roll on the ground** (qc non-zero, B4 water clear, W non-zero, after the yaw-rate call) [read]. Roll-rate(-target) is called when (the roll is below `0x50` or above `0x7030`) **and** W is above `0x360`; if the roll is within `0x50..0x7030` or W is `0x360` or less the next rule decides (roll below `0x3840`: roll-rate when qc >= 0, else Q = 0; from `0x3840`: roll-rate when qc <= 0, else Q = 0). The comparisons are signed.
- **Where `0x1557C` runs** [rig, read]. It is called only from the attitude step (`0x31AE0`, class nibble 1) and only when `V:4A984` bit 6 is set, so its "bit 6 clear" branch (clearing `V:4AC08` bits `0x43`) is unreachable: without bit 6 the flags are untouched. The attitude step has a branch for the pause (`V:4A866` set: straight to the composition with zero rates, `0x1557C` not called), but it is **dead code** [read]: the step's only caller (`0x2A4DA`, in the player step) lies behind the pause return of `0x2A2A2`, so `V:4A866` is never set when the attitude step runs. The live freeze is `V:4A867` (rates 0, `0x1557C` still called, 17.8). Was: "otherwise it only clears the warning flags".
- **Stall lamp** [read]. The block at the end of `0x1557C` (stall flag `B20` bit `0x04` with the wheels up sets `V:4AC0B` bit `0x80` and, the first time, `V:4AC0F` bit `0x20`; `V:4A9A8` is raised to 2 when negative) runs only for the player with `V:4A984` bit 6 and fuel not 0; in every other case it clears `V:4AC0B` bit `0x80`. So with fuel 0 the stall flag is cleared.
- **Gimbal kick** [read]. The kick is the word at `0x425E4` (entry 6 of the player response table, 144), added to E (first time) or Y (second time) in the direction of the rate's own sign, **zero counting as positive**.

#### 17.8 Attitude (section 6)

The step 0x31AE0 for the player, in the order of the code (the port's order must be this, the note's list was numbered correctly but did not say which items exclude each other):

| order | part | applies when | effect on the stored angles |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | rates | always | E, Y, Q from `0x1557C`; all three 0 when `V:4A867` is non-zero (`0x1557C` still ran, so its state words and flags are updated; `V:4A866` cannot be set here, 17.7) |
| 2 | bank-turn coupling | `V:4A847` set | adds to E and Y; see below |
| 3 | composition | always | new pitch, heading, roll from matrix A and the rates |
| 4 | roll recentre | `W2` bit `0x0010` (cleared) | replaces the composed roll |
| 5 | pitch override | state byte `+0x05` = `0x0A`, else `W2` bit `0x0040` | pitch from the **stored** pitch; heading and roll as composed; then store, nothing else |
| 6 | stall block | `V:4A83C` set and no override | when it nose-drops: pitch from the stored pitch, heading and roll as composed; then store, no gimbal guard |
| 7 | gimbal guard | no override and no nose drop | store, or abandon the step |
| 8 | store | | `W2` bit `0x0004` cleared (except after item 7 abandoned) and matrices rebuilt |

- **Coupling** [rig: the 7900-state comparison of the attitude step and the Task 5 test, and 5 cases here]. With `V:4A867` set (`V:4A866` cannot occur here, 17.7) the routine is not called: `+0x22`, `+0x24`, `+0x50`, `+0x52`, `+0x54` and `V:4A9FE` keep their values. With `W0` bit `0x8000` set it is called but jumps to the end with zero contributions and sets `V:4A9FE` = 0 (the state words are not updated). With the pitch fold above 3600 tenths the state words are updated first and then the contributions are 0 and `V:4A9FE` = 0. Was: "only with `V:4A847` set and `W0` bit `0x8000` clear".
- **The "wrong side" rule** (`+0x52`) [rig via Task 5, 7900 states]. With q the new `+0x54` and c the (sector-corrected) cosine word: if (q < 0 and c < 0) or (q >= 0 and c > 0) then `+0x52` = `+0x50`. Otherwise, if (q < 0 and c >= 256) or (q >= 0 and c <= -256), then **when `+0x50` and `+0x52` are equal** both sine and cosine words are negated (this is the whole of the "negation"); in all other cases nothing happens. Was: "negates (s, c) when c is outside +-256 on the wrong side and `+0x50`, `+0x52` agree in sign (read from the code, the case is rare)".
- **Override** [rig, Task 5 test]. The drift starts from the stored pitch: stored pitch at most `0x3840`: pitch minus the amount, kept when positive, else 0 (a zero or negative stored pitch gives 0); above `0x3840`: plus the amount, 0 when `0x7080` or more. The amount is `0x50` for state `0x0A` and `0xA0` for `W2` bit `0x0040`; **state `0x0A` wins when both hold**. `W2` bit `0x0040` is cleared at the start of `0x1557C` and set again only by the ground nose settle of the same step, so it is a "pitch on the ground below 5 degrees" marker. While an override applies the stall block is skipped completely: `+0x26` and `B20` bit `0x04` keep their values and no nose drop happens.
- **Stall nose drop** [rig, E7 and 7900 states]. Th is computed from the stored pitch. The nose drop zeroes E, sets `+0x26` to `+0x26 + ((0x50 - +0x26) >> 2)` (arithmetic shift), and the new pitch is the **stored** pitch plus the new `+0x26`; the composed pitch is discarded (worked example E7: pitch 101 after 6 steps). The result is 0 at `0x7080` or more, kept from `0x3840` up, kept at `0x12C0` or less (**negative values included**), and reduced by `0x50` between. A nose drop is also an override for the gimbal guard: the angles are stored without the guard (a stalled steep climb is stored). `B20` bit `0x02` (out of control) drops the nose at once, with the wheels down as well.

#### 17.9 Angle words outside 0..28799, negative pitch (open divergence 3) [rig]

**Convention.** All angle words are 16-bit. Every comparison and fold the step makes on them is signed, and every shift is arithmetic, with one exception: the two matrix builders (`0x32B52` for matrix A, `0x32BEC` for matrix B) shift the word right by 3 **logically**, as an unsigned 16-bit number. The normal range is 0..28799 (heading, roll) and, for the pitch, 0..28799 with the nose-up half above `0x3840`. The only source of an out-of-range word in play is the stall nose drop with a negative `+0x26`: after a catapult or ground roll the ground term can be as low as about -77 (the relaxation target is `-(((Vt - S) >> 8) + 0x42)`), a lift-off below the stall speed with pitch 0 stores -8 (g = -37, positive from the next step) or -38 (g = -77, then -47, -34, -5 on the following steps). The pitch word can therefore be about -1 to -50 for a few steps. Heading and roll words computed from a junk matrix leave the range as well (95 of 7200 steps of the 600 scenarios below, for example heading -21088 or roll -16488), and the builders then treat them in the same way.

**Decision: the junk is defined by the image, it is reproducible and the port must reproduce it.** The original never guards a table index; every read is an address computed from the word and every address is inside the loaded image, whose bytes the extractor can export. What each consumer does with a pitch word w below 0 (verified as stated):

| consumer | what it does with a negative word | reads |
| --- | --- | --- |
| drag term G (`0x16A32`) | the fold (from `0x3840` up subtract `0x7080`) leaves it; P = w >> 1 arithmetic: -3 gives -2 | none |
| gust (`0x2AB64`) | p = w >> 3 arithmetic; for p up to 1800 the index is 900 - p, so p = -1 reads the sine-table word 901, past the end of the table, and p = -4096 reads word 4996; the product (c times k) >> 3 is truncated to a 16-bit word | words at `0x436E8 + 2 i`, i from 901 to 4996 (to `0x45DF0`) |
| stall threshold | p2 = w >> 1 arithmetic is below `0x1C20`, so the branch with `min(p2, 0xE10) + 0x3840` is used | none |
| ground nose settle | pitch at most 0: nothing happens (negative counts as level) | none |
| ground pitch-rate test | "pitch non-zero and not above `0x6D60`" is true for negative words | none |
| override | a negative stored pitch drifts to 0 | none |
| coupling pitch scale (`0x32426`) | the index is the pitch >> 8 arithmetic times 2, so the scale is a word **before** the table `0x3259A`: -1 gives the image word `0x4E75` (20085, at `0x32598`, the last word before the table), -2 gives 84 (at `0x32596`), -3 gives `0x4469`, -4 gives 80; the contribution is about 78 times larger than at pitch 0 | image words at `0x3249A..0x32598` |
| matrix builders A and B | w >> 3 logical (-3 gives 8191, which is no angle); the four-way fold on the doubled index (the `<` tests are signed 16-bit) then reads the sine table at a byte offset down to -9182 or up to +10982 from `0x436E8` (first byte read `0x4130A`, last byte read `0x461CF`; exhaustive trace below); B uses `0x7080 - w` (-1 to -7 give exactly 3600 tenths, sine 0, cosine 16384; -8 and below read word -1 and word 901) | image bytes `0x4130A..0x461D0` |
| composition (`0x325B8`) after a junk matrix | the pitch path clamps its table index (`0x225`, `0xF8`); the heading and roll paths use a 16-bit quotient q and the byte offset is the signed 16-bit value of twice the magnitude of q (17.18 a; corrected: the earlier text said the absolute value is taken first and the reads lie in `0x42E92..0x4AE92`), so they read from `0x3AE92` (|q| from 16384, negative offsets) to `0x4AE91` | image bytes `0x3AE92..0x49A12`; the bytes from `0x49A12` to `0x4A6E8` are zero fill in the image and are written at run time (display data), and `0x4A6E8..0x4AE92` is the start of the live variable block (hunk 71): **neither is reproducible** by an image copy. Reads in one run of 600 random scenarios of 12 steps (7200 steps; pitch -1 to -60, any heading, roll 0 or any, speeds 200 to 1400, ground term -77 to 0; scratch driver, seed 99), counted for the table reads of the composition only: `0x461D0..0x49A12` in 53 steps of 49 scenarios, `0x49A12..0x4A6E8` in 12 steps of 12 scenarios (0.17 %), `0x4A6E8..0x4AE92` in 2 steps of 2 scenarios (the live variable words `0x4A8B0` and `0x4A8BC`; the routine's own scratch words `0x4AC48..0x4AC7F` in the same range are legitimate accesses and not counted). Other seeds of the same generator gave 40 scenarios and 7 steps for the first two ranges: the counts are not a constant, only the order of magnitude is |

Verified: the two builders against the rig for all 65536 pitch words (heading and roll 0) and 20000 random triples each, **0 mismatches** with a model that does exactly the table reads above; the gust for 1725 (pitch, speed, gust) cases including the whole negative range, 0 mismatches; the coupling scale for pitch 0, -3 and -300 (V:4A9FE -18, -1413, -6 at roll 3000); a perturbation test (the image bytes outside `0x4130A..0x461D0` replaced by random bytes, re-run with these exact bounds; the first run used `0x41300`) leaves a realistic run (pitch -5, 40 steps) unchanged, and replacing bytes inside changes it at step 0; an exhaustive read trace of both builders over all 65536 words in each of the three angle positions gives the first byte read `0x4130A` and the last `0x461CF`, so the span row, this section and note 01b now agree on `0x4130A`.

**What the port must do.** Keep the angle words as 16-bit values; implement the two matrix builders with the logical shift and without any range guard; implement the gust index, the coupling scale index and the composition table reads as address arithmetic on a byte copy of the image. The extractor exports two spans (the port reads them only for these lookups): `0x3249A..0x325B8` (the words before the scale table, 286 bytes) and `0x3AE92..0x49A12` (60,288 bytes; corrected start, 17.18 a: the composition's lookups read below the inverse-sine table; the image words in it are what the original read, except 337 relocated address longs (the loader rewrote them to RAM addresses on the reference machine: 145 in `0x3AE92..0x4130A`, in the code of hunks 43 to 57, 12 in `0x415B8..0x41E1B`, 180 more between `0x4767C` and `0x499D5`, in the data structures of hunks 67 to 70, none of them in a table the flight step uses as a value) and five run-time text bytes (`0x440C9..0x440CC`, `0x44EA1`); the rule for those reads follows). **The guards become exact reads.** The gust index 901 to 4996 (words `0x43DF2..0x45DF0`: index 901 is the word 0, then 77, 78, 79, 80, 81, 1060, ...), the sine and inverse-sine index beyond the table and the composition's index reach all lie inside the second span; the coupling scale index -1, -2, ... (words `0x32598`, `0x32596`, ...) lies inside the first. The port's `FL_GUARD_TRIG` and `FL_GUARD_PITCH_SCALE` therefore need no value of their own: they read the spans. The third guard (the terrain grid) needs no span at all (17.10: the Task 5 premise, a word before the grid, was wrong). **Ruling for what no span can supply:** a read in `0x49A12..0x4AE92` (display data written at run time, and the live variable block of hunk 71) is answered 0 and counted; the original's value there is a run-time word, and the port answers such a read from its own variable when it keeps the variable at that address, and with 0 otherwise, and counts it. None of the words that occurred is a flight variable (`0x4A8B0` belongs to the counters of the event-echo queue, `0x1EF7E` and `0x37856`; no instruction found names `0x4A8BC`), so in practice the answer is 0. Reads in `0x49A12..0x4AE92` are 12 + 2 of 7200 junk steps in the run above (0.2 %), and junk steps are a few per lift-off at most. The test that ends a sequence at "an angle word left 0..28799" can then continue and compare the matrices.

**Reads that land on a relocated long or on the text bytes (the span rule)** [rig; emu image]. Counted over the same 600 junk scenarios of 12 steps (7200 steps, seed 99; scratch driver, all reads of the code `0x31AE0..0x33100`, the builders and the composition): the reads that touch a relocated long occur in 6 steps of 6 scenarios (0.08 %): the builders in 2 (the longs at `0x41D76` and `0x41DEE`, with pitch words of -2 and -51 at the step start) and the composition's heading and roll lookups in 4 (longs near `0x47956`, `0x47DFC`, `0x47ED0` and `0x48F9E`, with pitch words of -49 to -55). None of the 94 steps that start with a junk heading or roll word (outside 0..28799, the 95 above counted at the step end; the same builders read them) reads one: all 6 steps start with a heading and roll inside the range, the junk comes from the negative pitch word and from the junk matrix. The five text bytes are read **0 times** in the 7200 steps. The builders' span (`0x4130A..0x461CF`) does cover them, so a pitch word far outside what the dynamics produce would read them; the gust index of these scenarios (901 to about 960, as the pitch words are -1 to -60) stays below `0x43E70` and would need a pitch word below about -360 to reach `0x440C9`. Ruling, in the style of the landmark z (section 11b): **a read in a relocated long returns the reference-machine value**, the same value the original read on its machine: the stored flat address F is replaced by the RAM base of the **target hunk H of the relocation entry** plus (F minus the flat start of H) (not the hunk inferred from F itself: the two differ for a pointer to the end of a hunk), with the segment table of the loader (`image-static.txt`, one RAM base per hunk; the high word is `0x00C3` or `0x00C4` for the hunks involved, as in the landmark case). The extractor applies that to all 337 longs of the corrected span 2 (the 192 of `0x4130A..0x49A12` and 145 in `0x3AE92..0x4130A`, 17.18 a) and to the three longs of span 1 (code operands at `0x32540`, `0x32560` and `0x32568`, the word indices -45/-44, -29/-28 and -25/-24 of the scale table `0x3259A`; the lowest-reaching index decides: index = pitch word >> 8 arithmetic, so the long at `0x32568..0x3256B` is reached at index -24 or lower, a pitch word below -5888 (-5889 and down), its first word at index -25, a pitch word below -6144, the other two longs below -6912 and -11008, their second words at indices -28 and -44; the first words, -29 and -45, would give -7168 and -11264; the dynamics cannot produce any of these; the first version of this rule said index -16 and -3840, which is wrong); every other byte of the spans is the image byte. The five text bytes keep **answer 0, counted**: the port returns 0 for them and increments the junk-read counter, no read occurs in the scenarios above, and the value on the reference machine (a text buffer written by the game) is not a flight variable. A scenario that does read a relocated long therefore sees the same word as the original; the values feed the heading or roll word of the junk step only, so the exact word matters for the compare but not for play (junk steps are a few per lift-off). The check that these 195 longs are fully explained by the relocation rule: all 581 differing bytes of the span between the file image and the emulator's loaded image are covered by the longs and the five text bytes, 0 left over.

**The guard with angle words outside 0..28799** [rig; code `0x31F80..0x32026`, resolves adversarial sequence 1594]. Every difference in the guard has the same form: a 16-bit subtraction, then a branch on the **true signed comparison** of the two operands (not on the sign of the 16-bit result), then a 16-bit negation when the first operand is the smaller. So the "absolute value" of two signed words a and b is: the 16-bit result of a - b when a >= b, the 16-bit result of b - a otherwise, **read back as a signed word**. For words within 0..28799 this is the ordinary |a - b|. When the true difference is 32768 or more (only possible with a junk word: a stored heading or roll of `0x8000` or more, that is a negative signed value, against a new angle in range) the result wraps to a **negative** number (true difference 32928 gives -32608, 65535 gives -1, exactly 32768 gives -32768), and it is **not** negated again. The later tests are signed compares, so a negative difference counts as "not above `0x4B0`": it is not flipped, in either dh or dr. The same holds for the last difference |dr - dh| (after the flips): when dh is negative and dr positive the true difference can reach 32768 or more, wraps to a negative number and passes the "below `0x320`" test, so the angles are stored. Rule, with the signed words oh, or (old), nh, nr (new), op (old pitch):

| step | rule |
| --- | --- |
| guard active | `0x1770` < op < `0x5910`, signed (op of `0x8000` or more: no guard, store) |
| dh, dr | D(nh, oh) and D(nr, or), where D(a, b) = the signed 16-bit value of the true absolute difference of a and b taken modulo 65536 (so D is that difference when it is below 32768, and the difference minus 65536 when it is 32768 or more) |
| flip | when `0x4B0` < d < `0x6BD0` (signed): the flag is set and d becomes D(d, `0x3840`) (no wrap possible, d is in range) |
| roll not flipped | store unless the heading flipped (a negative dr or dh is never flipped) |
| roll flipped | store when D(dr, dh) < `0x320` (signed: a negative D, from a wrapped dh or dr, stores) |
| abandon | unchanged: no angle stored, `W2` bit `0x0004` set (E gains `0x90` by its own sign, only when `+0x28` is non-zero) or, if it was set, Y gains `0x90` |

The port's earlier rule (the absolute value of the 16-bit result, so |-32608| = 32608) differs exactly when the true difference is 32768 or more; a wrapped difference of `0x8000` exactly (-32768) is its own negation in the original. The worked case, sequence 1594 step 6: old heading `0xCF18` (-12520), new `0x4FB8` (20408): the true difference 32928 wraps to -32608, no flip; old roll 26040, new 18048: dr 7992, flipped to 6408; D(6408, -32608) = true 39016 wraps to -26520 (`0x9868`), below `0x320` as a signed number: **stored** (the rig, which also clears `W2` bit `0x0004` set by steps 3 to 5, where the new heading 15992, 17336 and 18528 give true differences 28512, 29856 and 31048, no wrap and the guard abandons). The hypothesis "the guard uses the magnitude of the signed old word" is not the rule (it fits sequence 1594 by coincidence of the numbers); the rule is the wrap. The same wrap applies to the roll and to the new words, and nothing in the guard reduces modulo 28800. Port: compute D as above in `int16_t` after a signed comparison of the two operands; the pitch test is a signed compare of the stored pitch word.

#### 17.10 Motion and gear (sections 7 and 8)

- **Neighbour index `+0x0B`** [rig, 400 positions]. When the sub-cell byte `+0x0A` (4 × (3 - (fine z >> 12)) + (3 - (fine x >> 12)) of the 14-bit fine words, checked in the same run) changes, `V:4A910` is set to `0xFF` and, when the new cell words are both within 2 of the viewed cell (`V:4AA04`, `V:4AA06`; signed word differences, absolute value at most 2), `+0x0B` is rewritten. The rewritten value is **always `0x0F`**: the original reduces the 2-bit quantity (fine >> 12) by a further shift of 2 before it enters the formula, which leaves 0, so both terms are 3. (The runway start writes the direction index n into `+0x0B`; that value stays until the first rewrite; the carrier start writes a value derived from the cell, 17.18 f.) Was: "the neighbour index `+0x0B` is rewritten".
- **Gear loss over water** [rig]. The terrain query runs in a step only when B4 bits `0xC0` are both clear and (the wheels are up or `+0x4C & 7` is 0 after the decrement); the gear is lost (`+0x7C` bit 7 set when the retract progress is 0) and the skid `B20` bit `0x01` is set only in such a step. In the other steps the old B4 water bit does nothing.
- **Hard touchdown** [rig, E8 with `V:4A952` 0 and 1]. The touchdown block sets `W2` bit `0x0080`, `+0x26` = 0, `V:4A857` = 0 and the sound `V:4A878` = 1 and then compares the v_y of the step with `0x240`: at or above it the step either crashes (`V:4A952` = 0: `W0` bit `0x0200`, the statistics counter, `V:4A87D` = 1, `V:4A850` = 4) or, with `V:4A952` non-zero, does nothing more; below it S = W and the notice `0xFE` follow (when W is not 0). S = W is made **only** for the soft touchdown. Was: "Otherwise, with W != 0: S = W", which the port could read as including the immune hard case.
- **Gear sound** [rig]. `V:4A8FD` = 3 is requested on **every** change of the retract progress (bits 4-6 of `+0x7C`), rising or falling; the byte is stored with the low nibble cleared in that step (the low nibble's own processing, including the clearing of `W2` bit `0x0020`, runs first: 17.18 c). Was: "(sound `V:4A8FD` = 3)" on the rising branch only.
- **The terrain grid index for a negative x** (guard 3 of the Task 5 report) [rig, 3000 positions; read]. The Task 5 premise was wrong: the original does not read the word in front of the grid. It forms the column as the **unsigned byte** `(x >> 24) & 0xFF` and the row as `(z >> 24) & 0xFF`, and reads the grid word at byte offset `2 * column + 64 * row` from `0x47F40`, computed in 16-bit words. For x >= 0 (at most `0x1FFFFFFF`) the column is 0 to 31 as before. A negative x gives columns 128 to 255 (x down to -2^31), so with row 0 the word index (column + 32 * row) is 128 to 255, that is **cells inside the grid** (rows 4 to 7, a column c < 0 reading cell number 256 + c: c = -1 reads cell 255, row 7 column 31). With x < 0 the position update (section 7) sets z to 0 before adding `+0x46`, so z is just that displacement, limited to 0 or above, which is far below 2^24: the row is always 0 and every read is inside the extracted grid. Only a state with x < 0 and z of 25 cells or more (word index 1024 to 1247, the first 224 words of the cell records read as grid words) would leave the grid; the position rule makes it unreachable, and the extracted terrain words cover it anyway (1862 words follow the grid). The rig finds water for these positions because the cells read are sea cells: 1500 of 3000 random positions with x in -2^31..-1 and z below 24 cells gave the water bit, and a Python transcription with the unsigned column agrees with the original on all 3000. The port's `terrain_water` must index with `(col & 0xFF) + 32 * (row & 0xFF)` (words, wrapped to 16 bits, then the normal test "word <= 0 means no polygon"), which makes `FL_GUARD_TERRAIN_GRID` unreachable for every legal state; no extractor span is needed. Was: "cell column = `x >> 24`" (section 10), which gives -1 for the first column west of the world.
- **The ejection cycle's effects** [rig; read for the rows marked]. The nibble of `+0x7C` runs 10 (set by the key), 9, 8, 7, then 1 to 6 and holds at 6 (section 8, one value per step). The routine `0x26F60` of the world update looks at the player's nibble after the player's step **in the same pass**, once for each of the records 1 to 3 that is inactive (`W0` bit `0x0040` clear) while `V:4A872` is 0; it acts at three values:

| nibble seen | view | effect | tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 9 | cockpit only | a canopy effect request (`0x26FFE` into the visual-effect routine `0x1A042`, which does nothing without the graphics system); nothing the flight reads | read |
| 1 | any | `V:4A922` = `0x3F`, `V:4A921` = `0x78`, `V:4A870` = `0xFF`: the arguments and the trigger of a delayed effect `0x1A0DC` played by the cue routine (`0x14DD4`-`0x14E3A`); not read by the flight step | rig |
| 3 (the step in which the count reaches 3, and only then) | any | the **seat spawn**, if the latch `V:4AA7A` is 0: see below | rig |
| 14 or more | any | the nibble is cleared and a second object of class `0x31` is spawned (`0x2758E`, latch `V:4A97A`); the cycle never reaches these values | read |

  *The seat spawn* (`0x27522`, the spawner tail `0x276D2`; not `0x2AA8E`, which spawns effect records). In the first inactive record of 1 to 3 it writes a copy of the player's record (164 bytes) with `W0` bit `0x0080` cleared, class `0x30`, the player's attitude composed with fixed rotation increments (3600, 14400, 14400 angle units; sample: pitch 0, heading 2240, roll 0 gives pitch 3576, heading 16640, roll 14400), the player's position plus the model offset (0, 3, 20) rotated by the player's matrix and shifted right by 6, `W0` `|= 0x10C0`, `0x0100`, `0x0002`, `+0x26` = `0x14`, `+0x4C` = -20, `+0x2C` = `0xFFFF`, a thrust of `0x3C0` along the nose added to its velocity, `V:4A910` = `0x8C`; the record is stepped at once in the same pass (rig: `W0` `0x10CA`, class `0x30`, `+0x4C` -19 after that step). **The latch `V:4AA7A`** is set to the record's offset (`0x0200` for record 1; the rig reads the high byte, 2) and blocks `0x26F60` for the rest of the flight (only the start, `0x1D548`, clears it): a second ejection cycle, released by `W2` bit `0x0020`, spawns nothing and sets none of the values below (rig: second cycle with `V:4A8F6`, `V:4A8FD`, `V:4A968`, `V:4A921`, `V:4A922`, `V:4A870` cleared first: all stay 0). The player's own record is not changed by the spawn (whole-block comparison of the step with and without the latch: nothing differs in `0x4B23C..0x4B43B`).
  *In the cockpit view only* the spawn also switches the view to the seat (`0x1EC14`): `V:4A994` = 1, `V:4A996` = `0x0200`, `V:4A9F2` = `0xFFFF` (with `V:4A9EE`/`F` = `0xFF`), the notice `V:4A968` = `0xFE` (once), `V:4A895` bit 7 set, `V:4AAFA` = `0x80`, `V:4A8F5` = 3, a queued view-change event (`V:4A85B` = 1 and the event-echo queue from `V:4A899`), and the request table `V:4A8EE`, `V:4A8EF`, `V:4A8F1` to `V:4A8F9`, `V:4A8FB` to `V:4A8FD` (14 bytes, filled with 3 by `0x1EFF4`, which the frame routine and the view changes also call; the HUD code at `0x35630` and `0x35852` counts some of them down) is set to 3, so **`V:4A8F6` and `V:4A8FD` become 3**. In the external view none of that happens: the step differs from the latched one only in the new record, `V:4A910` and the latch (whole-block comparison: 162 bytes differ in the cockpit view, 132 in the external view, the common part being the record, the latch, `V:4A910` and the composition's scratch words `V:4AB04..`, `V:4ABF8`, `V:4AC50..V:4AC7D`). The seat's own life (its step as class `0x30`, who sets `W2` bit `0x0020`, the parachute) is milestone 5 (open question 6).

#### 17.11 The collision phase (sections 9, 10 and 11b)

- **The deck polygon list** [rig: 60,000 sampled points, the Task 5 comparison; read]. The structure S0 (the first pointer of the first group of the list `0x3E0F8`, `0x3E78A` for the carrier) is one array of words. It **starts at its first word**, which is at the same time the byte offset of the reference vertex r (refy is the y word of that vertex shifted right by L_c) and the first vertex offset of the first polygon. A polygon is a run of vertex byte offsets (relative to record `+0xA4`, 6 per slot, masked with `0xFFF`) whose **last word has bit 15 set**; the edges go from each vertex to the next and from the last back to the first. The list ends at a word with bit 15 set where a polygon would start (`0xFFFF`; vertex offset 0 is a valid vertex, so 0 does not end it). A polygon whose test fails is skipped by running to its last word. The first polygon that contains the point is the deck; none containing it leaves the deck bits clear. Was: "S0 holds deck polygons: each is a list of vertex byte offsets (bit 15 on the last one)" with no start or end.
- **Entities scanned and the box test** [read; the carrier box in the rig, six edge cases]. For each record 1 to 14: `W0` bit `0x0040` (valid) set, bits `0x0600` clear, a different group byte `+0x5E`, and `W0` bit `0x0008` set on either record. The test compares the other record's **current** position with the player's **previous** position (position minus the displacement of this step) on **all three axes**, and a difference of exactly the half-size is **inside** (a larger one rejects). For a class `0x20` record (carrier, second ship) the half-size is `0xA0000` (655,360 position units) on every axis; the deck test follows only for class `0x20`. (Other classes, read only: class `0x15` half-sizes `0x5000`; class nibble `0x30` skipped; otherwise `0x10000` when the player's class nibble differs, else `0x7000`, or `0x6000` when either record has `W2` bit `0x0001`.) A hit sets the contact flag `V:4A957` and `W2` bit `0x0001` of the other record. Run with the player at +-`0xA0000` and +-`0xA0001` from the carrier on x, z and y: the carrier's `W2` bit 0 is set exactly at the half-size.
- **Order inside a pass** [read, rig]. Pass 0 tests the player's model vertex 0 (nose), pass 1 vertex 3, pass 1 only for the viewed entity. For a pass: if the point is **below deck level** (P.y below refy) only the hull volumes are tested (the remaining pointers of the first group of `0x3E0F8` as one volume, then each later group as one volume); a hit returns `0x20`; **no hit ends the scan of this entity** (no polygon test, no deck bit, no corridor, no second list, and no pass 1). Otherwise the polygon list decides; on a hit B4 bit `0x40` (pass 0) or `0x80` (pass 1) is set and `+0x4E` = refy.
- **The arrestor corridor and the engaged flag** [rig, 14 cases]. In pass 1 with the wheels down (`W2` bit `0x0080`) the corridor tests of section 9 run. In pass 1 with the wheels **up** they count as failed. A failed test clears `W2` bit `0x4000` **unless the hook is down (`0x8000`) and the bit was already set**: a hook-down aircraft that has caught the wire keeps the bit with the wheels up as well (run over the deck, gear up, at 36,000 units: `W2` `0xC000` keeps bit `0x4000`; with the hook up or the bit not yet set it is cleared). Pass 0 does not touch the bit. Was: only the wheels-down case; the port cleared the bit unconditionally with the wheels up.
- **The second list T2** [rig, read]. It is tested in **both passes**, after the deck bit is set (and after the corridor in pass 1), not only in pass 1 with the wheels down. Was: placed in the paragraph of the arrestor corridor.
- **`V:4A957`** [rig]. The collision routine clears it at entry (`0x2B5E2`); the box overlap of any record and the landmark outer box set it; the player's tail (no collision result) reads it into `W2` bit `0x0002` and nothing clears it afterwards. It is therefore **a transient within the player's step**: the later entity steps of the same world update call the routine again and clear it, so it reads 0 after the update (the observation of Task 5, "consumed in every path") and `W2` bit 1 follows the value the tail saw (set when the player overlapped a record or a landmark box, cleared otherwise). The port keeps the flag local to the step.
- **Landmark slot numbering** [read; rig for the test]. The collision phase walks the queue C backwards from `V:4AA7E` (the last emitted 24-byte entry) in steps of 24 bytes, at most `V:4A916` entries; an entry whose flag byte (low byte of its first word) has bit 4 (`0x10`, entity) or bit 6 (`0x40`, secondary object) set uses no slot; every other entry takes the next slot number 0, 1, 2, ... up to 10 (**eleven ordinals, 0 to 10**; a slot whose pointer-table entry is -1 still uses its number but is skipped for the test). That is the same numbering as the build-time filling of note 04a section 7 ("slot 0 is the last generated one"; the first 11 non-entity entries from the end), applied to the **current** queue order (the gun's tracer test walks the same eleven ordinals: note 07 8.2, `weapon_check.py landmark`, 12,600 cases, 0 differences; there the z offset of a volume also carries the high word of the table pointer divided by 4, as the player's volumes do, and a miss at the last chain element returns with no hit and without the motion step), which differs from the build order only after the per-frame sort (3 of 400 states in 04a). A rig test of the phase can therefore be built by filling the slot pointer table `0x5283C` and the 11 slot records `0x52868` (stride 256) as 04a section 7 describes and putting matching 24-byte entries (any flags, a fresh descriptor) in queue C ending at `V:4AA7E`.

#### 17.12 Collision results and their handling (section 11)

- **`V:4A844`** (collisions enabled; the game's value is **1**, set by `0x1D38A` with `V:4A83C..V:4A847`; the three captured snapshots read 1; base block B0 now lists it). It is read in two places only: the result handler and the hand-over. With `V:4A844` = 0 a result `0x20` or `0x40` still zeroes E, Y and Q, still undoes the displacement (`0x20`, class not 0) and refreshes the cell fields, and then the handler **ends the step** (camera trail only): no `W0` bit `0x8000`, no crash bits, no notice, and also not the no-result tail (so `W2` bit 1 and the dying entry are not processed). The result `0x10` is **not** gated by it. With an ejection nibble the dying path is taken before this test and is not gated either (below). The hand-over clears a crash and posts `0x4021` (section 11). [read; Task 5 adversarial test; the nibble case rig]
- **`V:4A952`** (immune, set to 1 by the cockpit hand-over, cleared by the resume state and by the start). It is read in two places only: the touchdown crash (above) and the contact handler. A contact above 80 knots (W above `0x3C0`) with `V:4A952` set sets `W0` bit `0x8000` (every contact does) and **does nothing more** (no `0x0200`, no counters, no damage message). [read; Task 5 adversarial test]
- **First contact at 80 knots or less** [read, emu: `V:4AC12`]. The first result `0x20` with `W0` bit `0x8000` newly set calls the bump effect `0x1A0DC` (arguments `0x1C` and `0x30`); contacts after the first do not. With the sound system running (`V:4AC12` bit 0 set at `0x19ADE`; the captured game states read 7) the routine starts the bump sound and sets nothing the HUD shows; only with the bit clear (the rig, which has no sound system) does it set the notice `V:4A968` = `0xFA` and `V:4A84F` = 2. Was: "a bump sound once for a landmark". The port requests the sound and shows no notice.
- **Result `0x10` and the part id `+0x7B`** [rig, emu]. The last test of the routine (`0x2C08C`) is live only for an aircraft class (high nibble 1) whose `+0x7B` is non-negative with a **zero low nibble**; `+0x7B` is the **part id the renderer stored** (low byte of the first word of the model part drawn last for that entity; the single writer is `0x21F10`; the aircraft body is part 0, the cockpit block `0xFF`). For vertices 0, 1 and 2 in that order, the rotated vertex y word shifted right by L (`+0x7D & 15`, 3) plus the long `+0x10` (y >> 8) is tested; the first negative sum returns `0x10`. With `+0x7B` negative or with a non-zero nibble only `+0x10` itself is tested (negative means below y = 0). The handler for `0x10`: `W2` bit `0x0080` set, no displacement undo, `W0` bit `0x8000` set, and above 80 knots without `V:4A952` the crash bits of the other results; it is not gated by `V:4A844`. Which part is drawn for the player is settled in note 04a section 8a (the prelude `0x21C44`, run once per draw; the byte is stored by the single writer `0x21F10`, after the detail level is chosen, only for an active record). Summary [read; emu for the threshold]: **in the cockpit the body (part id 0) is drawn only with pitch band `V:4A923` = 3, or with band 1 or 2 and the eye measure `V:4AB30 >> LOD` at or above -32** (and for one pass after leaving an external view, `V:4A8ED`, and in the head views 3 to 9); in every other cockpit case, notably level flight and shallow dives (band 0), flares and the roll-out, **the cockpit block (id `0xFF`) is drawn at any height** and the test is off. In the external views the ring cameras always draw the body (id 0), and the front-end approach cameras (camera modes 2 to 6; `V:4A966` is 0 in a flight, note 07 6.1), standing farther away, can give 1, 2 or `0xFF` as well (the test is then off). The eye measure is about -(y >> 8) - 1.5 in 256-unit steps (`V:4AB70` is the level-of-detail shift of the entry being drawn, 0 for the player's own entry in the cockpit), so -32 is about 7,700 position units, 38 ft, for the bands 1 and 2 only; it is not a height threshold of its own. **`V:4A923` is not a setting**: its only writer is the pitch-gate routine `0x143F2`, which derives it every frame from v = pitch word / 8 (0.1 degree, nose down positive): v up to 100 gives 0, 101 to 300 gives 1, 301 to 600 and 3150 to 3199 give 2, 601 to 3149 gives 3, 3200 and above gives 0. The byte keeps its last value in any pass in which the record is not drawn (the restart does not reset it; a record never drawn reads 0), and the collision of step N reads the byte stored by the draw of frame N-1, chosen with the pitch band and eye measure of the camera set-up of frame N-1 (one-pass lag; the world update runs before the camera set-up and the draw). The capture (`V:4A923` = 1, view 1) brackets the code's threshold: the samples -40 (y = 9,744) and -23 (y = 5,368) lie on either side of -32, and `+0x7B` turned 0 in the sample that read -23. Was: "`+0x7B` negative (0xFF) for the player: disables the three-vertex ground test (inert...)", true only for the cockpit block (bands and eye measure above), not "above a height".
- **The result handler for the player, in order** [rig: `W0` `0x11C8`, `0x10C8`, `0x01C8`, `0x09C8`, `0x19C8`, `0x00C8` in both views, ejection nibble 0 and 5, speeds 1100 and 500, plus a scan of the initial speed; read `0x2A758`-`0x2A9C0`]. R is the result (`0x10` ground test, `0x20` hull or landmark, `0x40` aircraft), W the indicated speed `+0x6E` **as left by this step's speed step** (unchanged when the speed step did not run, that is when `W0` lacks `0x1000`), N the ejection nibble, I = `V:4A952`.
  1. R = `0x20`: E, Y and Q are zeroed; for class not 0 the displacement is undone (x and z, and y when `W2` bit `0x0080` is clear). All results: the cell words are refreshed.
  2. **N non-zero: go to 7.** No hit bit, no crash bits, no `V:4A87D`/`V:4A850`, no statistics counter, no notice, no first-contact effect, whatever W, I, `V:4A844` and the view are (rig: 0x07C8 from 0x11C8 at speed 500 and 1100, both views, `V:4A844` 0 and I = 1 included).
  3. R = `0x10`: `W2` bit `0x0080` is set; go to 5 (no `V:4A844` test, no first-contact effect).
  4. `V:4A844` = 0: the step ends. R = `0x20` with `W0` bit `0x8000` not yet set and W at most `0x3C0`: the bump effect `0x1A0DC` (17.12 first contact) before 5.
  5. `W0` bit `0x8000` is set. W at most `0x3C0` (signed word compare; `0x3C0` itself is not enough): the step ends. I non-zero: the step ends.
  6. R = `0x40`: the damage path (milestone 5). R = `0x20` or `0x10`: `W0` bit `0x0200`, the statistics counter, `V:4A87D` = 1, `V:4A850` = 4. **Cockpit view: the step ends here** (no finalisation). External view: go on.
  7. *The dying path* (external view after 6, or any view with N non-zero): `B20` bit `0x02`; for R not `0x40` `W0` bit `0x1000` is cleared; for R = `0x40` the camera follows (`V:54E72`), `W2` bit `0x1000` and `+0x05` = 1 are set and the step ends. For R `0x10` or `0x20` and a class with high nibble 1 (the player): `W0` bit `0x0400`, `+0x4C` = 15, `W0` bit `0x0200` (kept for the player, cleared for other records). The explosion record of section 11 is made only for class-0 entities, so the player gets none here; the explosion of the player's crash is the effect started by the hand-over in the cockpit view (section 11) and none in the external view.
  8. The camera trail; end.
  Consequences. (a) **The external finalisation does not depend on `W0` bits `0x1000` and `0x0100`**: all six words finalise in the external view above the threshold (`0x87C8` from `0x11C8`, `0x86C8` from `0x10C8`, `0x8FC8` from `0x09C8`, with `B20` bit `0x02`, `+0x4C` = 15, `V:4A87D` = 1, `V:4A850` = 4); the earlier port rule "finalise when `W0` & `0x1100` = `0x1100`" and the observation that `0x10C8` "does something else" are not reproduced: a hull hit of the `0x10C8` word in the **cockpit** view gives `0x92C8` (crash bit, no finalisation), and at W of `0x3C0` or less, with I non-zero, or with `V:4A844` = 0 the external view does not finalise either. (b) **The speed condition** is W greater than `0x3C0`, tested on the word after this step's speed step: for `W0` words with `0x1000` the initial speed 1021 is the first to crash (the speed step takes 60 off, W = 961; 1020 gives 960 and no crash), for words without `0x1000` (`0x01C8`, `0x09C8`, `0x00C8`) there is no speed step and the initial 961 is the first. `W0` `0x09C8` (brake on, no `0x1000`) therefore behaves as `0x01C8`; with `0x19C8` the brake changes nothing at the threshold (initial 1021 as for `0x11C8`). (c) **A hull hit with an ejection nibble** (step 2): the record becomes `W0` `0x07C8` from `0x11C8` (`0x06C8` from `0x10C8`, `0x0FC8` from `0x09C8`): out of control (`B20` `|= 0x02`), `0x1000` cleared, `0x0400` and `0x0200` set, `+0x4C` = 15, the displacement undone; `W0` bit `0x8000` is **not** set, `V:4A87D` and `V:4A850` stay as they were and the notice `V:4A968` is not raised, in both views and at any speed. (d) The same ejecting aircraft hit by result `0x10` takes the same path without the undo.
- **Q14, the extra `0x8000` of the stall run** [emu, rig]. Settled: it is result `0x10`. Reference emulator, stall run with records 1 to 14 and `+0x7B` peeked per blank (`openers-stall-records.txt`, `openers-stall-part.txt`; logs `stall-records.txt`, `stall-part.txt`): at the impact records 1 to 13 read `W0` = `0x0010` (not valid) and the carrier `0x405C`, six cells away, so no other aircraft (result `0x40`) and no hull; `+0x7B` changes from `0xFF` to `0x00` at y = 5,368 (eye measure -23, the sample before it -40 at y = 9,744), in the step before the touchdown; the impact step writes `W0` = `0x93C8` with the position advance kept (result `0x10` does not undo), `W2` `0x2080`, B4 `0x02`; `W0` reads `0x91C8` after the hand-over (the bit `0x0200` cleared, `0x8000` stays because the nose vertex is still below the ground and the result recurs) until the restart at the end of the hold. Rig: the E8 state with the model vertices of the F/A-18 rotated for pitch 2000 and `+0x7B` = 0 gives `0x93C8` and the position advance; with `+0x7B` = `0xFF` it gives `0x17C8` (the reading of section 14). Illustration [read]: with the gear down the reference point rests at y = 1,800 (7 steps); the nose vertex (0, -10, 384) is below the ground when its rotated y component is below -56 model units, that is, with the nose about 7 degrees or more below the horizon.

#### 17.13 Warnings and direct posts (section 12)

- **Acceptance** [rig]. The post routine `0x29BC8` (note 06: "post a message code word"; the arbiter proper, which picks the code word every frame, is `0x13D4C`) accepts every post: it stores the code in `V:4AB98`, sets `V:4A984` bit 0 and, for a code whose high byte has bit 5 set, `V:4A986` bit 13 (bit 15 cleared); codes `0x4000` and `0x4800` clear that bit. The **caller** refuses a repeat: each of the three direct posts is made only when `V:4AB98` differs from its code (any latch state). A refused post does nothing: no cue bit, no skipped speed step, and the later clauses of the chain run. Was: not stated; note 06 (the arbiter `0x13D4C`) is the owner of how `V:4AB98` is cleared again.
- **Order** [rig]. Only for the player and with `V:4A984` bit 6, after the input conversion: (1) out of control (`B20` bit `0x02`) and descending (`+0x42` negative): *only* the eject clause is considered: no ejection nibble, and `|+0x42| * 64` at least y: post `0xD02A`, `V:4AC0C` bit `0x40`, end of the chain; a slow descent posts nothing, **not even the bank clause**; (2) otherwise the crash-imminent clauses: the descent clause (pitch between `0x1E0` and `0x3840` and `|+0x42| * 16` at least y with a descending `+0x42`) or the bank clause (roll in `0x4B0..0x3840` or `..0x6BD0`, y at most `0x1800`); either posts `0x9C06` with `V:4AC0C` bit `0x80` unless the wheels are down (`W2` bit `0x0080`) or the code is already `0x9C06`; (3) the proximity post `0xD00A` (viewed player, cockpit, `W2` bit 0 set, code not already `0xD00A`) with `V:4AC0C` bit `0x80`. After a **made** post of (1) or (2) the chain ends and the speed step of this step is skipped (S keeps its value; the next step runs it); after (3) and after a refused post the chain continues to the speed step.
- **`V:4AC0C`** [read]. A long of one-shot cue bits that the cue player (`0x14B5A`) takes one per pass in this order and then clears whole (nothing is taken while the cool-down `V:4A942` is above 0, and then the bits stay; `0x40` is tested first and plays whatever the ejection nibble, every other bit is discarded without a cue when the nibble is non-zero; **bits not taken are lost**: after any cue is played, or discarded, the routine clears the whole long, `0x14DCE`): `0x40` eject cue (V:4AC0F bit 6), `0x80` crash-imminent and proximity cue (bit 7), `0x01`, `0x04`, `0x100` (`V:4AC0E` bit 0), `0x10`, `0x02`, `0x20` stall lamp (V:4AC0F bit 5), `0x800` (`V:4AC0E` bit 3), `0x08`. The flight step sets `0x80`, `0x40` (the posts), `0x20` (the stall flag, section 5) and `0x200` (`V:4AC0E` bit 1, the roll-out sound at `0x170F0`, which no branch of the cue player tests); the others are set by the HUD and weapon code (`0x36216`, `0x36246`, `0x362B0`, `0x275EA`, `0x3868E`, `0x386B6`, `0x38878`, `0x38958`, `0x38978`, `0x14054`, `0x14082`; notes 06 and 07). Cleared by the start. The Task 5 reading "`V:4AC0F`: 0x80 crash cue, 0x40 eject cue, 0x20 stall lamp; `V:4AC0E` bit 1 = long bit 0x200" is right.

#### 17.14 Start of a flight (section 15) [rig, emu]

- **The start blocks.** The table at `0x47ADE` has 16-byte entries of eight words, indexed by `V:4A900` (set by the mission setup: `0x121F8` gives 0, `0x12222` gives 2, `0x12102`, `0x12252` and `0x11C10` give 3; the location keys of the free-flight screen store 0 to 3 through `0x1EBBA`; note 08 owns the choice). A first word below zero marks a carrier-type start; a first word of -1 ends the table.

| `V:4A900` | entry | kind | words (cell x, cell z, n, ox, oz, fx, fz, degrees) | start position (x, y, z) | heading |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | `0x47ADE` | runway of airfield 1 | eight words (`tables/ai.bin`, see below) | 273963296, 1800, 279184624 | 2240 |
| 1 | `0x47AEE` | airfield 2 | eight words | 264178832, 1800, 286095856 | 24800 |
| 2 | `0x47AFE` | airfield 3 | eight words | 254084992, 1800, 265102320 | 27200 |
| 3 | `0x47B0E` | carrier deck, first word `0x800E` = `0x8000` + record 14 | | see below | the carrier record's |
| 4 | `0x47B1E` | second-ship deck, `0x800F` = record 15 (no mission-flow writer leaves 4 in `V:4A900`: the only write of 4, at `0x11C08`, is overwritten with 3 at `0x11C10`, two instructions later [read]; the location selection's digit keys can store 3 to 9 (note 08 11.3, captured for 4 and 5), and the start then clears the selector to 0 because record 15 is inactive) | | see below | the record's |

  The five 16-byte entries are the start-block sub-table of `tables/ai.bin` (note 07 section 3.6; FNV-1a-32 of its data `f2fbff97`); the extractor exports them and the port reads them, so the notes print no word.

  A runway start: x = cell x * 2^24 + dirx(n) * 1024 + ox * 1024 + fx * 16, z likewise with cell z, dirz(n), oz and fz; (dirx, dirz) is the word pair at `0x206F2 + 4 n` (the table of section 10); y = `0x708`; heading = degrees * 80; pitch and roll 0. The routine also sets `+0x0B` = n, the cell words `+0x06`, `+0x08` (and the viewed cell `V:4AA04`, `V:4AA06`) from the cell and the signed byte pairs at `0x207E6`, and the fields of section 15. Run in the rig (`0x1D4E2` with `V:4A901` = `0x11`): blocks 0, 1 and 2 give exactly the positions and headings of the table; block 0 is the position of E1.
- **A carrier start** (`0x1D730`). The record r (14 or 15) must be active (`W0` low byte bit `0x40`); if not, `V:4A900` is cleared and the start is made as block 0. The player's x and z are the record's position plus `((11 * M0 + 104 * M2) >> 4, (11 * M6 + 104 * M8) >> 4)`, M the nine words of the record's matrix at `+0x92`; so heading 0 gives (+11,264, +106,496) and heading 28600 gives (+15,900, +105,900). y = (D << 8) + `0x708` with D the deck height: the long at +2 of the model header named by the pointer at +0x10 of the record's descriptor, its bit 31 being a marker (it must be set) that is cleared; D = 112 for the carrier (header `0x3E1A0`), so y = 30,472 and `+0x10` = D + 7. Pitch, heading and roll are copied from the record. B4 becomes `(B4 & 0x35) | 0xC8`, `W2` is changed only by the common lines of the routine (bits `0xB108` cleared, `0x0080` set, all others kept: 17.17 rule 4); `+0x0A` and `+0x0B` are derived from the position (17.18 f). Run in the rig with the carrier at three positions and headings (0, 28600, 9000): the offsets above, y = 30,472 and the copied attitude.
- **The carrier record's placement** is not made by the start routine but by the mission setup (`0x2CE36`, the table at `0x2DEDA`; 17.17 rule 5 states the rule). **Free flight, both start kinds** (a runway start and a carrier start): the carrier is record 14 at (295174144, 0, 274202624), attitude (0, 0, 0), and the carrier-start player is at its position plus (+11,264, +106,496), `y` = 30,472 (captured: the cockpit-ground, airborne and cockpit-carrier snapshots read this position, attitude and cell (70, 65), with `W0` `0x405C`, `0x405C` and `0x404C` where the placement alone gives `0x0148`; the placement is the same for every selector value, rig). **The demonstration** (the demo snapshot, and `0x2CE36` for modes 0x7E and 0x7F) has it at (286785536, 0, 286785536) with heading **28600**: that is the position and heading of the worked examples E5 and E6, not free flight's (earlier text: "the carrier start of free flight (04a): 286785536" was wrong). The collision code uses the record's matrices (`+0x80`, `+0x92` and the vertex slots rotated by them) and the renderer draws the carrier with the same record, so the world module must give both the **same heading**; in free flight both are 0.
- **Base block B0** now lists `V:4A844` = 1 (it was 0 in the examples; no example has a collision result, so no printed row changes).

- **The heading of the out-of-table starts, key 0 (settled; V: rig).** The runway start computes the heading as the word at +14 of the entry times 80 **in a word shift**: the entry's degrees are multiplied by 10 (a long product) and shifted left by 3 as a **16-bit word operation**, so the stored heading is the **low 16 bits** of degrees x 80, not the reduced angle. Key 0 reads the degrees word 3200 from the entry at `0x47B6E` (`V:4A900` = 9): 3200 x 80 = 256,000 is **0xE800 = 59,392** in the word (signed -6,144), never the 256,000 of the long arithmetic and not 25,600 (256,000 modulo 28,800). The attitude setter `0x3202C` stores the three words at +0x66 to +0x6A **unchanged** and builds the two matrices from them with the builders of 17.9 (a rig call with pitch 0, heading 59,392, roll 0: the heading word stays 59,392; matrix A at +0x80 = (19801, 0, -64, 0, 16384, 0, 64, 0, 19801) and matrix B at +0x92 = (4, 0, -611, 0, 16384, 0, 611, 0, 4), not rotations (the heading is logically shifted right by 3 to 7,424, past the 3,600 entries of the angle, and the table over-read of 17.9 supplies the numbers); the same call with 25,600 gives the proper pair (12551, 0, -10531 ...)). **The port stores the word 0xE800 for key 0 and lets the builders and the over-read of 17.9 produce the matrix; it does not reduce the word modulo 28,800.** The keys 7, 8 and 9 read degrees 0 (heading 0). Evidence: `keyheading_check.py` runs the original's player start `0x1D4E2` for the selectors 0 to 9 (V:4A900; the heading words 2,240, 24,800 and 27,200 of the three airfields, 0 for the selectors 6 to 8, 59,392 for selector 9; the rule above gives all of them, 0 differences, and the selector 9 matrix is the non-rotation quoted), and the code `0x1D6C6`-`0x1D72C`.
- **The roll word `V:4AB4A` outside the cockpit, and the writers of the camera angle longs (settled; R by every reference, V by the rig).** `V:4AB40`, `V:4AB44` and `V:4AB48` (pitch, heading, roll as longs; the camera words `V:4AB42`, `V:4AB46`, `V:4AB4A` are their low words) have **three store sites and no others** (the scan of all absolute references: `0x3227C`, `0x3236A` and `0x321C0`; `0x14410`/`0x14418` and `0x3237E`/`0x330BA` only read). The first two are the cockpit branch `0x321F0` of the per-frame camera entry (code 0: the viewed record's words +0x66, +0x68, +0x6A sign-extended; other head codes: the three outputs of the composition 17.9). **The third is the end of the external branch (`0x32198`, entered by every path of `0x32092`: the stored aim pair is turned into the matrix and the horizon matrix is built, then the three words at record 0 (the player's own record) +0x66, +0x68, +0x6A are copied, sign-extended, to the three longs):** so in every external view, the map and the tower included, **`V:4AB4A` is the player's roll word (record 0 +0x6A), `V:4AB42` the player's pitch and `V:4AB46` the player's heading, rewritten every frame; they are not stale and they do not follow the viewed record or the camera** (the earlier remark that these words keep their last cockpit value is withdrawn). The only reader of `V:4AB4A` besides the matrix entry is the whole-window branch of the ground polygon routine (`0x330BA`: roll at or above 14,400 chooses the vertex order of the four corners), which draws the same rectangle either way, so **the value is unobservable in the pixels**; it is observable only as the vertex order left in `V:5043C` (and in `V:504DE` for the ball, which is not drawn in an external view). Port: `game.c` may pass the player's roll instead of the camera's in the external views (an exact, free change); nothing else depends on it. Evidence: the reference scan (4 references to the long, 2 to the word, listed above) and the code at `0x32188`-`0x321C8`, read; no rig run of the copy itself (tag R).
- **The player's `+0x38` byte (settled as "never written"; R by the reference scan, V by the census of captured records).** The byte is the target or leader field of note 07 2 (`0xFF` none, bit 7 set plus a record index, else a shift count). A scan of the whole code image for stores whose destination is the displacement `0x38` from an address register finds 12 byte stores: `0x27846` (copy of the owner's byte to a spawned record), `0x27A1C` and `0x27F54` (the value 0x80), `0x27EC6` (0x80 plus a record index), `0x281D0`, `0x2CF38`, `0x2D1AC` and `0x2D61C` (0xFF), `0x2CF90`, `0x2D1FE`, `0x2D472` and `0x2D17E` (a register or 0x80); all lie in the AI step, the placement and the spawn routines (`0x27xxx`, `0x28xxx`, `0x2Cxxx`, `0x2Dxxx`), which act on the record the loop or the placer holds in A0 or A1; no store to the absolute address of record 0's byte (`0x4B274`) exists. Whether the AI step (`0x27A1C`, `0x27F54`) is ever entered with record 0 in its register is not shown by the scan (R); the census settles it in practice: **record 0's byte is 0 in 2,841 of 2,841 captured dumps of the player's record** (all logs of `extracted/captures/logs` that hold it: free flight, missions, training, qualification, demo, crashes). Port: keep the player's byte at 0 (it is not written by the port, as now); the "open" comment in `entities.c` can name this section.

#### 17.15 Corrections (statements of the earlier text that the findings above show wrong)

Each is also fixed where it stood, marked "(corrected, 17.x)". The Task 5 code needs a fix round for the items marked **port** (C1 to C18 from the first version of the addendum, C19 onwards from the fix round); **extractor** marks a change for the extractor.

| # | earlier statement | correct rule | port |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | section 1 item a: external view without `W0` `0x1000`/`0x0100`: "only the bookkeeping below runs" (read as: the attitude step still runs) | the input conversion, warnings, speed, attitude and motion steps are all skipped (17.2) | **port** (two W2 divergences) |
| C2 | `W2` bit `0x4000` over the deck with the wheels up is cleared | cleared unless the hook is down and the bit was set (17.11) | **port** |
| C3 | section 3: exhaustion sets sound `V:4A8F6` = 2 (every step in the port) | only in the step in which `V:4AC08` `0x40` is newly set; the flags `0x43` are replaced by the one flag, not OR-ed (17.5) | **port** (the sound byte) |
| C4 | section 5 intro: without `V:4A984` bit 6 `0x1557C` clears `V:4AC08` `0x43` | the routine is not called without bit 6: the flags are untouched (17.7) | none (the port already leaves them) |
| C5 | section 5 decay: "otherwise x moves one unit toward 0" | 0 becomes -1 (17.7) | none |
| C6 | section 7 touchdown: "Otherwise, with W != 0: S = W" | S = W only when v_y is below `0x240` (17.10) | none |
| C7 | section 8: gear sound on the rising branch | on every change of the retract progress (17.10) | none |
| C8 | section 2 mouse: pointer height 0..960 | -960..960; negative = idle; the follow and fire rules (17.4) | **port** (the absolute throttle axis) |
| C9 | state block `+0x7B`: "negative (`0xFF`) for the player: disables the three-vertex ground test" and sections 11, 11b: "for the player it is inactive" | `+0x7B` is the renderer's part id (single writer `0x21F10`); the test is live when it is non-negative with a zero low nibble. In the cockpit that is the body (id 0), drawn only with pitch band `V:4A923` = 3, or band 1 or 2 with `V:4AB30 >> LOD` at or above -32, in the head views 3 to 9 and for one pass after leaving an external view (`V:4A8ED`) (17.12); level flight (band 0), flares and the roll-out draw the cockpit block (`0xFF`) at any height.
