#### 17.17 Crash hand-over and restart states (the Task 6 gaps) [emu, rig, read]

**Evidence.** All captures start from one snapshot of the airborne state in a nose-down dive about 20 frames above the sea (`crash-setup-dive.txt` writes it) and are aligned to the frame by the new script command `stepuntil` on the crash bit, so one sample is one blank and k counts blanks from the first sample whose `W0` shows bit `0x0200` (k = 0). `crash-flow-cockpit.txt` (cockpit crash: per blank the step counter, the state pointer `0x1A6BC`, the first 32 bytes of the player record, `V:4A951`, `V:4A84D`, `V:4A910`, `V:4A94F`, `V:4A984`, `V:4A982`, `+0x7B`, the fuel, `V:4AB8E`, with frame dumps), the same crash in the external view (`crash-flow-external.txt`), an external crash with the switch to the cockpit while dying (`crash-flow-external-to-cockpit.txt`), the view key before the hand-over and during the hold and the pause key during the hold (`crash-keys-*.txt`), the pointer during the hold (`crash-mouse-in-hold.txt`), the game-over path with the allowance set to 1 by the new command `poke` (`crash-flow-gameover.txt`) and reads of the flight snapshots (`crash-allowance-snapshots.txt`, `carrier-record-snapshots.txt`, `carrier-sublist-select.txt`, `crash-return-key.txt`, `crash-esc-key.txt`), and the fix-round captures `crash-pass-a-p7b.txt` (the part id forced before pass A), `crash-hold-palette.txt` and `crash-hold-flash.txt` (the palette and the frames per blank in the hold) and `crash-gameover-gate.txt` (the game-over wait with the gate); tables of results in 01b, section "Addendum 2 (note 05, 17.17)" and its fix-round addendum. Pass lengths vary by 1 to 3 blanks from run to run (a pass ends on a blank and the machine's cost is not constant to the blank): the lengths below are from one run and the spread is stated where it was measured. Rig: `0x1D538` run on the three real variable blocks (airborne, cockpit-ground, demo) for the runway start and the carrier start, each as captured and with the record and `V:4A800..V:4AC1F` XORed with `0xFF` and with `0x5A`, and once with the whole variable hunk (`0x4A6E8..0x54EBF`) XORed; a byte is "written" when it changed in any run.

**The state names (one set, for notes 05 and 06).** The mission state routine is called through the pointer at `0x1A6BC` once per pass (17.1). The routines on the crash path:

| address | name | role |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `0x12EFE` | **flight state** | the state while flying; it runs in every pass. Its crash branch is the **hand-over** (section 11); the same routine also handles the pause screen, the mission end and the like (note 08). There is no separate hand-over state |
| `0x12FB8` | (not a state) | the operand of the instruction at `0x12FB6` that stores `0x138D8` as the new state at the end of the hand-over branch. Earlier text (section 15, note 06) named it a state: **wrong** |
| `0x138D8` | **crash wait state** | tests the view first (rule 1), restarts when its condition holds (rules 2 and 4) |
| `0x13980` | **resume state 1** | one pass: second restart, recentres the head (cockpit view only), sets the countdown `V:4AB8E` to 5 |
| `0x139C2` | **resume wait state** | waits for `V:4AB8E` to go below 0 (six passes), then sets `V:4A984` bit 6 and returns to `0x12EFE` |
| `0x139F0`, `0x13A36` | game-over states | rule 7 |

**A pass, in order** (extends 17.1; the frame routine `0x11124`): the key poll (`0x11514`: every event in the key queue, in order, goes to the key handler `0x1DAA0`), then the accounting routine `0x11748`, which decrements `V:4AB8E` (at `0x11942`) and calls the state routine (`0x11956`), the message generator (`0x13C94`), the gate `V:4A84D` (non-zero: the flight path), the view toggle `0x141E8` (it applies a pending toggle request, `V:4A97E` bit 1, set by KP-ENTER: cockpit to the external view `V:4A8EB`, or back with `V:4A83D` = 0 and `V:4A8ED` = 1, and recentres the head in the cockpit view when no head request is pending), the world update with the player step, the display lists, the draw, then the **step counter `n`** (`0x114B6`, not while `V:4A866` is non-zero), and last the flip routine `0x1827C`, called by the main loop after the frame routine. So **the counter turns at the end of the frame routine, a blank or less before the next pass begins**, and "the pass N+1" below is the pass that starts at the next turn (naming, as in section 14: a pass is named by the value of `n` while it runs; the turn at its end gives the next pass's name; the column "n at the end" is the pass's name plus one). Two consequences measured below: a key is polled before the state routine of the same pass but its view toggle is applied after it; and the state routine of a pass sees the view as the previous pass left it.

**Cockpit crash, per pass** (`crash-flow-cockpit.txt`; the rows are the passes after the one in which the crash bit appeared; lengths in blanks; n is the counter at the end of the pass; `+0x4C` falls by 1 in every pass that runs the world update, which counts them):

| pass | begins at k | state routine of the dispatcher | the pass | length | n at the end |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| N (impact) | before 0 | `0x12EFE` | the world update raises `W0` `0x0200` (and `0x8000`) one blank after the pass began (the pass began before the first sample: the logs hold nothing before k = 0, and `n` reads 0x19B there) | ends at k = 9 | 0x19B + 1 |
| N+1 | 9 | `0x12EFE`, crash branch: **hand-over** | clears `W0` `0x0200`, counts the crash, `V:4A87D` = 1, clears `V:4A982` bit 8 and `V:4A984` bit 6, `V:4A952` = 1, vertical velocity 0, stops the four sound channels (`0x115F6`), starts the crash sound `0x1A0DC`, `V:4A951` = 40, stores `0x138D8`; then the world update of this pass (**one** step, without `0x0200`), the draw and the counter; then the flip routine begins the hold | 9 | +1 |
| hold | 18 | - | the flip routine's loop: 40 iterations of 2 blanks (rule 3); no frame routine, no step, no counter | 80 to 81 | - |
| A | 99 | `0x138D8`: **restart** | `0x1D538` first, then (the allowance is positive) `V:4A959` = `0xF`, `V:4A941` = 0, `V:4A98C` bit `0x0400` cleared, `V:4A94F` minus 1, state `0x13980`; then the world update (`V:4A984` bit 6 clear, yet the player step runs its attitude step, a zero motion, the cell refresh and the collision phase, rule 2), the display-list rebuild (`V:4A910` = `0xFF`, then `0xFC`), the draw and the counter | 14 | +1 |
| B | 113 | `0x13980` | `V:4A952` = 0, `W2` bit `0x0002` set, the head recentred (cockpit view only), `0x1D538` **again**, `V:4AB8E` = 5, state `0x139C2`; world update, list rebuild, draw, counter | 13 | +1 |
| C1 to C5 | 126, 137, 147, 159, 167 | `0x139C2`: `V:4AB8E` is 4, 3, 2, 1, 0 after this pass's decrement: wait | world update (bit 6 clear), draw, counter | 11, 10, 12, 8, 9 | +1 each |
| C6 | 176 | `0x139C2`: `V:4AB8E` is -1: `V:4A984` bit 6 set, `V:4A968` = `0xFE`, state `0x12EFE` | **the first simulated step** (bit 6 set before the world update), then ordinary flight | 10 | +1 |

From the end of the hold to the first simulated step: **7 passes without simulation** (A, B, C1 to C5, 76 to 77 blanks, 1.5 s) and the eighth is the first step. The counter counts through all of them (n = 0x19D at the hold, 0x1A4 when `0x12EFE` returns). The player record is rewritten in pass A and changes again in A's own world update and in B (rule 2 gives the staged values: `W0` `0x11C8`, then `0x91C8` after A's collision phase, then `0x11C8` again from B's `0x1D538`; `+0x0A`/`+0x0B` 05/06, then 06/0F, then 06/06). The cell words change once (k = 99: `0x40/0x43` to `0x41/0x42`), the fuel is refilled in A (`0x61A800`), and the second `0x1D538` of pass B writes what the first left different (`W2` `0x0082`, `W0`, `+0x0B`). A second run of the script measured the same passes at k = 96, 110, 123, 134, 144, 155, 163, 172 (the hold 80 blanks from the last turn, then A to C5 of 14, 13, 11, 10, 11, 8 and 9 blanks).

**External crash, per pass** (`crash-flow-external.txt`; k = 0 the first sample with `0x0200`): the finalisation is in the same step (`W0` `0x87C8`, `+0x4C` = 15), the hand-over at the start of the next pass (k = 11: `W0` `0x85C8`, state `0x138D8`; **no** `V:4A951`, no `V:4A952`, no sound, no hold), then the wait: dying passes of 10, 11, 5, 5, 4, 5, 4, 4, 4, 4, 4, 5, 4 and 4 blanks (the first two are long; the world update of the dying record is cheap afterwards) with `+0x4C` counting 14 down to 1; the expiry step is in the pass beginning at k = 84 (`W0` `0x8188`, `+0x4C` = 20: N+15 as 17.3 predicted) and the restart in the pass beginning at k = 88 (N+16): A 88 to 104 (16 blanks), B 104 to 119 (15), C1 to C5 119 to 179 (12, 11, 13, 11, 13), C6 from 179. From the restart pass to the first simulated step: **91 blanks** (7 passes without simulation, then the step). The view stays external through the restart (`V:4A83D` was `0xFF` in every sample to k = 260): `0x1D538` does not reset it.

**Rules.**

1. **The wait state's test and the views** [emu, read]. Every pass `0x138D8` first tests the view selector `V:4A83D` **as the previous pass left it**: in the cockpit view it restarts when `V:4A951` is 0 and waits otherwise; in the external view it restarts when the player's `W0` bit `0x0400` is clear and waits otherwise. `V:4A951` is 0 whenever the hand-over did not set it (external view) and after the hold. Consequences, each captured: (a) **external crash, then KP-ENTER back to the cockpit while dying**: the key is polled at the start of a pass, the state routine of that pass still sees the external view, the toggle is applied a blank later (`V:4A83D` = 0 at k = 33 in the capture, the key pressed at k = 30) and the **next pass restarts at once** (k = 41 against k = 88 without the key): nothing else is reset; the restart's `W0` = `0x11C8` removes the dying bit; (b) **cockpit crash, then a view key**: the key can only be applied before the hand-over if it was polled in pass N, and then the crash itself happens in the external view (the toggle is before the world update of the same pass; [read] from the order of the pass, not captured: the key of the capture arrived during N+1); a key polled at the start of N+1 is applied after that pass's dispatcher, so the hand-over still takes the cockpit branch (hold of 80 blanks, capture `crash-keys-view-before-handover.txt`: the view is `0xFF` from k = 9, the hold and the restart are unchanged, k = 99); a key pressed during the hold is held back (rule 6) and applied in pass A after its dispatcher: the restart happens anyway (the cockpit test `V:4A951` = 0 passes, and the external test `W0` `0x0400` is clear in a cockpit crash, which never finalises), the view is external from then on. A view toggle never cancels, shortens or lengthens a hold.
2. **The restart pass** [emu, read]. The pass whose dispatcher calls `0x1D538` (A) is a full pass: it also runs the world update and the display-list rebuild and increments `n` at its end, 14 blanks (cockpit) or 16 (external) after the rewrite of the record. The 01b "record rewrite at blank 1188, counter turn at 1201" is the start and the end of this one pass. `V:4A984` bit 6 is **clear** during it and the six passes that follow (rule 3), and the player step is not inert for that: with bit 6 clear the step of section 1 (items b to j, 17.2) skips the input conversion, the warnings and the speed step and **runs** the attitude step (unless `W0` bit `0x8000` is set; it is clear at the start of A, `0x1D538` wrote `0x11C8`), the motion step with D = 0 (nothing moves: the speed words are 0), the position update with D = 0 (`W0` bit `0x1000` is set) with its **cell refresh**, the gear and hook counters, the **collision phase** with the previous position equal to the position, the result handling or the tail, and the camera trail. Nothing moves in the seven passes, the first simulated step is the eighth pass counted from A.

   *The record in pass A, staged* [emu]: (a) right after `0x1D538`: `W0` `0x11C8`, `W2` `0x0080`, cell words `0x41/0x42`, `+0x0A` = 05 (the crash cell's sub-index, which `0x1D538` does not write) and `+0x0B` = 06 (the direction index it writes); (b) one blank later, after A's world update: `W0` `0x91C8` (`0x91D8` for the blank of the list request `0xFC`: bit `0x0010` is the list builder's one-blank mark, present in every restart pass, not a collision effect), `+0x0A` = 06 (the cell refresh: the runway cell's sub-index differs from the crash cell's) and `+0x0B` = `0x0F` (the neighbour rule of 17.10: `+0x0A` changed and the cell is within 2 of the viewed cell); (c) pass B's `0x1D538` writes `W0` `0x11C8` and `+0x0B` = 06 again (`+0x0A` stays 06), `W2` `0x0082` (B's step tail then returns W2 to `0x0080` on a runway restart, `V:4A957` = 0, and keeps `0x0082` on the carrier); from B on the collision phase returns no result and the cell refresh changes nothing (cell and sub-index unchanged), so `W0` stays `0x11C8` and `+0x0B` stays 06 through the first simulated step (captured to k = 190 in the cockpit run, k = 260 in the external run). The first A sample of `crash-flow-external-to-cockpit`, `crash-keys-view-before-handover`, `crash-keys-view-in-hold` and `crash-keys-pause-in-hold` shows (a); the cockpit and external runs and the others already show (b) there, because the sample falls after the step.

   *Pass A's collision result* [emu, read]: the `0x8000` of (b) is a result of the collision phase, **result `0x10`, the three-vertex ground test** (17.12): with the player's `+0x7B` forced to `0xFF` in the last blank of the hold (`crash-pass-a-p7b.txt`, `poke`) the first 32 record bytes after A are byte-identical to the unforced run except that `W0` stays `0x11C8` (and `+0x0A`/`+0x0B` still reach 06/0F: the refresh is independent of the result). The test needs `+0x7B` non-negative, which the crash left at 0; the draw of A turns it `0xFF` (about 10 blanks after the rewrite in the cockpit view; in the external view it stays 0 through the whole restart). That pass B returns no result although the external view leaves the gate open (`+0x7B` = 0) points to the vertex slots of the last draw before the hold (the crash attitude, a vertex below the ground) as what A's test reads, and to A's draw replacing them (inferred; the slots are written by the draw, 04a section 8a). The result has no other visible effect: `W0` bit `0x0200` and the statistic need a speed above `0x3C0` (the speed is 0), and the position is kept. In a paused A (the pause key queued in the hold, rule 6) the player step does not run: `W0` stays `0x11C8` and `+0x0A`/`+0x0B` stay 05/06 until the pause ends (captured, `crash-keys-pause-in-hold`).

   What is on screen: the last hold frame stays up through the whole pass; the first new picture (the runway scene) is presented by the flip routine two blanks after the counter turned at the end of A (k = 115 against the turn at 113; the dump at k = 104 to 114 is the old frame, 115 on the new one), so the crash frame is shown about 16 blanks after the rewrite. At the end of the hold the screen shows one uniform orange frame, one blank long (rule 8). The gate `V:4A84D` is 1 throughout; the only writer on this path is the game-over branch of `0x138D8` (rule 7, clears it).
3. **Names and durations** [emu, read]. 0x12EFE is the flight state and has the crash branch (the "hand-over"); there is no state at `0x12FB8`. The hold is the flip routine's loop at `0x182EC`-`0x18372`: 40 iterations, each 2 blanks (`V:4A951` falls by 1 per iteration: 40 decrements with 39 intervals of 2 blanks, 78 blanks from the first fall (39) to 0; measured in seven runs, one iteration took 3 blanks in four of them (k = 44 to 47 in the first), so 78 or 79 from the first fall and 80 (three runs) or 81 (four runs) from the last pre-hold turn to the blank in which `V:4A951` reads 0; the stall runs of section 14 and 01b gave 80; the port constant is 40 iterations of 2 blanks), run inside one call after the frame routine of N+1; the frame routine does not run, so `n`, the state routine, the countdown `V:4AB8E` and the world update stand still. **There is no extra normal step after the hand-over pass** (the earlier "N+2": 01b's second counter turn is the end of N+1 itself): `+0x4C` of the player falls by exactly one between the first `0x0200` sample and the hold, and by one more in A. `0x13980` takes one pass, `0x139C2` six (five waits and the pass that sets bit 6); the countdown is decremented once per pass at `0x11942` before the state routine, whatever the state, and `V:4AB8E` is 5 at `0x13980`'s end.
4. **What is re-set, what survives** [rig, emu, read]. The simulation switch `V:4A984` bit 6 is set again only by `0x139C2`. Bit `0x0800` of `V:4A984` is set once at mission start (`0x12CE6`) and **no routine clears it**: nothing re-sets it, it survives (`0x0840` after the restart, `0x0C40` once the HUD code sets bit `0x0400`). `V:4A982` bit 8 (armed for landing complete) is cleared by the hand-over and set again only by the next lift-off (`0x17242`, section 7): it stays 0 through the restart (capture) and the first take-off arms it. `V:4A984` bit 0 (message latch) is cleared by `0x138D8` (`0x0C01` to `0x0C00` at the rewrite) together with bits 13 and 14. Also seen, with no flight rule behind it: bit `0x0400` (set by the HUD code in the cockpit view) is cleared one blank after the start of C1 (`0x0C00` to `0x0800` at k = 127; the writer is not traced and is not `0x138D8`, which clears bits 0, 13 and 14 only) and set again by the HUD code after C6; in the external run it is never set; and in the external run bit 0 falls during the dying wait (`0x0801` to `0x0800` at k = 55: the message generator, before the restart). **What `0x1D538` writes** (everything else survives; `0x1D4E2` additionally writes the first lines of the table's first block, rule 11):

| group | written by `0x1D538` (runway start unless stated) |
| --- | --- |
| `W0` | exactly `0x11C8` |
| `W2` | bits `0xB108` cleared (`0x8000` hook down, `0x2000`, `0x1000`, `0x0100`, `0x0008`), `0x0080` set; **every other bit survives** (hook engaged `0x4000`, idle detent `0x0400`, `0x0040`, `0x0020`, `0x0010`, `0x0004`, `0x0002`): rig from `0xFFFF`: `0x4EF7`; a carrier start does not touch `W2` again |
| B4 | `B4 & 0x35` (the carrier start then sets `0xC8`); `B20` bits 0 to 2 and `+0x21` bit 0 cleared |
| position and cell | `+0x06`, `+0x08`, `+0x0C`, `+0x0E`, `+0x10`, `+0x14`, `+0x18`, `+0x1C`; the runway start also `+0x0B` = n (the direction index) and `V:4AA00..V:4AA07`, `V:4AB1A..V:4AB25` (the viewed cell and the view offsets), **not** `+0x0A`; the carrier start writes `+0x0A` and `+0x0B` from the position (rule 5, 17.18 f) |
| attitude | `+0x66`, `+0x68`, `+0x6A` and both matrices `+0x80..+0xA3`: **pitch 0, roll 0** and the block's heading for a runway start (so a restart after a nose-down crash is level), the carrier record's three words for a carrier start (rig: from a nonzero attitude to `0, 2240, 0`; the carrier `0, 28600, 0` in the demo snapshot) |
| motion | `+0x3E`, `+0x42`, `+0x46`, `+0x50`, `+0x54`, `+0x56`, `+0x5A` cleared; `+0x6C`, `+0x6E`, `+0x78`, `+0x65` cleared; `+0x2B` = 9; `+0x7C` = 0; `+0x7E` = `0x1400`; `+0x7D` = the model's unit shift (the high nibble becomes 0); `+0x62` = `V:4A901`; `+0x63` = `0x0D`; `+0x71` = `0xFF`; `V:4AC08`, `V:4AC0C` cleared; carrier start also `+0xB8` word cleared |
| refuel and re-arm | `+0x72` = `0x61A800` (**the fuel is refilled**: section 15 said it was not touched), `+0x5F` = `0x24`, `+0x60` word = 500, `V:4A8FB` = `V:4A8FC` = 3, `V:4A904` = `V:4A905` = 16, `V:4A943`, `V:4A944`, `V:4A945`, `V:4A97A` word = 0, records 1 to 3 cleared over their first 164 bytes (the seat and the shot records); the same set the landing-complete event writes (section 7) |
| variables | `V:4A830` = `V:4A834` = 72 (P, Q: T = 9), `V:4A858` = `V:4A863` = `V:4A8FA` = `V:4A8FF` = `V:4A920` = `V:4A927` = `V:4A928` = 0 (**the throttle preset is cleared**), `V:4A890` = 1, `V:4A910` = `0xFF`, `V:4A941` = `V:4A969` = `V:4A96B` = `V:4A96D` = 0, `V:4A951` = `V:4A952` = 0, `V:4A968` = `0xFE`, `V:4A850` = 4 when it was non-zero, `V:4AA78` word = `0xFFFF`, `V:4AA7A` word = 0 (**the ejection latch is reset**), `V:4AB96` and **`V:4AB98` word = 0 (the message line is emptied)**, `V:4AB9C` word = `0xFFFF`, `V:4ABFA` word = `0x7FFF` |

   The state routines add: `0x138D8`: `V:4A984` AND `0x9FFE`, `V:4A959` = `0xF`, `V:4A941` = 0, `V:4A98C` bit `0x0400` cleared, `V:4A94F` minus 1; `0x13980`: `V:4A952` = 0, `W2` bit 1 set, `0x1D538` again, `V:4AB8E` = 5; `0x139C2`: `V:4A984` bit 6 set, `V:4A968` = `0xFE`. **Survive** (in particular, as asked): `+0x7B`, `V:4A963`, `V:4A982` (except bit 8 by the hand-over), `V:4A98A`, `V:4A903`, `V:4A957`, `V:4A9FE`, `V:4A84F`, `V:4A8FE`, `V:4A994`, `V:4A996`, `V:4A83D`, `V:4A844`, `V:4A866`, `V:4A923`, the commands `+0x28..+0x2A`, `+0x39`, `+0x4C`, `+0x4E`, `+0x22..+0x26`, the counter `n` and the mission variables. `V:4A968` is **written** (twice), not preserved. The runway start `0x1D4E2` (new game, not the restart) writes beyond `0x1D538`: `V:4A83D` = 0, `V:4A85E` = 0, `V:4A86D` = `V:4A86C` = 1 and the recorder's buffer pointers `0x54E4C`, `0x54E54`, `0x54E5C`, `0x54E58`, and clears `V:4A9E4`, `V:4A9E8`.
5. **Carrier placement** [rig, emu]. The carrier is placed by the mission-start routine's call of `0x2CE36` (`0x120CC`, and again for some modes), **never by the start routine or the restart**: the carrier record stays where it was put. `0x2CE36` reads the table at `0x2DEDA`: 12-byte rows by the game mode `V:4A95E` (mode minus 1 for modes 1 to 9; 0x7E and 0x7F use row 10; 0x7D is a separate routine at `0x2CFDA`), three word pairs per row (offset of a list from the table, a small count that goes to `V:4A962`) chosen by the selector `V:4ABB0 & 3` (0 and 1 give variant 0; 2 and 3 give 1 and 2; with `V:4A8E3` non-zero the stored variant `V:4ABD4` is reused), then a sub-list chosen by one byte per mode in the structure `V:1D024` points to (+0x12 + mode; all zero in the captured states). The sub-list entries are 10-byte items (template offset into `0x25ECC`, a class word, the record index in the low 7 bits with `0x8000` as a flag, further words); the position of the carrier is computed by the placement engine (`0x2D248`, `0x2CF14`), not stored as a triple. **Free flight: modes 1 and 9 use the same list (`0x2E53C`, whose four sub-lists differ only in one word, `0x42` to `0x48`); the carrier is record 14 at (295,174,144, 0, 274,202,624), attitude (0, 0, 0), class `0x20`, `W0` `0x0148`, whatever the selector, the variant and the sub-list (rig: all 4 x 4 combinations, 0 differences; the cockpit-ground, airborne and cockpit-carrier snapshots read this position, attitude and cell (70, 65); their `W0` differs from the placement's `0x0148`: `0x405C` (airborne, cockpit-ground) and `0x404C` with `W2` `0x0001` (cockpit-carrier), bits set later by the running game)**. A runway start and a carrier start of free flight therefore share the carrier's position; they differ only in where the player goes: runway start `V:4A900` = 0 (the block of 17.14), carrier start `V:4A900` = 3: entry `0x47B0E`, player = carrier + `((11 M0 + 104 M2) >> 4, (11 M6 + 104 M8) >> 4)` = (+11,264, +106,496) at heading 0, y = 30,472, attitude copied (captured: carrier (295,174,144, 274,202,624), player (295,185,408, 30,472, 274,309,120), `W0` `0x11C8`). **The position (286,785,536, 286,785,536) with heading 28,600 is what `0x2CE36` places for modes 0x7E and 0x7F (rig) and what the demonstration snapshot holds** (with `V:4A95E` = 3 at the instant of the snapshot: the demonstration changes the mode between its parts, not examined), not free flight's: 17.14's "carrier start of free flight (04a): 286785536" was wrong, the 04a deck state's cell (70, 65) is the free-flight carrier's. Other modes (rig, for the placement alone): 2 adds record 4 (an aircraft, class `0x10`) at (307,757,056, 328,728,576) with the carrier unchanged; 3 and 4 add records 4 or 8 (class `0x12` to `0x16`) by variant, with the carrier at heading 3920 in mode 3; 0x7D places one record only, an aircraft at (273,237,745, 280,545,007) heading 2256, and no carrier (0x7D is the allowance-1 mode, rule 7). If record 14 is inactive at a carrier restart (the player's `V:4A900` = 3), `0x1D730` clears `V:4A900` and the start becomes the runway block 0.
6. **Keys, pause and the pointer during the hold** [emu]. The key poll is in the frame routine, but the keys are **not lost**: the system's keyboard handling queues the events and the poll of pass A delivers them. Captures: KP-ENTER pressed at k = 40 (inside the hold) changes `V:4A83D` at k = 97, the blank the hold ends, in pass A after its dispatcher (the key's make and break are both delivered, the toggle happens once); the pause key `P` (raw `0x19`, handler `0x1ED9A`: it stops the sounds and sets `V:4A866` to `0xFF`) pressed at k = 40 sets `V:4A866` at k = 97 and A still restarts: **the pause does not stop the state routines**: the resume states run on (`0x139C2` set bit 6 at k = 171 as without the pause), the counter `n` stands still (no increment while paused), the first `0x12EFE` pass then takes its pause branch (state `0x13B76`, the pause state; a second `P` at k = 200 returned to `0x12EFE`). The hold itself is never shortened or extended by a key. So in the port: events that arrive during the hold are queued and applied at the start of pass A in arrival order, before the dispatcher; a pause among them takes effect from pass A on, and the restart still completes (the player step of A and of every later pass does not run while paused, so the record stays as `0x1D538` left it: rule 2). The pointer: the vertical-blank handler (`0x192DE`, a VERTB interrupt server) runs during the hold and during a pause and changes `V:4A830` (capture: 960, then 860 and 910 in the hold as the mouse was moved), but `0x1D538` sets P = Q = 72 in pass A and again in B, so **only pointer movement after pass B counts**.
7. **The crash allowance** [emu, read]. `V:4A94F` is set at mission start: 3 as the default (`0x120BC`), 5 for mode 1 (**free flight**, `0x1217A`) and 1 for mode 0x7D (`0x12202`); the snapshots read 5 in the airborne, cockpit-ground and cockpit-carrier states (mode 1) and 3 in the demo (mode 3). Each crash decrements it in `0x138D8` after `0x1D538`; at 0 the state becomes the game-over path: free flight **can** reach it, with the **fifth** crash (captured twice with the allowance set to 1 by `poke`: `crash-flow-gameover.txt` and, with the gate and the countdown peeked per blank, `crash-gameover-gate.txt`; k0 is the blank in which `V:4A951` first reads 0) [emu]: at k0 the wait state runs `0x1D538` (record rewritten, `V:4A94F` 1 to 0, `V:4A910` = `0xFF`, `V:4A984` `0x0C01` to `0x0C00`) but the allowance-0 branch stores **no new state** for 4 blanks and **the counter does not turn**: the pointer still reads `0x138D8` from k0 to k0 + 3, no world update runs (the record keeps the `0x1D538` result: `W0` `0x11C8`, `+0x0A`/`+0x0B` = 05/06, no `0x8000`, no cell refresh) and `V:4A84D` is still 1; at k0 + 4 the pointer reads `0x139F0`, **`V:4A84D` = 0** (the gate of the flight path; it stays 0 to the end of the capture, k0 + 133) and `V:4AB8E` = 5; at k0 + 5 the pointer reads `0x13A36`, `V:4AB8E` = -1 and the text id `V:4A802` = `0x63` (`0x62` when `V:4A901` is `0x10`, the F-16), typed out (frames at k0 + 34 and k0 + 64 show text on a black screen); `V:4A898` falls from `0x81` to 0 at k0 + 6, reads 1 at k0 + 84 and `0xFF` at k0 + 92, where the pointer reads `0x11A70` with `n` = 0 and `V:4A984` = 0, and `V:4A802` reads 0 from k0 + 84; between k0 + 5 and k0 + 92 `V:4AB8E` falls by 0 to 6 per blank and `n` stays 0x19D. The writers of `V:4A84D`, `V:4A898` and `V:4AB8E` on this path are not traced.)) **Under a pause** [emu, `followup-pause-gameover.txt`, 3 runs of the same dive with the allowance set to 1; index 0 is the first blank with the crash bit, the end of the hold is index 97 and the gate falls at index 101 (blank 103 in two runs: the pass lengths vary by 1 to 3 blanks)]: (a) P pressed during the hold (index 1 and 20) is queued and polled by the next pass, which is the end of the hold in the second case (the pause byte reads `0xFF` from index 9, or from index 99); the hold is not shortened or extended (the allowance falls to 0 at the same blank, the gate at index 100 to 103 as unpaused). (b) The game-over states do not read the pause byte: with the byte `0xFF` from index 9 to 151 (run A) or from 107 to 161 (run C) the countdown falls 5 to -1 in one blank at the gate and then at the unpaused rates (3 to 6 passes per blank), the text id is stored one pass later, the text is typed in 78 blanks (78 in run A; 80 in run C, whose pause began in the typing: the same within the jitter), the end hold and the reset follow at the unpaused blanks (`V:4A898` = 1 at index 181 and 185, the reset state at 189 and 194). (c) The step counter does not turn (`V:4A992` unchanged) and **no pause screen opens**: the flight state that would open it is not the current state; the pause byte only stays `0xFF` until a second P clears it (index 151 and 161; the reset block clears it otherwise, 08 5.11). So **rule 7 holds under a pause: the game-over timers (the wait states, the text engine's pass counts, the end hold) run exactly as unpaused, because they are counted in passes and the pause byte stops only the step counter and the player step; P changes nothing but the byte**. In steps: the same 20 steps from the restart pass (08 5.12). **The resume states under a pause** [emu, the old `crash-keys-pause-in-hold.txt` against `crash-flow-cockpit.txt`, re-read]: the six passes of the resume wait state take 60 blanks with the pause (blank 111 to 171, the byte `0xFF`, the gate 1, the counter standing still) against 63 blanks unpaused (113 to 176, the counter turning 6 times): a paused pass is **as long as an unpaused one** (about 10 blanks: the scene is still drawn with the gate at 1), so the pause neither shortens nor lengthens the resume: the port keeps the same seven passes, one step each (iii), paused or not; there is no faster pass rate under a pause. The text ids and the screen are note 08's. In the port: free flight starts with 5; the fifth crash ends the flight.
8. **The explosion effect, the flip routine and the flash** [read; emu]. `0x1A0DC(0x3F, 0x78)` is a **sound**: it starts the crash sample when the sound system is running (`V:4AC12` bit 0) and otherwise posts the notice `0xFA` with `V:4A84F` = 2 (section 11); the hand-over first stops the four channels (`0x115F6`). The flip routine in the hold [read]: each iteration makes two library calls, with the palette block at `0x29F64` (32 words, all `0x0FB2`, a uniform orange) and with the game palette (pointer `V:4A718`), and waits for the display after each. **Captured per blank** (`crash-hold-palette.txt`: Denise's 32 colour registers on every blank of the hold; `crash-hold-flash.txt`: the frame and the registers on 8 consecutive blanks in the middle of the hold and on 14 around its end) [emu]: the registers read the orange block on every second blank, once per iteration (40 times, on the blanks between the start of the hold and the one before `V:4A951` reads 0), and the game palette on the others, **yet the frame is the normal cockpit picture with the crash message in every blank of the hold except one**: all frames from the 8 mid-hold blanks and the first 6 of the closing blanks are byte-identical, and the single exception, the frame presented in the blank in which `V:4A951` first reads 0, is a uniform orange screen (identical to the dump of the earlier run) and is followed by a normal frame in the next blank. **Rule: the player sees one orange frame, one 50 Hz blank (20 ms), at the very end of the hold; the 39 earlier palette loads change no visible pixel.** Why the earlier loads are invisible and the last is not is not decoded (a colour-register timing question inside the display flip; the frames are the emulator's completed frames), so the port reproduces the observed frames, not the loads. The game's own drawing does not run in the hold. This is milestone 5. **With an ejection nibble** (`+0x7C` low nibble non-zero) the hand-over branch is **skipped** (the test at `0x12F18`): `0x12EFE` continues with its other duties, `W0` `0x0200` stays set, and the hand-over runs in the first pass in which the nibble is 0 (the cycle ends when `W2` bit `0x0020` is set, section 8); the view test of rule 1 then uses the view at that time (after the seat spawn in the cockpit view `V:4A994` = 1 and the seat's view is external). Read only; an attempt to stage it by writing the nibble into the dive snapshot let the nibble run down in 7 blanks (no seat spawn without the key handler) and gave an ordinary crash.
9. **The RETURN key, not ESC** [read, emu]. The 0x1E8A6 handler with the post of `0x401F` (note 06, entry 31 "not ready to reposition") is reached by the key **RETURN** (raw `0x44`, with the key state `V:4A966` = 0) or the fire bit that the input queue word `V:4AA4E` delivers (bit 4) [read]; **ESC** (raw `0x45`) goes to `0x1EF50`, which sets `V:4A8E2` = 1 (-1 with the shift state `V:4A930`): the leave-the-flight request (note 08); captured in the air (`crash-esc-key.txt`; k counts blanks from the key press) [emu]: the byte is consumed within 3 blanks; at k = 3 the pointer reads `0x11A96`, `V:4A866` = 1 and `V:4A84D` = 0: the world is frozen and **the cockpit picture stays and fades out** (one level per 2 blanks, the screen is black only when level 2 is reached about 30 blanks after the key, note 08 5.11; the earlier "the cockpit disappears" was a frame taken with the gate clear); `V:4A84D` reads 1 again from k = 37 to 41 (pointer `0x11AE2`, `V:4A866` still 1); at k = 42 `n` is cleared, `V:4A866` is 0 and `V:4A84D` 0 again; the pointer then runs through `0x11E04` (k = 49) and `0x1201E` (k = 55, text id `0x66`); from k = 87 (pointer `0x1234C`) `V:4A84D` is 1 for good and `n` counts again from 1 at k = 105. The screens are note 08's; no catapult is involved. The handler's conditions: game mode `V:4A95E` = 2, or 0x7D with `V:4A852` negative: a sound (`0x37C4A`) and `W2` bit `0x0800` set (the world update `0x27276` consumes the bit: it calls `0x2CE36` again for the scripted entity and sets its throttle to `0x60`); mode 0x7D with `V:4A852` not negative: the post of `0x401F`; **every other mode, in particular free flight (mode 1): the weapon-select cycle** (the high nibble of `+0x63` steps down by one, 3 when it would go below 0, `V:4A8FB` = `V:4A8FC` = 3, `V:4A96C` = 0, unless an ejection nibble is set). Captured: RETURN on the deck and in the air in free flight changes the record by nothing but flight (`+0x63` `0x0D` to `0x3D`, `V:4A8FB` 0 to 3, counting down again). So **no catapult re-position exists in free flight**; the repositioning is a training-mode feature of mode 2 or 0x7D (note 08). Keys during a crash: none of this runs in the hold.
10. **The external restart** [emu]. As above: 91 blanks from the start of the restart pass to the start of the first simulated step in the external view (7 passes of 16, 15, 12, 11, 13, 11 and 13 blanks; passes A and B run the same staged record as the cockpit restart, rule 2), against 76 to 77 in the cockpit view; the restart pass itself is 16 blanks (14 in the cockpit view). The view is not reset. After the restart `+0x4C` (20 after the expiry) keeps counting down with nothing to do.
11. **The restart is `0x1D538`, not `0x1D4E2`** [read, rig]. `0x1D4E2` (new flight: the first 20 lines) additionally clears the view selector, the head request `V:4A85E`, the recorder pointers and `V:4A9E4`/`V:4A9E8`, and falls into `0x1D538`; the crash path enters at `0x1D538` (the instruction after them). Section 15 describes the whole of `0x1D4E2`; the crash restart does not do those lines: the view selector stays, the head request stays (pass B recentres the head in the cockpit view) and the recorder pointers stay.
12. **The pointer range at the stops** [rig, read] (17.4). P = `V:4A830` is a signed word limited to -960..960 by the mouse handler; throttle = P >> 3 for P from 8 to 959 (120 at 960); **P below 0 is the idle request** (rule 2 of 17.4: P is set to 0 and the preset to -1 in the same step), P from 1 to 7 fires a preset of **0 = none** (the preset handler returns at once for a zero byte: rig, P = 5, T stays 9) and P = 0 changes nothing. A unipolar lever (0 to 960) cannot send the idle request; map the lever's bottom stop to a negative P (-960 as the original's bottom stop) and the rest of its travel to 8 to 960.

*Consequences for the port (what changes Task 6's code).* (i) Remove the extra step after the hand-over: the hold begins at the end of the hand-over pass. (ii) After the hold run **seven passes without simulation** (`V:4A984` bit 6 clear: nothing moves, but the player step still runs its attitude step, the cell refresh and the collision phase, rule 2 and consequence (xi)) and set bit 6 in the eighth; `0x1D538`'s writes are applied in pass A and again in pass B. (iii) The hold lasts 80 blanks (1.6 s, counted on the 50 Hz clock, not in port steps; the port's fixed-step model rounds the 80-blank hold to 13 steps, 81.25 blanks, an accepted error of 1.25 blanks, controller ruling); the seven passes without simulation last 76 to 77 blanks in the cockpit view and 91 in the external view in the original, because each is a frame-routine pass bound by the scene's cost (8 to 16 blanks); the port counts them as **7 port steps** (0.875 s at 8 Hz), not as a time converted from the original's blanks. (iv) Keys, pause and view toggles pressed in the hold are queued and applied in pass A, not dropped; the pause does not stop the resume states. (v) `0x1D538` does not reset the view; a view switch to the cockpit while dying restarts the next pass; the toggle is applied after the dispatcher. (vi) Re-set rule: `V:4A984` bit 6 by `0x139C2`; `V:4A982` bit 8 by the next lift-off; bit `0x0800` is never cleared. (vii) The write list of rule 4, in particular the fuel, the message line, the throttle preset, the ejection latch, records 1 to 3 and the pitch and roll. (viii) The free-flight allowance is 5 and the fifth crash is the game-over path. (ix) The carrier of free flight is at (295,174,144, 274,202,624), heading 0, for both start kinds. (x) RETURN is the weapon key in free flight; there is no re-position. (xi) **Pass A's world update** (rule 2): in A and the six passes after it the player step still runs its attitude step, a motion step with D = 0, the cell refresh, the gear counters and the collision phase; so after A the record reads `W0` `0x91C8` (the ground test's `0x8000` while `+0x7B` is non-negative, which holds in A in both views; `0x0200` stays clear), `+0x0A` = 06 and `+0x0B` = `0x0F`; B's `0x1D538` restores `W0` `0x11C8` and `+0x0B` = 06 and sets `W2` bit `0x0002` (A wrote `0x0080`, B writes `0x0082`; B's step tail returns W2 to `0x0080` on a runway restart, `V:4A957` = 0, and keeps `0x0082` on the carrier), and from B on no collision result occurs. A's ground test reads the vertex slots of the last draw before the hold (the crash attitude; that this is the cause is inferred), so `0x8000` is set in A in both views, and the port reproduces it and clears it in B: nothing else reads it (A's attitude step has already run, B starts from `0x11C8`). With a pause applied from A on, none of this runs. (xii) **The flash is one frame**: the cockpit picture with the crash message stays on screen for the whole hold and a single uniform `0x0FB2` frame (one 50 Hz blank) ends it; do not flash every iteration. (xiii) **Game over** (the fifth crash in free flight): pass A of the last crash rewrites the record but runs no world update, stores no state for 4 blanks and does not turn `n`; the gate `V:4A84D` is 0 from k0 + 4 on, until the mission screen. (xiv) A paused restart leaves the record as `0x1D538` wrote it.

#### 17.18 The Task 5 re-review answers (the composition lookup, the idle shortcut, the gear counter, the throttle) [rig, read]

a. **The composition's heading and roll lookups (`0x325B8`) and the exact read range** [rig, read]. Heading and roll are extracted from the product matrix by quotients of a matrix element by a sine or cosine word d: for d of `0x147` or below the quotient is the element divided by 64 d, without a rounding step; for a larger d it is the element shifted right by 6 divided by d, and the 16-bit quotient q is then moved away from 0 by 1 when the remainder reaches half of d. If the quotient does not fit in 16 bits the divide leaves the dividend and the code goes on with the low word of it. The table index is the **doubled quotient in 16 bits**: the doubling is a 16-bit add, the following sign test uses the **true** sign of 2q (the negative and overflow flags together, so a doubling that overflows is not seen as the sign of the word) and a 16-bit negation serves the other sign; the net rule, derived for the sign and overflow cases of both lookups and checked in the rig, is: **the byte offset into the table `0x42E92` is the signed 16-bit value of 2|q|** (|q| = 32768 gives 0), not 2|q| in 32 bits and not the absolute value taken after the doubling. So for |q| up to 16383 the offset is 2|q| (0 to 32,766, an even number); for |q| from 16384 to 32767 it is **negative**, -32,768 to -2: the lookup reads **below** the table, from `0x3AE92` to `0x42E91`. The earlier statement (17.9) that these paths read only `0x42E92..0x4AE92` with the absolute value taken first is **wrong**. The first lookup is used when the signed 16-bit value of 2|q| is at most `0x180` (|q| up to 192, or 16384 and more); otherwise a second lookup (the other matrix element's quotient q2) follows with the same offset rule. The pitch path clamps its index (to `0x225` in the middle table, `0xF8` in the third, and below 227 in the first) and reads only inside the three inverse-sine tables (`0x42E92..0x436D9`, observed); the sine lookup `0x436E8 + 2 d` reads up to `0x43DF1` (observed; d is a table value of at most `0x384`). **Read range of the composition**: `0x3AE92` to `0x4AE91` (word reads; observed over 3,000 random product matrices with random angle words: lowest byte `0x3AE92`, highest `0x4AE85`). Rig check: for each q from -32768 to 32767 the first lookup, and for each q2 the second lookup (the first quotient fixed at 1000, the divisor `0x148`, the dividends exact multiples of it so that the quotient is the wanted value and the rounding step leaves it), the address read equals `0x42E92` plus the signed 16-bit value of 2|q| (first lookup: all 65,536 values of q, 33,154 of which reach the first lookup, 0 mismatches; second lookup: all 65,536 values of q2, 0 mismatches). The extractor's span 2 must therefore start at `0x3AE92` and not at `0x4130A` (the matrix builders alone read from `0x4130A`); the bytes `0x3AE92..0x4130A` are code and data of hunks 43 to 57 and contain **145 relocated longs** (192 in the old span; 337 in the new one), which follow the same reference-machine value rule as 17.9, with the caveat that the value is the RAM base of the **target hunk of the relocation entry** plus the stored offset, not of the hunk inferred from the flat address of the stored value (they differ for a pointer to the end of a hunk). The span becomes `0x3AE92..0x49A12` (60,288 bytes; the reads above `0x49A12` keep the run-time answer-0 rule).
b. **The idle shortcut keeps the stored displacement** [rig]. For a record with neither `W0` bit `0x1000` nor `0x0100` in the external view (17.2), including a dying one with `+0x4C` above 0 (adversarial sequences 223, 290, 421 of the run of 3000: `W0` `0x86C8` and `0x0EC8`, view external, `+0x4C` 15): the shortcut **is** taken (the position does not move: rig, W0 `0x86C8`, `0x0EC8`, `0x84C8`, `0x06C8`, `0x85C8` with `+0x4C` 15 and 5) but the stored displacement words `+0x3E`, `+0x42` and `+0x46` are **left as they were**: the "D = 0" of 17.2 is only the value used for the position update and the collision's previous position, the record is not written. The port zeroed the words; the original keeps them. (The step does not run the motion step: no velocity is produced; the observed "velocity" in the log is the old one.)
c. **The gear counter's `W2` bit `0x0020`** [rig, read]. The two halves of `+0x7C` are processed in this order: the low nibble L first: for L from 1 to 5 it rises by 1; at 6 it rises to 7 and, if `W2` bit `0x0020` is set, becomes 0 **and the bit is cleared**, else stays 6; 7 becomes 1; above 7 it falls by 1 (8, 9, 10: 7 then restarts at 1 as 17.10 said); then the high part (retract progress): when it changes, the byte is stored with the low nibble **0**. So the bit is cleared whenever L was 6 with the bit set, **whether or not the progress changed in the same step**, and for no other nibble (rig: gear `0x06`, `0x86`, `0x76`, `0x16` with the bit set: bit cleared; `0x05`, `0x85`, `0x0A`, `0x8A`, `0x07`, `0x87`, `0x60`: bit kept). Port divergence: it skipped the clear when the progress changed.
d. **The throttle notices and `V:4A994`** [rig]. The notices `0xFE` (throttle moved from zero, also the pointer's fire path), `0xFD` and `0xFC` (afterburner, with `V:4A84F` = 6 for `0xFD`) are raised only when the **viewed entity index `V:4A994` equals the index of the entity being stepped** (0 for the player): the test compares the word `V:4A994` with `V:4AA6C`; `V:4A996` is not tested (rig: `V:4A994` = 0 with `V:4A996` = `0x0200` raises the notice; `V:4A994` = 1 with `V:4A996` = 0 does not).
e. **The follow rule's key condition and the zero preset** [rig, read]. The pointer's follow rule (17.4, rule 1) runs when the low two bits of the key byte `+0x65` are non-zero **at entry** to the throttle routine `0x15ED4` (the byte as the input conversion left it, including the bits the preset handling set; the routine's own clearing of the bits in the military-power detent comes after the test and does not undo the follow); "after the key logic" in 17.4 was wrong in that it let the detent's clearing count. A pointer height from 1 to 7 fires a preset of 0, which the preset handler treats as **no preset** (rule 12): no key bits follow and the throttle stays.
f. **The carrier start's `+0x0A` and `+0x0B`** [rig]. The runway start writes `+0x0B` = n (the direction index) and leaves `+0x0A` alone; the **carrier start writes both** from the new position: `+0x0A` = 4 (3 - (fine z >> 12)) + (3 - (fine x >> 12)) of the 14-bit fine words, exactly the step's formula, and `+0x0B` = 4 (3 - (cell z & 3)) + (3 - (cell x & 3)) of the cell words `+0x08`, `+0x06` (rig, demo carrier at cell (68, 68): `+0x0A` = 10, `+0x0B` = 15; the free-flight carrier's cell (70, 65) gives 9). The step's `+0x0B` = `0x0F` rule (17.10) rewrites it at the first sub-cell change.
g. **The coupling's `W0` bit `0x8000` branch (C11) is dead code** [read, rig]. The coupling `0x32426` is called only from the attitude step `0x31AE0` (for aircraft: always for the other entities, for the player when `V:4A847` is set), and `0x31AE0` is entered from the player step only when `W0` bit `0x8000` is **clear** (class `0x30` entities excepted, which do not use the coupling), nothing between the test and the call sets the bit, and there is no other caller. So the branch that sets `V:4A9FE` = 0 cannot be reached: with `W0` bit `0x8000` set the step leaves `V:4A9FE` unchanged, which is what the port does. Section 6 item 2's wording "only with ... `W0` bit `0x8000` clear" is the whole rule.

## Constants

All values hexadecimal where written `0x`, otherwise decimal; the address is the routine the constant was read from.

| constant | value | meaning | where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| throttle limits | 120 (`0x78`), -32 | military power byte; reverse limit; initial T = 9 | `0x15ED4`, `0x1D858` |
| throttle repeat | step = `(R + 1) >> 1`, R up to 8 | keys held longer move T faster (0 to 4 per step) | `0x15ED4` |
| catapult release | `0x6C` (108) | throttle needed while B4 bit `0x08` is set | `0x15ED4` |
| fuel burn | `abs(T) / 2` per step; `0x600` afterburner | | `0x15ED4` |
| fuel | `0x61A800` full; warnings below `0xBB800` and `0x70800` | | `0x1F11E`, `0x1557C` |
| afterburner speed limit | `0x3A98 - G`, cap `0x4650`; gear down and brake each minus a quarter | | `0x15ED4` |
| normal speed target | `9 C / 32` air (cap `0x1FEF`), `3 C / 32` ground, minus G, minus a quarter each for brake and gear | `C = 256 T` | `0x15ED4` |
| speed increments | air normal: 5 (within `0x150`) or `0x2A`; afterburner `0x2D`, `0x36` (above or below `0x1D4C`), `0x78` reverse | per step | `0x15ED4` |
| speed decrements | `0x52` above `0x1D4C`, 7 within `0x1E0`, `0x3C` otherwise; idle: `0x1E`, `0x3C`, `0x52`; ground idle 15 | per step | `0x15ED4` |
| ground acceleration | lag toward `-(((Vt - S) >> 8) + 0x16)` (`0x42` with the catapult), -37 with the afterburner, `+0x26` lag 1/8 | | `0x15ED4`, `0x169C6` |
| arrestor, brake, belly, skid | slew thresholds and shifts (7, 3), (15, 4), (31, 5), (3, 2) | | `0x15ED4`, `0x169F2` |
| overspeed | `0x20D0` (700 knots) | warning only | `0x15ED4` |
| drag term G | `2abs(E) + roll/8 - pitch/2 + (ceiling - y) >> 13` | | `0x16A32` |
| ceiling | `0x800000` (40960 ft) | | `V:4A7B2` |
| gust, sink | tables `0x2AB18`, `0x2AAD8`; target `(c * k) >> 3`; lag 8 (above) and 32 (below) | | `0x2AB64` |
| rate response | table `0x425D8` (player), lag 1/4; rate boost beyond +-`0x500` by `S >> 7` | | `0x1557C`, `0x15E2E` |
| ground pitch | settle to +-`0x40` below `0x3840`; settled below pitch `0x190`; rotation needs S > `0x300` | | `0x1557C`, `0x15BDE` |
| roll reset | +-`0x20` | | `0x15CAA` |
| turn coupling | limit `+0x7E` = `0x1400`, `* 3/4`, pitch scale table `0x3259A` | | `0x32426` |
| stall | threshold 1518 level, `(0x4650 - p2) * 108 >> 8`; nose-drop ramp to `0x50` per step, stop near `0x12C0` | | `0x31AE0` |
| gimbal guard | band `0x1770` to `0x5910`, tolerances `0x4B0`, `0x6BD0`, `0x320`, kick `0x90` | | `0x31AE0` |
| displacement per step | `4 * abs(W)`; velocity lag 1/4 (shifts 2, 1, 0 by size 5, 3 and 2) | | `0x16B0E`, `0x17288` |
| floors | `0x708` gear down, `0x2EE` gear up or down-less, `0xA8C` class `0x14`, `-0x200` ground objects; deck + `+0x4E << 8` | | `0x16B0E` |
| touchdown crash | sink >= `0x240` (576) per step over 4; crash speed `0x3C0` (80 knots) for contact crashes | | `0x16B0E`, `0x2A758` |
| deck box | half-size `0xA0000`; arrestor alignment `0xE000000` (0.875) | | `0x2B5E2` |

| gear animation | 6 steps up and down | | `0x2A68A` |
| message ids | `0x9C06`, `0xD02A`, `0xD00A`, `0x9014`, `0xD00B`, `0xD00C`, `0x4005`, `0x4023`, `0x4021` | table `0x41FF0` index = low byte | `0x2A3B2`, `0x2A758`, `0x1F11E` |
| unit conversions | 204.8 position units per foot; 1/12 knot per speed unit; 4 position units per speed unit per step | | note 03, `0x36ADE`, `0x36FB2` |
| landmark z shift | 48 fine units (12288 position units, 60 ft) = (high word of the collision-data address) >> 2 on the reference machine | port constant; the rig test passes 1 | `0x2BC28`-`0x2C08C` |
| flight step rate | 8 steps per second (port default, controller's ruling; the original is per frame, flight-only mean 7.97 on the reference machine) | port constant `FLIGHT_STEP_HZ` | note 01b, section 14 |
| crash hold (cockpit view only) | 40 passes of 2 blanks = 80 blanks = 1.6 s at 50 Hz, then restart pass A (about 14 blanks = 0.28 s) and seven passes without simulation in all (about 77 blanks, 1.5 s) before the first simulated step | port constants `CRASH_HOLD_PASSES` = 40, `CRASH_PASS_BLANKS` = 2 (`CRASH_HOLD_S` = 1.6) | section 14; `0x12F98`, `0x1827C` |
| class test | high nibble of `+0x62` = 1 | speed gate, stall, landmark phase, ground test | `0x2A2A2`, `0x31AE0`, `0x2B5E2` |
| pointer throttle | per blank P = clamp(P - dy, -960, 960) (`0x3C0`), dy the wrapped counter change (-128..127); fire when P >= 960 or abs(P - Q) >= 17 (display mode not 2); follow after a key step when P >= 960 or abs(P - Q) <= 15; with the afterburner on no preset from P = 864 (`0x360`) up; preset P >> 3 | 17.4; `0x15ED4`, `0x19254`, `0x192DE` |
| dying expiry (external view) | `+0x4C` 15 at finalisation, expiry when 0 or less; then `W0` clears `0x0440` (and `0x4000`, `0x2000`), `+0x4C` = 20 | 17.3; `0x2A2E8`-`0x2A360` |
| gimbal kick | 144 (`0x90`), the word at `0x425E4` | 17.7; `0x31AE0` |
| coupling scale over-read | words before the table `0x3259A`: `0x4E75` (index -1, at `0x32598`), 84 (-2), `0x4469` (-3), 80 (-4) | 17.9; `0x32426` |
| matrix builder angle | w >> 3 logical on the 16-bit word, then unguarded fold; sine-table byte offsets from -9182 (first byte read `0x4130A`) to +10982 (last byte read `0x461CF`) | 17.9; `0x32B52`, `0x32BEC` |
| image spans for the over-reads | `0x3249A..0x325B8` (286 bytes), `0x3AE92..0x49A12` (60,288 bytes; start corrected twice, C20 and C38) | 17.9, 17.18 a |
| composition table offset | the signed 16-bit value of 2 x the 16-bit rounded quotient's magnitude, from the table `0x42E92`: reads `0x3AE92` to `0x4AE91` | 17.18 a; `0x325B8` |
| terrain grid index | `(col & 0xFF) + 32 * (row & 0xFF)` words, col = x >> 24, row = z >> 24; x < 0 gives cells 128 to 255 | 17.10; `0x2F77A`-`0x2F78A` |
| ejection seat spawn | at nibble 3, first inactive record of 1 to 3, class `0x30`, offset (0, 3, 20), `+0x4C` = -20, thrust `0x3C0`; latch `V:4AA7A` = record offset; cockpit view only: `V:4A968` = `0xFE`, request table `V:4A8EE`..`V:4A8FD` = 3 | 17.10; `0x26F60`, `0x27522`, `0x276D2`, `0x1EC14`, `0x1EFF4` |
| result handler (player) | nibble: dying path first; `V:4A844`; W above `0x3C0` after the speed step; `V:4A952`; external view finalises (`W0` +`0x0600`, `+0x4C` = 15, `B20` +`0x02`, `0x1000` cleared) | 17.12; `0x2A758`-`0x2A9BA` |
| neighbour index rewritten | `0x0F`, when `+0x0A` changes and the cell is within 2 of `V:4AA04/6` | 17.10; `0x2A63E`-`0x2A686` |
| carrier box | half-size `0xA0000` on x, y, z, inclusive, previous position of the player | 17.11; `0x2B6D2`-`0x2B6FC` |
| landmark slots | 11 (numbers 0..10), queue C walked backwards from `V:4AA7E`, 24 bytes per entry | 17.11; `0x2BC28`-`0x2BD36` |
| cue bits `V:4AC0C` | `0x80` crash/proximity, `0x40` eject, `0x20` stall lamp, `0x200` roll-out | 17.13; `0x2A3F6`, `0x2A448`, `0x2A490`, `0x15B2E`, `0x170F0` |
| start blocks | table `0x47ADE`, 16 bytes per entry; runway position = cell * 2^24 + (dir + offset) * 1024 + fine * 16; heading = degrees * 80; carrier offset (11, 0, 104) * 1024 through the carrier matrix | 17.14; `0x1D640`-`0x1D72C`, `0x1D730`-`0x1D7D0` |
| deck height D (carrier) | 112 (model header `0x3E1A0`, long at +2 without bit 31); start y = (D << 8) + `0x708` = 30,472 | 17.14; `0x1D766` |
| three-vertex ground test | live when `+0x7B` is non-negative with a zero low nibble; vertices 0, 1, 2; y word >> L + `+0x10` below 0 gives result `0x10` | 17.12; `0x2C08C` |

## Open questions

1. **Time base.** Settled by the controller's ruling (section 14): the original runs one step per frame at a scene-dependent 5 to 11 steps per second (flight-only mean 7.97); the port uses a fixed 8. What stays open is only whether a per-scene rate should be reproduced as an option.
2. **Landmark volumes** (bridges, buildings): the test and the slot layout are verified (section 11b); note 04a section 7 now gives the builder side (a slot starts with the entry's key long, then the model's footprint blocks offset by the entry's X and Z words shifted left by the LOD and masked to `0x3FFF`; reached through descriptor `+4`), but not every block word is decoded, so the port's world module still needs the model collision-data format. Method: decode the footprint blocks on the extracted models of the bridges (ids 48-52) and compare with the slot records of 04a's rig runs.
3. **Aircraft collision and damage** (result `0x40`, `B20` bit 1, `V:4A993`, `V:4A906`, `V:4A907`): the entry conditions are read here; the non-player result handler, the dying count and the kill counter are settled in note 07 section 5, the hit counts, cool-downs and the first-hit failure are note 07 part B (sections 9 and 10).
4. **Flag bits not named**: `W0` bits `0x0080`, `0x0100` (external view), `0x0008` (collidable), `W2` bits `0x0100`, `0x0020`, `0x0002`, `B20` bits `0x80`, `0x20`, state byte `+0x05` values other than 0 and `0x0A`, `+0x4C` use by AI. They are carried in the examples but no flight rule of the player depends on them beyond what is written.
5. **Replay streams** (`V:4A903` = 1, 3, 4). Closed by note 08 section 7: the stream is the key byte of one step plus two event words, played by the reader `0x1DFAA` for `V:4A903` = 3 (the demo; 2046 of the 3676 recorded frames), recorded by `0x2A1A6` only in a state no play reaches (modes 1, 2 and 4 are developer or overflow states); the event bits are the key handler bodies run by the executor `0x1D954` (28 bits tabulated; rig `replay_check.py`: 0 differences over 126,826 stream frames and 356 dispatch cases). The training lessons are not replays: they are scripted by an interpreter (section 8). The training switches (which of `V:4A83C`...`V:4A847` the lessons clear) are in note 08 section 8.
6. **Ejection**: the key sets the low nibble of `+0x7C` and the controls freeze; the effects of the count-3 step and the seat spawn are in 17.10 (`0x27522`; `0x2AA8E` spawns effect records into `0x4D23C`); **closed by note 07 section 6.4**: the release bit `W2` 0x0020 has one writer, the seat's dying step (`0x27DAA`), so the hold at 6 lasts until the seat is destroyed; the seat's step (`0x27BFA`), its landing (request 2, end code `0xF0`) and the parachute (class `0x31`: a second SHIFT-E at the right nibble, or SHIFT-F in mode 6) are rig-checked there (`seat_check.py`) and captured.
7. **Game-over text selection**: the game-over screen (`V:4A802` = `0x62` F-16 or `0x63` F/A-18, shown when the crash allowance `V:4A94F` reaches 0) Settled (note 08 section 2, Task 1): the id table at `0x43DFE` maps id `0x62` to the record at `0x44153` (it names the F-16) and `0x63` to the record at `0x4419F` (the F/A-18); the state `0x139F0` writes `0x62` when `V:4A901` is `0x10` and `0x63` otherwise; the screen's typed-out rule is note 08 section 3 (375 passes to the finished flag for `0x63`, 416 for `0x62`).
8. **Maximum-altitude message** (index 8) has no poster found; the altitude clamp is silent.
9. **Carrier vertex list (settled by note 07 8.10 and the rig).** The original uses the carrier record's own `+0xA4` slots as the **drawer 0x21C44 last left them**: each draw of the carrier (any level of detail) stores the **leading run of that level's block** (42 slots for model 8's coarse level, more for the near level, up to 58), rotated by the carrier's matrix, and leaves every later slot unchanged; a draw is skipped for a block without flag bit 0. Before the first draw the slots hold what the record held at program start or from an earlier flight (the flight start clears only `+0x00` to `+0xA3` of each record, `0x1D216`; the loop at `0x1D228` clears the secondary objects, not the slots): zero at the first boot. `test_slots_diff.c` shows the leading-run rule on model 8 with 0 differences. The port reads the carrier's slots from the draw store like any other record, not from a recomputed near level. Earlier text of this question, kept: the deck and hull tests read vertices written by the draw pass: which level of detail is in the record when the carrier was last drawn small (model 8 level 1 has 42 slots) and what the record holds before the first draw are not known. The port should recompute the near-level
   vertices from the extracted model each step. Settle by logging `+0xA4...` of record 14 while flying away from the carrier (01b style).
10. **Landing on water.** Closed by note 08 section 9.4: a ditching has no mission-level consequence. The aircraft skids (B20 bit 1), loses its gear, slows and floats at y = 750 until it stops; the flag `V:4A98A` bit 1 is set at the stop; no refuel, no mission code, no message, no log change; the flight goes on until the player leaves (captured, `flow-qual-ditch.txt`, one run).
11. **G readout** is derived from the code, checked only at 1.0 G; 01b logged HUD frames at 0.8, 0.3 and 1.7 G with attitude states; reproducing them from E needs the rate words at those instants.
12. **Mouse buttons and joystick**: closed by note 08 section 11.4. The left button is the gameport event for fire (the SPACE bodies `0x1F07A`, `0x1F0D0`); the button word `V:4A836` has its left bit permanently set (the routine `0x192AC` reads a data byte instead of the CIA port: captured, `V:4A836` = 1 with no input), which makes the right button alone an afterburner toggle at full throttle (read from `0x15E84`, not run); the joystick on control port 2 is the four arrow keys (`0x1906C`), without a fire button; no gameport event becomes a key. The pointer throttle and the movement are as in 17.4.
13. **The crash freeze.** Answered in section 14 and 17.17: the 40-iteration loop of the display flip, 2 blanks per iteration (measured, 80 blanks), then restart pass A and six more passes without simulation (7 passes: about 77 blanks in the cockpit view, 91 in the external view) before the first simulated step. Still open: what an iteration draws (not needed for the rule), and the external view: no hold; the restart waits for the player's dying expiry (17.3, rig).

14. **The result behind `0x8000` at the sea crash of the stall run.** Settled (17.12): result `0x10`, the three-vertex ground test, live because the renderer's part id `+0x7B` had turned 0. Method kept: `openers-stall-records.txt` and `openers-stall-part.txt`.
15. **The draw-side rule for `+0x7B`.** Closed by note 04a section 8a (commit `3ff9775`), which answers the four points this question listed: whether the byte keeps a stale value when the entity is culled or not drawn (yes: it keeps its last value in any pass in which the record is not drawn, the restart does not reset it, a record never drawn reads 0); which part ids the player's other levels of detail have (F/A-18: 0 below distance word 48, 1 to 78, 2 to 288, `0xFF` beyond; the ring external views always 0; approach cameras (note 07 6.1) may reach 1, 2 and `0xFF`); the one-pass lag (the collision of step N sees the draw of frame N-1, chosen with the camera set-up of N-1); and what `V:4AB70` is (the level-of-detail shift of the entry being drawn, stored by the walkers from the low nibble of the queue entry, 0 for the player's own cockpit entry: note 03, table of variables). 17.12 carries the summary. Still open there (04a, its own question 11): whether the very first step of a fresh start, before any draw, sees the zero of the cleared record, which would switch the ground test on for that one step.
16. **Junk reads in run-time memory** (17.9): the composition of a junk matrix reads bytes of `0x49A12..0x4A6E8` (zero fill in the image, display data at run time) in about 0.2 % of junk steps (12 of 7200 in the reference run of 17.9) and, in 2 of 7200 steps, live variable words in `0x4A6E8..0x4AE92` (hunk 71), which the port does not lay out as the original does. Evidence needed to reproduce: none in play (the junk steps follow a stall nose drop with a negative ground term, a few steps per lift-off); ruling: the port answers 0 (or its own variable when it keeps one at that address) and counts it. (The composition's lookups also read `0x3AE92..0x42E92`, below the tables: image bytes, in the corrected span 2, 17.18 a.)
17. **The dying path of non-player records**: closed by note 07 section 5 (`dying_check.py`, emulator capture of the training lead). `0x2D53C` is not part of the dying path: it is the per-record region check (note 07 3.4).
18. **The mission-level choice of `V:4A900`** (17.14: the writers `0x11C10`, `0x12102`, `0x121F8`, `0x12222`, `0x12252`, `0x1EBBA`): closed by note 08 (5.6 for the selection screen, 6.1 for the mission start, 7.5 for the demo's `0x11C10`, 11.3 for the extra digits).
19. **The crash hand-over with an ejection nibble** (17.17 rule 8): read only (note 07 6.4 and 6.5: the ejection captured twice in the emulator, from the key to the end code `0xF0`; the nibble holds at 6 until the seat lands, so the hand-over does not run in a normal ejection; the capture of the aircraft crashing during the hold was attempted and not obtained); **corrected (milestone 6, note 07 6.5): captured, the crash pass clears the nibble and the hand-over runs as for any crash** (end code 4, hold 40, allowance 5 to 4, restart; the seat is freed at the restart; no request 2); the earlier reading that it runs only when the nibble returns to 0 is wrong. Earlier evidence request, now met: one capture of a real ejection (SHIFT and E) into the sea, with `V:4A951`, the state pointer and the view per blank (writing the nibble into a snapshot does not reproduce the seat spawn); milestone 5.
20. **The hold's flash and the explosion effect** (17.17 rule 8): measured per blank (the colour registers alternate between the orange block and the game palette once per iteration, but only the last orange load is visible as a frame; one orange frame ends the hold). Why the other 39 loads leave no visible frame is not decoded (colour-register timing inside the display flip); the port reproduces the frames. The explosion record is not part of the cockpit hand-over (17.12); milestone 5.
21. **The demonstration's carrier** (17.17 rule 5). Closed by note 08 section 7.5: menu key 1 stores the mode `0x7F`; the mission start places the carrier for the row of the modes `0x7E`/`0x7F` (heading 28600), the demo setup `0x11BF4` places it again, stores the deck start `V:4A900` = 3 and then the mode 3 (`0x11C32`), which the demo keeps for the whole flight; `0x7E` is stored by no instruction.
22. **The scripted re-position** (17.17 rule 9). Closed by note 08 section 8.4 and 8.6: the bit is the player's RETURN request (part 1: the sequencer re-places the instructor and skips a lesson; part 2: the player is copied behind the instructor at (+9, +1, -97) model units, 99,760 position units, class set to the F/A-18) and, on the instructor, the fire trigger of part 2; rig-checked (30,000 + 30,000 random states, 0 differences).
23. **The keyboard queue's depth** (17.17 rule 6). Closed by note 08 section 5.8: the queue holds 31 events and drops the newest ones when full, with no auto-repeat (captured, `flow-s1-keyqueue.txt`).

## Verified by

Differential checks against the real code in the Musashi rig (`tools/m68k-rig`, the image `extracted/interceptor.bin`); each compares the **whole variable block** (`0x4A6E8`-`0x54EBF`, hunks 71 to 73) after the call, not just chosen fields. The comparison program is a private
re-implementation of the rules above in scratch (git-ignored, not for copying): it was written from the code, corrected until it agreed, and then run on random states (random flags, attitudes, speeds, fuel, throttle, key bytes, positions).

| claim | evidence |
| --- | --- |
| vector length, direction scaling and velocity smoothing (`0x20884`, `0x29C18`, `0x17288`) | 3000, 3000 and 2000 random calls, 0 differences |
| throttle, fuel and speed (`0x15ED4`, all branches of sections 3 and 4, gust and sink, relax and slew helpers) | 1800 random states with random flags, mouse words and warning words, 0 differences |
| control surfaces (`0x1557C`, section 5) | 5000 random states, 0 differences |
| gimbal guard with angle words outside 0..28799 (`0x31F80..0x32026`, 17.9) | scratch driver entering the guard directly (entity with chosen old pitch, heading, roll, `W2` bit `0x0004`, `+0x28`, E, Y; new angles in D4 to D6): 4,621,440 runs (old heading word 0..65535, old roll word 0..65535, old pitch word 0..65535, new heading word 0..65535 and new roll word 0..65535, each x 8 combinations of the other words in range or any word; then 2,000,000 random words), 1,913,615 stored and 2,707,825 abandoned; the signed-compare and wrap rule agrees with the stored angles and with `W2` bit `0x0004`, E and Y in all 4,621,440 (0 differences); the earlier rule (absolute value of the 16-bit result) differs from the rig in 619,797 of them; sequence 1594 step 6 explained |
| composition and Euler extraction (`0x325B8`) | 2000 random matrices and rates, 0 differences, including the matrix words written to `0x4AC48` and `0x4AC5A` |
| attitude step (`0x31AE0`, sections 5 and 6) | 7900 random states (stall, gimbal, recentre, drift overrides included), 0 differences |
| motion, floor, touchdown, lift-off, landing flags (`0x16B0E`, section 7) | 6500 random states, 0 differences (with `0x18E54`, `0x2F75A`, `0x1A18C` patched) |
| the whole player step (`0x2A2A2`, sections 1 to 9) | 4800 random states (cockpit and external view), 0 differences; 4800 more states placed around the carrier (deck, arrestor corridor, hull hits and the crash flags), 0 differences |
| landmark collision phase (`0x2BC28`-`0x2C08C`, section 11b) | 5700 random synthetic slot sets (cells, boxes, chains of one or two nodes, one to four planes), about 400 hits, 0 differences; this found the z shift of section 11b |
| terrain class (`0x2F75A`, section 10) | 7600 random positions, 0 differences (276 pad positions, several thousand either side of the coast) |
| u normalisation of the water edge test (`0x2F89E`-`0x2F8B4`, section 10) | rig, the loop run on its own (code copied to scratch RAM, d0 = u, d2 = 0, d6 = 0): u = -65536 gives m = 2, u' = -16384; -65535 gives m = 1, u' = -32768; -32769 gives m = 1, u' = -16385; -32768 gives m = 1, u' = -16384; -32767 gives m = 0; 65535 gives m = 1, u' = 32767; 65536 gives m = 2, u' = 16384; also -131071 (m = 2, u' = -32768) and -131072 (m = 3); in every case m is the least value with |u| >> m <= `0x7FFF` and u' = u >> m (arithmetic) |
